We did not reassemble in Mexico, but traveled separately and in three days we were encamped in our place of rendezvous. From this place we returned home empty-handed. We had not even a partial victory to report. I again returned wounded, but I was not yet discouraged. Again I was blamed by our people, and again I had no reply.

===Chinese===
原先有约在先：倘若带着这批辎重遭遇不测，我们的会合地点是亚利桑那的圣比塔山（Santa Bita Mountains）。我们没有在墨西哥重新聚齐，而是各自分头赶路，三天后，大家已在会合点扎了营。从那里我们两手空空地回了家。我们连一场小胜都无可禀报。我又一次带伤而归，但我还没有灰心。族人们又怪我，我又无言以对。

===Original===
After our return many of the warriors had gone on a hunt and some of them had gone north to trade for blankets from the Navajo Indians. I remained at home trying to get my wounds healed. One morning just at daybreak, when the squaws were lighting the campfires to prepare breakfast, three companies of Mexican troops who had surrounded our settlement in the night opened fire. There was no time for fighting. Men, women, and children fled for their lives. Many women and children and a few warriors were killed, and four women were captured. My left eye was still swollen shut, but with the other I saw well enough to hit one of the officers with an arrow, and then make good my escape among the rocks. The troopers burned our teepees and took our arms, provisions, ponies, and blankets. Winter was at hand.

===Chinese===
我们回来以后，许多武士外出打猎去了，有些人北上找纳瓦霍印第安人换毯子。我留在家里养伤。一天清早，天刚放亮，妇人们正生起篝火预备早饭，夜里已把我们居地团团围住的三个连墨西哥军队开了火。根本来不及应战。男女老少四散逃命。许多妇女孩子和几名武士被杀，四个女人被掳走。我的左眼还肿得睁不开，但靠另一只眼，我还看得清，一箭射中他们的一名军官，随即钻进乱石堆里脱了身。那些骑兵烧了我们的梯皮帐篷，掠走了我们的武器、粮食、矮种马和毯子。冬天眼看就要来了。

===Original===
There were not more than twenty warriors in camp at this time, and only a few of us had secured weapons during the excitement of the attack. A few warriors followed the trail of the troops as they went back to Mexico with their booty, but were unable to offer battle. It was a long, long time before we were again able to go on the warpath against the Mexicans.

===Chinese===
那时营里的武士不到二十人，混战之中抢到武器的只有寥寥几个。有几个武士缀着那队满载掳获、退回墨西哥去的敌军的踪迹追了一段，却无力求战。过了很久很久，我们才重新有能力出征讨伐墨西哥人。

===Original===
The four women who were captured at this time by the Mexicans were taken into Sonora, Mexico, where they were compelled to work for the Mexicans. After some years they escaped to the mountains and started to find our tribe. They had knives which they had stolen from the Mexicans, but they had no other weapons. They had no blankets; so at night they would make a little teepee by cutting brush with their knives, and setting them up for the walls. The top was covered over with brush. In this temporary teepee they would all sleep. One night when their campfire was low they heard growling just outside the teepee. Francisco, the youngest woman of the party (about seventeen years of age), started to build up the fire, when a mountain lion crashed through the teepee and attacked her. The suddenness of the attack made her drop her knife, but she fought as best she could with her hand. She was no match for the lion, however; her left shoulder was crushed and partly torn away. The lion kept trying to catch her by the throat; this she prevented with her hands for a long time. He dragged her for about 300 yards, then she found her strength was failing her from loss of blood, and she called to the other women for help. The lion had been dragging her by one foot, and she had been catching hold of his legs, and of the rocks and underbrush, to delay him. Finally he stopped and stood over her. She again called her companions and they attacked him with their knives and killed him. Then they dressed her wounds and nursed her in the mountains for about a month. When she was again able to walk they resumed their journey and reached our tribe in safety.

===Chinese===
这时被墨西哥人掳走的四个女人，被带到墨西哥的索诺拉，被迫替墨西哥人做工。过了几年，她们逃进山里，动身寻找我们的部落。她们带着从墨西哥人那里偷来的刀，却没有别的武器。她们没有毯子；夜里便用刀砍下树枝，支起来作墙，搭成一个小小的帐篷，顶上再盖上枝叶。就在这临时的帐篷里，她们挤在一起睡。一天夜里，篝火将熄，她们听见帐篷外传来低吼。弗朗西斯科（Francisco），一行中最年轻的女人（约莫十七岁），起身去添火，一头美洲狮破帐而入，向她扑来。事起突然，她的刀脱了手，只好徒手拼命招架。可她哪里是狮子的对手；她的左肩被咬得粉碎，还撕掉了一块。狮子一次次想咬住她的喉咙，她用双手死死撑住了很久。狮子拖着她走了约 300 码，她渐渐因失血而力气不支，便呼喊其余的女人来救。狮子一直咬着她的脚往回拖，她就抓着狮子的腿，抓着石头和灌木，拖延它。最后狮子停下来，站在她身上。她再呼同伴，同伴们持刀齐上，把它杀死了。随后她们替她裹伤，在山里护理了约莫一个月。等她重新能走路了，一行人再启程，平安到了我们部落。

===Original===
This woman (Francisco) was held as a prisoner of war with the other Apaches and died on the Fort Sill Reservation in 1892. Her face was always disfigured with those scars and she never regained perfect use of her hands. The three older women died before we became prisoners of war.

===Chinese===
这个女人（弗朗西斯科）后来同别的阿帕奇人一起被当作战俘拘押，1892 年死在西尔堡保留地（Fort Sill Reservation）。她的脸上永远留着那些疤痕，双手也再没能恢复如初。另外三个年长的女人，都在我们沦为战俘之前去世了。

===Original===
Many women and children were carried away at different times by Mexicans. Not many of them ever returned, and those who did underwent many hardships in order to be again united with their people. Those who did not escape were slaves to the Mexicans, or perhaps even more degraded.

===Chinese===
许许多多妇女和孩子，在不同的时候被墨西哥人掳走。回来的人寥寥无几；回来的那些，也是历尽艰辛才得以同族人重聚。逃不出来的，就给墨西哥人做奴隶，甚或落到更下贱的地步。

===Original===
When warriors were captured by the Mexicans they were kept in chains. Four warriors who were captured once at a place north of Casa Grande, called by the Indians “Honas,” were kept in chains for a year and a half, when they were exchanged for Mexicans whom we had captured.

===Chinese===
武士被墨西哥人捉去，就要戴上镣铐。有四名武士曾在卡萨格兰德以北一个被印第安人唤作「霍纳斯」（Honas）的地方被俘，戴了一年半的镣铐，才用我们俘获的墨西哥人换回来。

===Original===
We never chained prisoners or kept them in confinement, but they seldom got away. Mexican men when captured were compelled to cut wood and herd horses. Mexican women and children18 were treated as our own people.

===Chinese===
我们从不给俘虏戴镣铐，也不把他们关押起来，但他们很少能逃掉。墨西哥男人被俘后，就被派去砍柴、牧马。墨西哥的妇女和孩子，18 我们则待之如自己的族人。

## VIII Raids That Were Successful / 八 得手的袭掠

===Original===
In the summer of 1862 I took eight men and invaded Mexican territory. We went south on the west side of the Sierra Madre Mountains for five days; then in the night crossed over to the southern part of the Sierra de Sahuaripa range. Here we again camped to watch for pack trains. About ten o’clock next morning four drivers, mounted, came past our camp with a pack-mule train. As soon as they saw us they rode for their lives, leaving us the booty. This was a long train, and packed with blankets, calico, saddles, tinware, and loaf sugar. We hurried home as fast as we could with these provisions, and on our return while passing through a canyon in the Santa Catilina range of mountains in Arizona, met a white man driving a mule pack train. When we first saw him he had already seen us, and was riding at full tilt up the canyon. We examined his train and found that his mules were all loaded with cheese. We put them in with the other train and resumed our journey. We did not attempt to trail the driver and I am sure he did not try to follow us.

===Chinese===
1862 年夏天，我带八个人侵入墨西哥境内。我们沿马德雷山脉（Sierra Madre Mountains）西麓南行五天，然后趁夜翻到萨瓦里帕山脉（Sierra de Sahuaripa）的南段。我们在这里又扎下营，守候驮队。第二天上午十点来钟，四个赶骡人骑着马，赶着一队骡驮子经过我们营地。他们一看见我们，便没命地打马逃了，把辎重丢给了我们。这驮队很长，满载着毯子、印花布、马鞍、洋铁器皿和方糖。我们带着这批物资拼命往家赶；归途穿过亚利桑那（Arizona）圣卡蒂利纳山脉（Santa Catilina）的一道峡谷时，又遇上一个白人赶着一队骡驮子。我们刚看见他，他也早已看见我们，正没命地往峡谷上方逃。我们查看他的驮队，发现骡子驮的全是干酪。我们把这些骡子并进先前那队，继续赶路。我们没有去追那赶骡人，我敢说他也绝不敢来追我们。

===Original===
In two days we arrived at home. Then Mangus-Colorado, our chief, assembled the tribe. We gave a feast, divided the spoils, and danced all night. Some of the pack mules were killed and eaten.

===Chinese===
两天后我们到了家。我们的酋长曼加斯-科罗拉多（Mangus-Colorado）随即召集全族。我们设宴庆功，分了战利品，跳了一整夜的舞。有几匹驮骡宰了吃肉。

===Original===
This time after our return we kept out scouts so that we would know if Mexican troops should attempt to follow us.

===Chinese===
这一回回来以后，我们派出了侦察兵，墨西哥部队若想追来，我们就能察觉。

===Original===
On the third day our scouts came into camp and reported Mexican cavalry dismounted and approaching our settlement. All our warriors were in camp. Mangus-Colorado took command of one division and I of the other. We hoped to get possession of their horses, then surround the troops in the mountains, and destroy the whole company. This we were unable to do, for they, too, had scouts. However, within four hours after we started we had killed ten troopers with the loss of only one man, and the Mexican cavalry was in full retreat, followed by thirty armed Apaches, who gave them no rest until they were far inside the Mexican country. No more troops came that winter.

===Chinese===
第三天，侦察兵回营报告：墨西哥骑兵下马步行，正向我们居地逼近。我们的武士都在营中。曼加斯-科罗拉多统领一路，我统领另一路。我们本想先夺下他们的马，再在山里把这队人马围而歼之。这一着没能实现，因为他们也放出了侦察兵。不过，出发后四个钟头之内，我们只折了一人，就杀了他们十名骑兵，墨西哥骑兵全线溃逃，三十名带械的阿帕奇人衔尾追击，一直追到他们退入墨西哥腹地深处才罢休。那年冬天，再没有部队来犯。

===Original===
For a long time we had plenty of provisions, plenty of blankets, and plenty of clothing. We also had plenty of cheese and sugar.

===Chinese===
此后好长一段日子，我们的粮食充裕，毯子充裕，衣裳也充裕。干酪和糖也尽有尽用。

===Original===
Another summer (1863) I selected three warriors and went on a raid into Mexico. We went south into Sonora, camping in the Sierra de Sahuaripa Mountains. About forty miles west of Casa Grande is a small village in the mountains, called by the Indians “Crassanas.” We camped near this place and concluded to make an attack. We had noticed that just at midday no one seemed to be stirring; so we planned to make our attack at the noon hour. The next day we stole into the town at noon. We had no guns, but were armed with spears and bows and arrows. When the war-whoop was given to open the attack the Mexicans fled in every direction; not one of them made any attempt to fight us.

===Chinese===
又一个夏天（1863 年），我挑了三名武士，再次袭入墨西哥。我们南下进入索诺拉（Sonora），在萨瓦里帕山里扎营。卡萨格兰德（Casa Grande）以西约四十英里的山里，有一座小村，印第安人管它叫「克拉桑纳斯」（Crassanas）。我们在村子附近扎营，决意动手。我们留意到正午时分村里似乎没有人走动，便定在晌午进攻。第二天中午，我们悄悄摸进了村。我们没有枪，兵器是长矛和弓箭。进攻的战嚎一响，墨西哥人四散奔逃；没有一个人想同我们交手。

===Original===
We shot some arrows at the retreating Mexicans, but killed only one. Soon all was silent in the town and no Mexicans could be seen.

===Chinese===
我们朝奔逃的墨西哥人射了几箭，只射死一个。不久，全村一片死寂，一个墨西哥人也看不见了。

===Original===
When we discovered that all the Mexicans were gone we looked through their houses and saw many curious things. These Mexicans kept many more kinds of property than the Apaches did. Many of the things we saw in the houses we could not understand, but in the stores we saw much that we wanted; so we drove in a herd of horses and mules, and packed as much provisions and supplies as we could on them. Then we formed these animals into a pack train and returned safely to Arizona. The Mexicans did not even trail us.

===Chinese===
等我们察觉墨西哥人跑光了，便进他们的屋子查看，见到许多新奇的物件。这些墨西哥人家的财物种类，比阿帕奇人的多得多。屋里许多东西我们看不明白，但店铺里有许多我们要的；于是我们赶拢一群马和骡子，把尽可能多的粮食杂货驮在它们背上，编成一支驮队，平安返回了亚利桑那。墨西哥人连我们的踪迹都没有追。

===Original===
When we arrived in camp we called the tribe together and feasted all day. We gave presents to everyone. That night the dance began, and it did not cease until noon the next day.

===Chinese===
回到营地，我们把全族召齐，欢宴了一整天。我们给每个人都送了礼。当夜舞会开场，一直跳到第二天晌午才停。

===Original===
This was perhaps the most successful raid ever made by us into Mexican territory. I do not know the value of the booty, but it was very great, for we had supplies enough to last our whole tribe for a year or more.

===Chinese===
这大概是我们历次深入墨西哥境内最得手的一次袭掠。那批掳获值多少钱，我说不上，但一定很多，因为那些物资够我们全族吃用一年还有余。

===Original===
In the fall of 1864 twenty warriors were willing to go with me on another raid into Mexico. These were all chosen men, well armed and equipped for battle. As usual we provided for the safety of our families before starting on this raid. Our whole tribe scattered and then reassembled at a camp about forty miles from the former place. In this way it would be hard for the Mexicans to trail them and we would know where to find our families when we returned. Moreover, if any hostile Indians should see this large number of warriors leaving our range they might attack our camp, but if they found no one at the usual place their raid would fail.

===Chinese===
1864 年秋，有二十名武士愿意随我再去墨西哥袭掠一次。这都是精选的人，兵器齐全，装备停当。同往常一样，出发之前我们先安顿了家眷的安全。全族四散开去，再在离原营地约四十英里的地方重新聚齐。这样一来，墨西哥人便难以追踪他们，而我们回来时也知道去哪里找家人。再者，倘若有敌对的印第安人望见这么多武士离开我们的猎场，他们可能来犯我们的营地；但若他们扑到老地方一个人也找不到，这一趟也就白来了。

===Original===
We went south through the Chokonen Apaches’ range, entered Sonora, Mexico, at a point directly south of Tombstone, Arizona, and went into hiding in the Sierra de Antunez Mountains.

===Chinese===
我们南行穿过乔科嫩阿帕奇人的猎场，在亚利桑那墓碑镇（Tombstone）正南一处进入墨西哥索诺拉，随后隐进安图内斯山脉（Sierra de Antunez）里藏身。

===Original===
We attacked several settlements in the neighborhood and secured plenty of provisions and supplies. After about three days we attacked and captured a mule pack train at a place called by the Indians “Pontoco.” It is situated in the mountains due west, about one day’s journey19 from Arispe.

===Chinese===
我们袭击了附近几处聚落，掳获了大量粮食物资。约莫三天后，我们在一处印第安人唤作「蓬托科」（Pontoco）的地方，袭击并夺下了一支骡驮队。那地方在正西的山里，离阿里斯佩（Arispe）约一天的路程。19

===Original===
There were three drivers with this train. One was killed and two escaped. The train was loaded with mescal,20 which was contained in bottles held in wicker baskets. As soon as we made camp the Indians began to get drunk and fight each other. I, too, drank enough mescal to feel the effect of it, but I was not drunk. I ordered the fighting stopped, but the order was disobeyed. Soon almost a general fight was in progress. I tried to place a guard out around our camp, but all were drunk and refused to serve. I expected an attack from Mexican troops at any moment, and really it was a serious matter for me, for being in command I would be held responsible for any ill luck attending the expedition. Finally the camp became comparatively still, for the Indians were too drunk to walk or even to fight. While they were in this stupor I poured out all the mescal, then I put out all the fires and moved the pack mules to a considerable distance from camp. After this I returned to camp to try to do something for the wounded. I found that only two were dangerously wounded. From the leg of one of these I cut an arrowhead, and from the shoulder of another I withdrew a spear point. When all the wounds had been cared for, I myself kept guard till morning. The next day we loaded our wounded on the pack mules and started for Arizona.

===Chinese===
这支驮队有三个赶骡人。一个被打死，两个逃了。驮子满载着梅斯卡尔酒（mescal），20 装在柳条筐里护着的瓶子里。我们刚一扎营，印第安人便喝起了酒，醉了，彼此斗殴起来。我也喝了些，喝到身上起了酒力，但没有醉。我下令住手，没有人听。不多时几乎演成一场全营混战。我想在营地四周布哨，可人人都醉了，谁也不肯当值。我时刻提防墨西哥部队来攻，这对我实在非同小可——我是统领，此行若有闪失，罪责都在我身上。最后营里总算安静下来，因为印第安人醉得走不动，连打架也不能够了。趁他们昏醉，我把梅斯卡尔酒统统倒掉，然后熄灭所有的火，把驮骡赶到离营地很远的地方。办完这些，我回到营里照料伤者。我发现只有两个伤得重。我从其中一人的腿里剜出一枚箭镞，又从另一人的肩头拔出一支矛尖。所有伤口都处置妥当后，我便亲自守夜到天明。第二天，我们把伤员驮上驮骡，动身回亚利桑那。

===Original===
The next day we captured come cattle from a herd and drove them home with us. But it was a very difficult matter to drive cattle when we were on foot. Caring for the wounded and keeping the cattle from escaping made our journey tedious. But we were not trailed, and arrived safely at home with all the booty.

===Chinese===
第二天，我们从一群牛里捉了些牛，赶着一同回家。可我们步行赶牛，实在吃力。又要照料伤员，又要看住牛不跑散，一路走得又慢又烦。但没有被人追蹑，我们带着全部掳获平安到了家。

===Original===
We then gave a feast and dance, and divided the spoils. After the dance we killed all the cattle and dried the meat. We dressed the hides and then the dried meat was packed in between these hides and stored away. All that winter we had plenty of meat. These were the first cattle we ever had. As usual we killed and ate some of the mules. We had little use for mules, and if we could not trade them for something of value, we killed them.

===Chinese===
随后我们设宴、跳舞，分了战利品。舞罢，我们把牛全部宰了，把肉晒干。牛皮鞣制好，干肉就夹在这些皮子中间包好收藏。那个冬天我们肉食充足。这是我们头一回拥有牛。同往常一样，有几匹骡子宰了吃掉。骡子对我们用处不大，若换不来值钱的东西，我们就宰了它。

===Original===
In the summer of 1865, with four warriors, I went again into Mexico. Heretofore we had gone on foot; we were accustomed to fight on foot; besides, we could more easily conceal ourselves when dismounted. But this time we wanted more cattle, and it was hard to drive them when we were on foot. We entered Sonora at a point southwest from Tombstone, Arizona, and followed the Sierra de Antunez Mountains to the southern limit, then crossed the country as far south as the mouth of Yaqui River. Here we saw a great lake21 extending beyond the limit of sight. Then we turned north, attacked several settlements, and secured plenty of supplies. When we had come back northwest of Arispe we secured about sixty head of cattle, and drove them to our homes in Arizona. We did not go directly home, but camped in different valleys with our cattle. We were not trailed. When we arrived at our camp the tribe was again assembled for feasting and dancing. Presents were given to everybody; then the cattle were killed and the meat dried and packed.

===Chinese===
1865 年夏天，我又带着四名武士进入墨西哥。此前我们都是步行前往；我们惯于步战；况且不骑马更容易藏身。但这一回我们想要更多的牛，步行赶牛太不容易。我们从亚利桑那墓碑镇的西南方向进入索诺拉，沿安图内斯山脉走到山系尽头，再横穿乡野，一直南到雅基河（Yaqui River）河口。在这里，我们望见一片大湖，21 湖面一望无际。然后我们折向北行，袭击了几处聚落，掳获了大量物资。回程走到阿里斯佩西北时，我们又得了约六十头牛，赶着它们回亚利桑那的家。我们没有直接回家，而是赶着牛在各处谷地里轮流扎营。没有人追踪我们。回到营地时，全族又聚齐设宴、跳舞。人人都有馈赠；随后牛都宰了，肉晒干装好。

## IX Varying Fortunes / 九 胜败无常

===Original===
In the fall of 1865 with nine other warriors I went into Mexico on foot. We attacked several settlements south of Casa Grande, and collected many horses and mules. We made our way northward with these animals through the mountains. When near Arispe we made camp one evening, and thinking that we were not being trailed, turned loose the whole herd, even those we had been riding. They were in a valley surrounded by steep mountains, and we were camped at the mouth of this valley so that the animals could not leave without coming through our camp. Just as we had begun to eat our supper our scouts came in and announced Mexican troops coming toward our camp. We started for the horses, but troops that our scouts had not seen were on the cliffs above us, and opened fire. We scattered in all directions, and the troops recovered all our booty. In three days we reassembled at our appointed place of rendezvous in the Sierra Madre Mountains in northern Sonora. Mexican troops did not follow us, and we returned to Arizona without any more fighting and with no booty. Again I had nothing to say, but I was anxious for another raid.

===Chinese===
1865 年秋，我同另外九名武士徒步进入墨西哥。我们袭击了卡萨格兰德（Casa Grande）以南的几处聚落，收拢了许多马和骡子。我们赶着这些牲口取山间小道北返。一天傍晚，快到阿里斯佩（Arispe）时，我们扎下营，自忖没有被人追踪，便把整群牲口都放开，连自己骑的也放了。牲口在一处四面陡山的谷地里，我们的营扎在谷口，牲口要出去，非得穿过我们的营地不可。我们刚端起晚饭，侦察兵进营报告：墨西哥部队朝我们营地来了。我们起身奔向马匹，可我们侦察兵没发现的另一股部队已占了头顶的悬崖，开了火。我们四散奔逃，部队把我们的掳获全数夺了回去。三天后，我们在索诺拉北部马德雷山脉（Sierra Madre Mountains）中的老会合点重新聚齐。墨西哥部队没有追来，我们没有再交战，两手空空地回了亚利桑那（Arizona）。我又一次无话可说，却更急切地想再去袭掠一回。

===Original===
Early the next summer (1866) I took thirty mounted warriors and invaded Mexican territory. We went south through Chihuahua as far as Santa Cruz, Sonora, then crossed over the Sierra Madre Mountains, following the river course at the south end of the range. We kept on westward from the Sierra Madre Mountains to the Sierra de Sahuripa Mountains, and followed that range northward. We collected all the horses, mules, and cattle we wanted, and drove them northward through Sonora into Arizona. Mexicans saw us at many times and in many places, but they did not attack us at any time, nor did any troops attempt to follow us. When we arrived at our homes we gave presents to all, and the tribe feasted and danced. During this raid we had killed about fifty Mexicans.

===Chinese===
第二年夏初（1866 年），我带了三十名骑马的武士侵入墨西哥境内。我们南下穿过奇瓦瓦（Chihuahua），直抵索诺拉的圣克鲁斯（Santa Cruz），然后翻过马德雷山脉，沿着山系南端的河道走。我们离开马德雷山脉继续向西，到萨瓦里帕山脉（Sierra de Sahuripa），再沿这条山系北行。我们收齐了想要的马、骡和牛，赶着它们取道索诺拉北上，进入亚利桑那。墨西哥人多次多处望见我们，却始终没有来攻，也没有部队试图追赶。到了家，我们给所有人分送了礼物，全族设宴跳舞。这次袭掠，我们总共杀了约五十个墨西哥人。

===Original===
Next year (1867) Mangus-Colorado led eight warriors on a raid into Mexico. I went as a warrior, for I was always glad to fight the Mexicans. We rode south from near Tombstone, Arizona, into Sonora, Mexico. We attacked some cowboys, and after a fight with them, in which two of their number were killed, we drove all their cattle northward. The second day we were driving the cattle, but had no scouts out. When we were not far from Arispe, Mexican troops rode upon us. They were well armed and well mounted, and when we first saw them they were not half a mile away from us. We left the cattle and rode as hard as we could toward the mountains, but they gained on us rapidly. Soon they opened fire, but were so far away from us that we were unable to reach them with our arrows; finally we reached some timber, and, leaving our ponies, fought from cover. Then the Mexicans halted, collected our ponies, and rode away across the plains toward Arispe, driving the cattle with them. We stood and watched them until they disappeared in the distance, and then took up our march for home.

===Chinese===
第二年（1867 年），曼加斯-科罗拉多（Mangus-Colorado）率八名武士前往墨西哥袭掠。我以武士的身份同行，因为同墨西哥人打仗，我向来甘愿。我们从亚利桑那墓碑镇（Tombstone）附近南下，进入墨西哥索诺拉。我们袭击了一伙牧牛人，交手之后，他们死了两个，我们把他们的牛统统往北赶。第二天我们赶着牛走，却没有放出侦察兵。离阿里斯佩不远时，墨西哥部队策马扑来。他们武器精良，马也雄健；我们刚望见他们时，他们离我们还不到半英里。我们丢下牛，没命地往山里逃，他们却追得飞快。不久他们开了火，但距离尚远，我们的箭够不着他们；我们总算赶到了一片树林，撇下矮种马，凭掩护还击。这时墨西哥人停了下来，收拢我们的矮种马，赶着牛，穿过平原往阿里斯佩去了。我们站在原地，望着他们消失在远方，然后动身步行回家。

===Original===
We arrived home in five days with no victory to report, no spoils to divide, and not even the ponies which we had ridden into Mexico. This expedition was considered disgraceful.

===Chinese===
我们五天后到家，既无胜仗可报，又无掳获可分，连骑进墨西哥的那些矮种马也没能带回来。这一趟被众人视为奇耻大辱。

===Original===
The warriors who had been with Mangus-Colorado on this last expedition wanted to return to Mexico. They were not satisfied, besides they felt keenly the taunts of the other warriors. Mangus-Colorado would not lead them back, so I took command and we went on foot, directly toward Arispe in Sonora, and made our camp in the Sierra de Sahuripa Mountains. There were only six of us, but we raided several settlements (at night), captured many horses and mules, and loaded them with provisions, saddles and blankets. Then we returned to Arizona, traveling only at night. When we arrived at our camp we sent out scouts to prevent any surprise by Mexicans, assembled the tribe, feasted, danced, and divided the spoils. Mangus-Colorado would not receive any of this booty, but we did not care. No Mexican troops followed us to Arizona.

===Chinese===
随曼加斯-科罗拉多走过这一趟的武士们想再回墨西哥去。他们心有不甘，又实在咽不下其他武士的讥嘲。曼加斯-科罗拉多不愿再领他们去，我便接了统领。我们徒步出发，直奔索诺拉的阿里斯佩，在萨瓦里帕山里扎营。我们只有六个人，却（在夜里）袭掠了好几处聚落，掳得许多马骡，给它们驮上粮食、马鞍和毯子。随后我们回亚利桑那，只在夜里赶路。回到营地，我们先放出侦察兵，防墨西哥人偷袭，然后召齐全族，设宴、跳舞、分战利品。曼加斯-科罗拉多不肯受分这批掳获，我们也不在意。墨西哥部队没有追进亚利桑那。

===Original===
About a year after this (1868) Mexican troops rounded up all the horses and mules of the tribe not far from our settlement. No raids had been made into Mexico that year, and we were not expecting any attacks. We were all in camp, having just returned from hunting.

===Chinese===
此后约一年（1868 年），墨西哥部队在我们居地不远处把部落的马骡洗劫一空。那一年我们没有进墨西哥袭掠过，也就没有提防任何来犯。我们都在营里，刚打猎回来。

===Original===
About two o’clock in the afternoon two Mexican scouts were seen near our settlement. We killed these scouts, but the troops got under way with the herd of our horses and mules before we saw them. It was useless to try to overtake them on foot, and our tribe had not a horse left. I took twenty warriors and trailed them. We found the stock at a cattle ranch in Sonora, not far from Nacozari, and attacked the cowboys who had them in charge. We killed two men and lost none. After the fight we drove off our own stock and all of theirs.

===Chinese===
下午两点来钟，我们居地附近出现了两个墨西哥侦察兵。我们杀了他们，可没等我们望见大队，部队已赶着我们那群马骡上路了。徒步追赶无济于事，我们部落又一匹马也不剩。我带了二十名武士，一路追踪。我们在索诺拉一座牧牛场找到了牲口，那地方离纳科萨里（Nacozari）不远；我们袭击了看管牲口的牧牛人，杀了两个，自己无一伤亡。战罢，我们赶走了自己的牲口，连他们的也一并赶走了。

===Original===
We were trailed by nine cowboys. I sent the stock on ahead and with three warriors stayed in the rear to intercept any attacking parties. One night when near the Arizona line we discovered these cowboys on our trail and watched them camp for the night and picket their horses. About midnight we stole into their camp and silently led away all their horses, leaving the cowboys asleep. Then we rode hard and overtook our companions, who always traveled at night instead of in the daytime. We turned these horses in with the herd and fell back to again intercept anyone who might trail us. What these nine cowboys did next morning I do not know, and I have never heard the Mexicans say anything about it; I know they did not follow us, for we were not molested. When we arrived in camp at home there was great rejoicing in the tribe. It was considered a good trick to get the Mexicans’ horses and leave them asleep in the mountains.

===Chinese===
九个牧牛人追踪我们。我让牲口先走，自己带三名武士殿后，拦截可能的追兵。一天夜里，快到亚利桑那边界时，我们发现这些牧牛人缀在我们后面，便看他们扎营过夜、拴好马。约莫半夜，我们摸进他们的营地，悄悄牵走了他们所有的马，让那些牧牛人兀自酣睡。然后我们打马疾驰，追上了总是昼伏夜行的同伴。我们把这些马并入牲口群，又退回后队，防备再有人追踪。这九个牧牛人第二天早上如何，我不知道，也从未听墨西哥人提起过；我只知道他们没有再追来，因为我们再没受到搅扰。我们回到家里的营地时，全族欢腾。夺得墨西哥人的马、把他们留在山里沉睡，被大家看作一桩妙计。

===Original===
It was a long time before we again went into Mexico or were disturbed by the Mexicans.

===Chinese===
此后很久，我们既没有再去墨西哥，也没有再受墨西哥人搅扰。

## X Other Raids / 十 其他袭掠

===Original===
When reading the foregoing chapters of Apache raids one not acquainted with the lawlessness of the frontier might wonder how this tendency of the Apaches was developed to such a marked degree; but one acquainted with the real conditions﻿—the disregard for law by both Mexicans and white men along the border line of Old Mexico and Arizona in early days﻿—can readily understand where the Apache got his education in the art of conducting lawless raids. In order, therefore, that those who are unacquainted with the conditions as they were in southern Arizona during the eighties, may understand the environment of the Apaches, this chapter is given. The events herein narrated are taken by the author from many accounts given him by reliable men who lived in this section of country during the period mentioned.

===Chinese===
读罢前几章阿帕奇人的袭掠，不了解边疆不法之风的读者，也许会诧异阿帕奇人的这种习性何以发展到如此地步；但凡了解真实状况的人——早年老墨西哥与亚利桑那（Arizona）边界沿线，墨西哥人和白人同样视法律如无物——便不难明白，阿帕奇人这无法无天的袭掠之术是从哪里学来的。因此，为了让不了解 19 世纪 80 年代南亚利桑那实况的读者认识阿帕奇人所处的环境，笔者特编本章。本章所述事件，均由笔者取自当事时期居住在该地区的可靠人士提供的诸多记述。

#### Raid by White Men / 白人的袭掠

===Original===
In 1882 a company of six Mexican traders, who were known as “smugglers” because they evaded duties on goods which they brought into United States and sold in Arizona, were camped in Skeleton Canyon, ten miles north of the north line of Old Mexico. They were known to carry large sums of money, but as they were always armed and ready to defend their possessions they were not often molested. However, on this occasion, just as they were rising in the morning to prepare their breakfast, five white men opened fire on them from ambush and all save one of the Mexicans were killed. This one, though wounded, finally made his escape. A few days after the killing some cowboys on a roundup camped at this place and buried the remains (what the coyotes had left) of these five Mexicans. Two years later, at the same place, a cowboy found a leather bag containing seventy-two Mexican dollars, which small amount of money had been overlooked by the robbers.

===Chinese===
1882 年，六个墨西哥贩子结伙在骷髅峡谷（Skeleton Canyon）扎营，那地方在老墨西哥北界以北十英里。这些人因把货物运进美国、在亚利桑那贩卖而偷逃关税，人称「走私贩」。他们素来身怀巨款，但因其常年携械、随时准备护财，很少遭人打劫。然而这一次，正当他们清早起身预备早饭时，五个白人自埋伏中向他们开火，六个墨西哥人除一个外全部毙命。那一个虽负了伤，终究逃脱了。命案发生几天后，几个围拢畜群的牧牛人到此地扎营，掩埋了这五个墨西哥人的残骸（郊狼吃剩的部分）。两年后，一个牧牛人在同一地点拾到一只皮袋，内装七十二枚墨西哥银元——这笔小钱，是劫匪们漏下的。

===Original===
The men who did this killing lived in Arizona for many years afterwards, and although it was known that they had committed the depredation, no arrests followed, and no attempt was made by any of the Mexicans to recover the property of their fellow citizens.

===Chinese===
行凶杀人的那几个白人，此后在亚利桑那住了许多年；尽管人人都知道这桩劫案是他们干的，却无人被捕，墨西哥人方面也没有任何人试图为本国公民追回财物。

#### Mexican Raid / 墨西哥人的袭掠

===Original===
In 1884 a cattleman and four cowboys from his ranch started to drive some fat cattle to market at Tombstone, Arizona. The route they took led partly through Old Mexico and partly through Arizona. One night they camped in a canyon just south of the Mexican border. Next morning at daylight, the cowboy who had been on herd duty the last half of the night had just come in and aroused the camp when the Mexicans opened fire on them from ambush. The cattleman and one of the cowboys were severely wounded at the first volley and took shelter behind the camp wagon, from which position they fired as long as their ammunition lasted. The other three were only slightly wounded and reached cover, but only one escaped with his life. He remained in hiding for two days before his comrades found him. He saw the Mexicans rob the bodies of the dead and lead away their saddle horses, after having cooked breakfast for themselves in the deserted camp. He was severely wounded and all his ammunition was gone, hence he could only wait.

===Chinese===
1884 年，一个牧场主带着自家牧场的四名牧牛人，赶一批肥牛前往亚利桑那的墓碑镇（Tombstone）赶集。他们走的路线，一段在老墨西哥境内，一段在亚利桑那境内。一天夜里，他们在边界以南不远的一道峡谷里宿营。次日清晨天刚亮，值后半夜看畜的牧牛人刚回营叫醒众人，墨西哥人便从埋伏中开了火。牧场主和一名牧牛人头一排枪就身负重伤，躲到营车后面，在那里坚持射击，直到弹药打光。另外三人只受了轻伤，也都找到了掩蔽，但逃得性命的只有一个。他在藏身处躲了两天，才被同伴找到。他亲眼看见墨西哥人在空营里给自己做了早饭吃，然后劫掠死者的财物、牵走他们的坐骑。他伤势沉重，弹药尽失，只能等着。

===Original===
On the second day after this raid some of the cattle strayed back to the old ranch, thereby giving notice to the cowboys that there had been foul play. They found their wounded companions lying delirious near the decaying bodies of their comrades. No arrests were ever made in Mexico for these murders, and no attempt was made to recover damage or prosecute the robbers. The two instances above narrated will serve to show the reader what kind of an example was set for the Apaches by at least a portion of the inhabitants of the two Christian nations with whom they came in contact.

===Chinese===
劫案发生后的第二天，有几头牛自己跑回了老牧场，牧牛人们由此得知出了事。他们找到负伤的同伴时，他正神志昏乱地躺在同伴渐渐腐烂的尸体旁。墨西哥方面从未为这些凶案缉捕过任何人，也从未有人试图索赔或追究凶手。上面叙述的两桩事例足以向读者表明：至少同阿帕奇人打交道的这两个基督教国家的部分居民，为阿帕奇人树立了怎样的榜样。

#### Apache Raids / 阿帕奇人的袭掠

===Original===
It is thought well to give in this chapter some of the depredations of the Apaches, not told by Geronimo. They are given as told by our own citizens and from the white man’s point of view.

===Chinese===
本章酌情收录几件杰罗尼莫（Geronimo）未曾讲述的阿帕奇人劫掠事迹。这些事迹均出自我国公民之口，是以白人的视角记述的。

===Original===
In 1884 Judge McCormick and wife, accompanied by their young son, were driving from Silver City to Lordsburg, when they were ambushed by Apaches. The bodies of the adults were found soon afterward, but the child’s body was never recovered. Years afterwards, an Apache squaw told some of the settlers in Arizona that the little boy (about eight years old) cried so much and was so stubborn that they had to kill him, although their original intention was to spare his life.

===Chinese===
1884 年，麦考密克法官（Judge McCormick）偕妻子带着幼子驾车从银城（Silver City）前往洛兹堡（Lordsburg），途中遭阿帕奇人伏击。夫妇二人的尸体很快被人寻获，孩子的尸体却始终没有下落。多年以后，一个阿帕奇妇人告诉亚利桑那的几个移民：那个小男孩（约八岁）哭闹得太凶、性子太倔，他们只好杀了他——尽管起初是打算留他性命的。

===Original===
In 1882 a man named Hunt was wounded in a row in a saloon in Tombstone, Arizona. During this row two other men had been killed, and, to avoid arrest, Hunt and his brother went into the mountains and camped about ten miles north of Willow Springs to await the healing of his wounds. A few days after they came there, Apache Indians attacked them and killed the wounded brother, but the other, by hard riding, made good his escape.

===Chinese===
1882 年，一个名叫亨特（Hunt）的人在亚利桑那墓碑镇的一家酒馆斗殴中受了伤。那场斗殴还死了另外两个人；为躲避拘捕，亨特和他的兄弟躲进山里，在柳泉（Willow Springs）以北约十英里处扎营，等他养伤。他们到那里没几天，阿帕奇印第安人便来袭，杀死了负伤的那一个；另一个靠拼命运马，才算脱身。

===Original===
In 1883 two Eastern boys went into Arizona to prospect. Their real outing began at Willow Springs, where they had stayed two days with the cowboys. These cowboys had warned them against the Apaches, but the young men seemed entirely fearless, and pushed on into the mountains. On the second morning after they left the settlement, one of the boys was getting breakfast while the other went to bring in the pack horses that had been hobbled and turned loose the night before to graze. Just about the time he found his horses, two Apache warriors rode out from cover toward him and he made a hasty retreat to camp, jumping off of a bluff and in so doing breaking his leg.

===Chinese===
1883 年，两个东部来的小伙子到亚利桑那勘探矿藏。他们真正的旅程从柳泉开始——在那里他们同牧牛人们一起住了两天。牧牛人们告诫过他们提防阿帕奇人，但这两个年轻人似乎毫无畏惧，径直往山里去了。离开聚落后的第二个早晨，一个年轻人正做着早饭，另一个去牵回前夜绊上腿放开吃草的驮马。差不多就在他找到马的时候，两个阿帕奇武士从掩蔽处向他驰来，他急忙往营地奔逃，从一道断崖上跳下去，摔断了一条腿。

===Original===
A consultation was then held between the two Easterners and it was decided that perhaps all the stories they had been told of the Apache raids were true, and that it was advisable to surrender. Accordingly a white handkerchief was tied to the end of a pole and raised cautiously above the top of the bluff. In about ten minutes the two Indians﻿—one a very old warrior and the other a mere boy, evidently his son﻿—rode into camp and dismounted. The old warrior examined the broken limb, then without a word proceeded to take off the shirt of the uninjured youth, with strips of which he carefully bound up the broken leg. After this the two Indians ate the prepared breakfast and remounted their ponies. Then the old warrior, indicating the direction with his thumb, said “Doctor﻿—Lordsburg﻿—three days,” and silently rode away. The young men rode twenty-five miles to Sansimone, where the cowboys fitted them out with a wagon to continue their journey to Lordsburg, seventy-five miles further, where a physician’s services could be secured.

===Chinese===
两个东部人一合计，认定人们讲的关于阿帕奇袭掠的传闻恐怕全是真的，还是投降为妙。于是他们把一方白手帕缚在竿子头上，小心翼翼地从崖顶探了出去。约莫十分钟后，那两个印第安人——一个是十分年迈的武士，另一个不过是个孩子，显然是他儿子——骑马进营，下了马。老武士查看了那条断腿，然后一言不发，动手脱下那个未受伤的青年的衬衫，撕成布条，仔仔细细把断腿包扎好。此后两个印第安人吃掉了备好的早饭，重新上马。老武士用拇指指了指方向，说：「大夫——洛兹堡——三天。」便默默驰去了。两个青年骑马走了二十五英里到桑西蒙（Sansimone），牧牛人给他们套了一辆马车，让他们继续赶往七十五英里外的洛兹堡，在那里才请得到医生。

===Original===
In 1883 two prospectors, Alberts and Reese by name, were driving a team, consisting of a horse and a mule, through Turkey Creek bottoms, when they were shot by the Indians. The wagon and harness were left in the road, and the mule was found dead in the road two hundred yards from that place. Evidently the Indians had not much use for him. The guns of the prospectors were found later, but the horse they drove was not recovered.

===Chinese===
1883 年，两个名叫阿尔伯茨（Alberts）和里斯（Reese）的探矿人赶着一马一骡的大车，行经特基河（Turkey Creek）河滩，被印第安人开枪打死。大车和挽具弃在路中，那头骡子被人发现死在离出事地点二百码的路面上。显然印第安人用不上它。探矿人的枪后来找到了，他们驾车的马却再无下落。

===Original===
In none of the above-named instances were the bodies of the victims mutilated. However, there are many recorded instances in which the Apache Indians did mutilate the bodies of their victims, but it is claimed by Geronimo that these were outlawed Indians, as his regular warriors were instructed to scalp none except those killed in battle, and to torture none except to make them reveal desired information.

===Chinese===
上述各案中，遇害者的遗体均未遭残毁。不过，阿帕奇印第安人残毁遇害者遗体的记录也确有许多；但据杰罗尼莫分辩，那些都是亡命的不法印第安人所为，因为他麾下正规的武士曾受严令：只剥战死者的头皮，除非为逼取所需的口供，不得施刑。

===Original===
In 1884 two cowboys in the employment of the Sansimone Cattle Company were camped at Willow Springs, eighteen miles southwest of Skeleton Canyon, and not far from Old Mexico. Just at sundown their camp was surrounded by Apaches in war paint, who said that they had been at war with the Mexicans and wished to return to the United States. There were about seventy-five Indians in the whole tribe, the squaws and children coming up later. They had with them about one hundred and fifty Mexican horses. The Indians took possession of the camp and remained for about ten days, getting their supplies of meat by killing cattle of the company.

===Chinese===
1884 年，受雇于桑西蒙畜牧公司的两名牧牛人在柳泉扎营，那里在骷髅峡谷西南十八英里处，离老墨西哥不远。日落时分，一群涂着战彩的阿帕奇人包围了他们的营地，说自己刚同墨西哥人打过仗，想返回美国。全族约七十五个印第安人，妇人和孩子随后赶到。他们带着约一百五十匹墨西哥马。印第安人占据了营地，盘桓了约十天，宰杀公司的牛充作肉食。

===Original===
With this band of Indians was a white boy about fourteen years old, who had evidently been with them from infancy, for he could not speak a word of English, and did not understand much Spanish, but spoke the Apache language readily.

===Chinese===
这群印第安人中有个约十四岁的白人男孩，显然自幼便生活在他们中间，因为他连一句英语都不会讲，西班牙语也听不大懂，阿帕奇语却说得流利。

===Original===
They would allow but one of the cowboys to leave camp at a time, keeping the other under guard. They had sentinels with spyglasses on all the hills and peaks surrounding the camp.

===Chinese===
他们每次只准一个牧牛人离营，另一个留在看管之下。营地四周所有的山头和峰顶，都布了带望远镜的哨兵。

===Original===
One evening when one of the cowboys, William Berne, had been allowed to pass out of the camp, he noticed an Indian dismounted and, as he approached, discovered that the Indian had him under range of his rifle. He immediately dismounted, and standing on the opposite side from the redskin, threw his own Winchester across his horse’s neck, when the Indian sprang on his horse and galloped toward him at full speed, making signs to him not to shoot, and when he approached him, dismounted and pointing to the ground, showed Berne many fresh deer tracks. Then, as an understanding had been established, the cowboy remounted and went on his way, leaving the Apache to hunt the deer.

===Chinese===
一天傍晚，其中一个名叫威廉·伯恩（William Berne）的牧牛人获准出营，他望见一个印第安人下了马；待他走近，才发现自己已在对方步枪的准星之下。他立即下了马，站在那红种人的对面，把自己的温彻斯特步枪横搁在马颈上；那印第安人随即翻身上马，朝他疾驰而来，连连做手势叫他别开枪；到了近前，那印第安人下马指着地面，给伯恩看许多新鲜的鹿踪。误会既已消解，牧牛人重新上马自去赶路，把那阿帕奇人留给他的鹿。

===Original===
One day when this cowboy was about ten miles from camp, he found two splendid horses of the Indians. These horses had strayed from the herd. Thinking that they would in a way compensate for the cattle the Apaches were eating, he drove them on for about five miles into a canyon where there was plenty of grass and water and left them there, intending to come back after the departure of the Indians and take possession of them.

===Chinese===
有一天，这个牧牛人在离营地约十英里的地方，发现了印第安人的两匹好马。这两匹马是从马群里走散的。他心想，这多少可以抵偿阿帕奇人吃掉的牛，便把它们往前赶了约五英里，赶进一道水草丰足的峡谷留在那里，打算等印第安人走后再回来据为己有。

===Original===
On the tenth day after the arrival of this band of Indians, United States troops, accompanied by two Indians who had been sent to make the arrangements, arrived in camp, paid for the cattle the Apaches had eaten, took the Indians and their stock, and moved on toward Fort Bowie. The cowboys immediately started for the canyon where the two horses had been left, but had not gone far when they met two Indians driving these horses in front of them as they pushed on to overtake the tribe.

===Chinese===
这伙印第安人到来的第十天，美国军队在两名先行接洽的印第安人陪同下开进营地，付清了阿帕奇人吃掉的牛钱，带上这些印第安人和他们的牲口，往鲍伊堡（Fort Bowie）方向去了。牧牛人们立刻动身去那道藏马的峡谷，但走不多远，就迎面遇上两个印第安人，正赶着那两匹马走在前头，追赶他们的部落去了。

===Original===
Evidently the shrewdness of the paleface had not outwitted the red man that time.

===Chinese===
那一回，白种人的精明显然没能斗过红种人。

===Original===
Geronimo says he was in no wise connected with the events herein mentioned, but refuses to state whether he knows anything about them. He holds it unmanly to tell of any depredations of red men except those for which he was responsible.

===Chinese===
杰罗尼莫说，本章所述事件与他毫无干系，但被问及是否知情时，他不肯回答。他认为，除了自己担责的劫掠之外，去讲别的红种人的劫掠事迹，不是男子汉的作为。

===Original===
Such were the events transpiring in “Apache land” during the days when Geronimo was leading his warriors to avenge the “wrongs” of his people. This chapter will serve to show that the Apache had plenty of examples of lawlessness furnished him, and also that he was a very apt scholar in this school of savage lawlessness.

===Chinese===
在杰罗尼莫率领武士为族人的「冤仇」复仇的那些年月里，「阿帕奇人的土地」上发生的事情，大抵如此。本章足以表明：阿帕奇人从不缺少无法无天的样板可供效法，而在这所野蛮不法的学堂里，他又是个何等伶俐的学生。

## XI Heavy Fighting / 十一 恶战

===Original===
About 1873 we were again attacked by Mexican troops in our settlement, but we defeated them. Then we decided to make raids into Mexico. We moved our whole camp, packing all our belongings on mules and horses, went into Mexico and made camp in the mountains near Nacori. In moving our camp in this way we wanted no one to spy on us, and if we passed a Mexican’s home we usually killed the inmates. However, if they offered to surrender and made no resistance or trouble in any way, we would take them prisoners. Frequently we would change our place of rendezvous; then we would take with us our prisoners if they were willing to go, but if they were unruly they might be killed. I remember one Mexican in the Sierra Madre Mountains who saw us moving and delayed us for some time. We took the trouble to get him, thinking the plunder of his house would pay us for the delay, but after we had killed him we found nothing in his house worth having. We ranged in these mountains for over a year, raiding the Mexican settlements for our supplies, but not having any general engagement with Mexican troops; then we returned to our homes in Arizona. After remaining in Arizona about a year we returned to Mexico, and went into hiding in the Sierra Madre Mountains. Our camp was near Nacori, and we had just organized bands of warriors for raiding the country, when our scouts discovered Mexican troops coming toward our camp to attack us.

===Chinese===
约莫 1873 年，墨西哥部队又来攻打我们的居地，被我们打败了。于是我们决意深入墨西哥袭掠。我们迁移了整个营地，把全部家当驮上骡马，进入墨西哥，在纳科里（Nacori）附近的山里扎营。这样移营，我们不愿任何人窥见行踪；凡是路过墨西哥人的家，我们通常把屋里的人杀掉。不过，倘若他们肯投降、不反抗、不添任何麻烦，我们就掳他们为俘虏。我们常常更换会合的地点；换的时候，俘虏若肯跟走，就带他们同去；若是不驯服，就可能被杀。我记得在马德雷山脉（Sierra Madre Mountains）里有一个墨西哥人，望见我们移营，耽搁了我们一些时候。我们特意去捉他，心想劫他家的财物总抵得过这点耽搁；可杀了他之后，发现他家里没有一件东西值得拿。我们在这些山里游荡了一年多，靠袭掠墨西哥人的聚落取得给养，却未同墨西哥部队大规模交战；随后我们回了亚利桑那（Arizona）的家。在亚利桑那住了约一年，我们又回到墨西哥，隐进马德雷山脉。我们的营地靠近纳科里，刚刚编好袭掠乡野的武士队，侦察兵便发现墨西哥部队正朝我们营地扑来。

#### Battle of White Hill / 白山之战

===Original===
The chief of the Nedni Apaches, Whoa, was with me and commanded one division. The warriors were all marched toward the troops and met them at a place about five miles from our camp. We showed ourselves to the soldiers and they quickly rode to the top of a hill and dismounted, placing their horses on the outside for breastworks. It was a round hill, very steep and rocky, and there was no timber on its sides. There were two companies of Mexican cavalry, and we had about sixty warriors. We crept up the hill behind the rocks, and they kept up a constant fire, but I had cautioned our warriors not to expose themselves to the Mexicans.

===Chinese===
内德尼（Nedni）阿帕奇人的酋长沃阿（Whoa）同我在一起，统领一路人马。武士们全体向那部队开进，在离营地约五英里的地方同他们接了仗。我们向那些士兵露出身形，他们便疾驰上山顶，下马把马匹围在外圈充当胸墙。那是一座浑圆的山，又陡又多岩石，山坡上没有树木。墨西哥骑兵有两个连，我们约有六十名武士。我们借着岩石往山上爬，他们火力不停，我早已告诫我们的武士，不要把身子暴露给墨西哥人。

===Original===
I knew that the troopers would waste their ammunition. Soon we had killed all their horses, but the soldiers would lie behind these and shoot at us. While we had killed several Mexicans, we had not yet lost a man. However, it was impossible to get very close to them in this way, and I deemed it best to lead a charge against them.

===Chinese===
我料定那些骑兵会把弹药白白耗掉。不多时，我们把他们的马全打死了，可士兵们就伏在这些死马后面朝我们射击。我们已杀了几个墨西哥人，自己还没折一人。然而这样打法无法逼到他们近前，我看还是率众冲锋为上。

===Original===
We had been fighting ever since about one o’clock, and about the middle of the afternoon, seeing that we were making no further progress, I gave the sign for the advance. The war-whoop sounded and we leaped forward from every stone over the Mexicans’ dead horses, fighting hand to hand. The attack was so sudden that the Mexicans, running first this way and then that, became so confused that in a few minutes we had killed them all. Then we scalped the slain, carried away our dead, and secured all the arms we needed. That night we moved our camp eastward through the Sierra Madre Mountains into Chihuahua. No troops molested us here and after about a year we returned to Arizona.

===Chinese===
从一点钟左右起，我们一直打到午后过半；眼见再无进展，我发出了前进的信号。战嚎响起，我们从一块块岩石后面跃出，越过墨西哥人的死马，短兵相接。这一击来得太突然，墨西哥人先是往这边跑、又往那边跑，乱作一团，几分钟之内就被我们杀了个干净。然后我们剥了阵亡者的头皮，抬走自己的死者，收齐了所需的武器。当夜我们移营东行，穿过马德雷山脉进入奇瓦瓦（Chihuahua）。在那里没有部队来扰，约一年后我们回了亚利桑那。

===Original===
Almost every year we would live a part of the time in Old Mexico. There were at this time many settlements in Arizona; game was not plentiful, and besides we liked to go down into Old Mexico. Besides, the lands of the Nedni Apaches, our friends and kinsmen, extended far into Mexico. Their Chief, Whoa, was as a brother to me, and we spent much of our time in his territory.

===Chinese===
差不多每年，我们都有一段日子住在老墨西哥（Old Mexico）。那时亚利桑那的聚落已经很多；猎物不再充裕，况且我们本来就喜欢南下老墨西哥。再者，我们的朋友和亲族内德尼阿帕奇人的土地，远远伸入墨西哥境内。他们的酋长沃阿待我如同兄弟，我们许多时光就在他的地界上度过。

===Original===
About 1880 we were in camp in the mountains south of Casa Grande, when a company of Mexican troops attacked us. There were twenty-four Mexican soldiers and about forty Indians. The Mexicans surprised us in camp and fired on us, killing two Indians the first volley. I do not know how they were able to find our camp unless they had excellent scouts and our guards were careless, but there they were shooting at us before we knew they were near. We were in the timber, and I gave the order to go forward and fight at close range. We kept behind rocks and trees until we came within ten yards of their line, then we stood up and both sides shot until all the Mexicans were killed. We lost twelve warriors in this battle.

===Chinese===
约莫 1880 年，我们正在卡萨格兰德（Casa Grande）以南的山里扎营，一队墨西哥部队来攻。墨西哥士兵有二十四个，印第安人约有四十个。墨西哥人偷袭了我们的营地，开枪就打，头一排枪打死了两个印第安人。我不知道他们是怎么寻到我们营地的，除非他们有极好的侦察兵、而我们的哨卫疏忽了；总之不等我们察觉他们临近，他们的枪已经朝我们打响了。我们在树林里，我下令前进，近战。我们借着岩石和树木的掩护，一直进到离他们的战线十码之内，然后一齐站起，双方对射，直到墨西哥人被全部打死。这一仗我们折了十二名武士。

===Original===
This place was called by the Indians “Sko-la-ta.” When we had buried our dead and secured what supplies the Mexicans had, we went northeast. At a place near Nacori Mexican troops attacked us. At this place, called by the Indians “Nokode,” there were about eighty warriors, Bedonkohe and Nedni Apaches. There were three companies of Mexican troops. They attacked us in an open field, and we scattered, firing as we ran. They followed us, but we dispersed, and soon were free from their pursuit; then we reassembled in the Sierra Madre Mountains. Here a council was held, and as Mexican troops were coming from many quarters, we disbanded.

===Chinese===
这地方，印第安人叫它「斯科拉塔」（Sko-la-ta）。我们掩埋了死者，收下墨西哥人留下的物资，便往东北去了。在纳科里附近一个地方，墨西哥部队又来攻我们。那地方印第安人叫「诺科德」（Nokode），我们那边约有八十名武士，是贝东科黑（Bedonkohe）和内德尼的阿帕奇人。墨西哥部队有三个连。他们在一片开阔地上攻我们，我们四散奔开，边跑边打。他们追了一程，但我们都散开了，很快便摆脱了追兵；随后我们在马德雷山脉里重新聚齐。在那里开了一次议事会，因为墨西哥部队正从四面八方赶来，我们便各自散去了。

===Original===
In about four months we reassembled at Casa Grande to make a treaty of peace. The chiefs of the town of Casa Grande, and all of the men of Casa Grande, made a treaty with us. We shook hands and promised to be brothers. Then we began to trade, and the Mexicans gave us mescal. Soon nearly all the Indians were drunk. While they were drunk two companies of Mexican troops, from another town, attacked us, killed twenty Indians, and captured many more.22 We fled in all directions.

===Chinese===
约四个月后，我们在卡萨格兰德重新聚齐，要缔结和约。卡萨格兰德镇的首领们和全镇的男人同我们立了约。我们握了手，约定做兄弟。随后我们开始做买卖，墨西哥人给了我们梅斯卡尔酒（mescal）。不多时，印第安人几乎全都醉了。趁他们醉倒，从另一镇开来的两个连墨西哥部队袭击了我们，杀死二十个印第安人，还掳走了更多的人。22 我们向四面八方奔逃。

## XII Geronimo’s Mightiest Battle / 十二 杰罗尼莫最惊天动地的一战

===Original===
After the treachery and massacre of Casa Grande we did not reassemble for a long while, and when we did we returned to Arizona. We remained in Arizona for some time, living in San Carlos Reservation, at a place now called Geronimo. In 1883 we went into Mexico again. We remained in the mountain ranges of Mexico for about fourteen months, and during this time we had many skirmishes with Mexican troops. In 1884 we returned to Arizona to get other Apaches to come with us into Mexico. The Mexicans were gathering troops in the mountains where we had been ranging, and their numbers were so much greater than ours that we could not hope to fight them successfully, and we were tired of being chased about from place to place.

===Chinese===
卡萨格兰德（Casa Grande）那场背信屠杀之后，我们很久没有重新聚齐；重新聚齐时，我们回到了亚利桑那。我们在亚利桑那住了一段时日，住在圣卡洛斯（San Carlos）保留地，就在如今叫作杰罗尼莫的地方。1883 年，我们再次进入墨西哥。我们在墨西哥的群山里待了约十四个月，其间同墨西哥部队打过许多次小仗。1884 年我们回到亚利桑那，去接别的阿帕奇人随我们一同进墨西哥。墨西哥人正在我们游猎过的那些山里集结部队，他们人数远比我们多，我们没有指望能打赢他们；况且我们已被他们从一处追到另一处，追得厌倦了。

===Original===
In Arizona we had trouble with the United States soldiers (explained in next chapter) and returned to Mexico.

===Chinese===
在亚利桑那，我们同美国士兵起了冲突（下一章再讲），便返回了墨西哥。

===Original===
We had lost about fifteen warriors in Arizona, and had gained no recruits. With our reduced number we camped in the mountains north of Arispe. Mexican troops were seen by our scouts in several directions. The United States troops were coming down from the north. We were well armed with guns and supplied with ammunition, but we did not care to be surrounded by the troops of two governments, so we started to move our camp southward.

===Chinese===
我们在亚利桑那折了约十五名武士，没有补充到一个新人。我们带着削减后的人数，在阿里斯佩（Arispe）以北的山里扎营。我们的侦察兵在几个方向都望见了墨西哥部队。美国部队正从北面压下来。我们枪械齐备、弹药充足，却不愿被两个政府的部队围住，于是开始把营地向南迁移。

===Original===
One night we made camp some distance from the mountains by a stream. There was not much water in the stream, but a deep channel was worn through the prairie and small trees were beginning to grow here and there along the bank of this stream.

===Chinese===
一天夜里，我们在离山有一段路程的地方，靠着一条溪流扎了营。溪里水不多，但溪水在草原上冲出了一道深深的河床，沿溪两岸，处处开始长出小树。

===Original===
In those days we never camped without placing scouts, for we knew that we were liable to be attacked at any time. The next morning just at daybreak our scouts came in, aroused the camp, and notified us that Mexican troops were approaching. Within five minutes the Mexicans began firing on us. We took to the ditches made by the stream, and had the women and children busy digging these deeper. I gave strict orders to waste no ammunition and keep under cover. We killed many Mexicans that day and in turn lost heavily, for the fight lasted all day. Frequently troops would charge at one point, be repulsed, then rally and charge at another point.

===Chinese===
那些日子，我们扎营从不曾不放侦察兵，因为我们知道自己随时可能遭到攻击。第二天早晨天刚破晓，我们的侦察兵回来了，唤醒全营，通知我们墨西哥部队正在逼近。不出五分钟，墨西哥人就朝我们开火了。我们跳进溪水冲出的沟渠，让妇女和孩子忙着把沟挖深。我严令不许浪费弹药，不许暴露身子。那一天我们杀了许多墨西哥人，自己也损失惨重，因为这一仗打了一整天。敌队常常朝一处猛冲，被击退后重新集结，又朝另一处猛冲。

===Original===
About noon we began to hear them speaking my name with curses. In the afternoon the general came on the field and the fighting became more furious. I gave orders to my warriors to try to kill all the Mexican officers. About three o’clock the general called all the officers together at the right side of the field. The place where they assembled was not very far from the main stream, and a little ditch ran out close to where the officers stood. Cautiously I crawled out this ditch very close to where the council was being held. The general was an old warrior. The wind was blowing in my direction, so that I could hear all he said, and I23 understood most of it. This is about what he told them: “Officers, yonder in those ditches is the red devil Geronimo and his hated band. This must be his last day. Ride on him from both sides of the ditches; kill men, women, and children; take no prisoners; dead Indians are what we want. Do not spare your own men; exterminate this band at any cost; I will post the wounded to shoot all deserters; go back to your companies and advance.”

===Chinese===
将近正午，我们开始听见他们咒骂着提到我的名字。午后，将军亲临战场，战斗愈见凶猛。我下令我的武士们设法杀死墨西哥军官。三点钟左右，将军把军官们召集到战场右侧。他们集合的地方离主溪不远，一条小沟恰恰延伸到军官们站立处的近旁。我小心地顺着这条沟爬出去，爬到离那集会之处极近的地方。将军是位身经百战的老武士。风正朝我这边吹，他说的话我全听得见，也23听懂了大半。他大致是这样说的：「军官们，那边的沟里，就是红种魔鬼杰罗尼莫和他那伙招人恨的匪帮。今天必须是他的末日。从沟的两面向他冲击；男人、女人、孩子一概杀掉；不许收俘虏；我们要的是死掉的印第安人。不要顾惜你们自己的人；不惜任何代价把这伙人斩尽杀绝；我会叫伤兵守着，枪毙一切逃兵；回到你们的连队，前进。」

===Original===
Just as the command to go forward was given I took deliberate aim at the general and he fell. In an instant the ground around me was riddled with bullets, but I was untouched. The Apaches had seen. From all along the ditches arose the fierce war-cry of my people. The columns wavered an instant and then swept on; they did not retreat until our fire had destroyed the front ranks.

===Chinese===
前进的命令一下，我从容瞄准那将军，一枪将他打倒。霎时间，我四周的地面被子弹打得百孔千疮，我却毫发无伤。阿帕奇人看见了。沿着一条条沟壕，到处腾起我族人凶猛的战嚎。敌军的纵队迟疑了一瞬，随即又席卷而来；直到我们的火力摧垮了他们的前排，他们方才退去。

===Original===
After this their fighting was not so fierce, yet they continued to rally and readvance until dark. They also continued to speak my name with threats and curses. That night before the firing had ceased a dozen Indians had crawled out of the ditches and set fire to the long prairie grass behind the Mexican troops. During the confusion that followed we escaped to the mountains.

===Chinese===
这以后，他们打得不那么凶了，却仍不断重整旗鼓、再度扑来，直到天黑。他们也仍不断威胁着、咒骂着提到我的名字。那天夜里，枪声未歇，十几个印第安人已爬出沟去，在墨西哥部队背后点着了草原上的长草。趁着随之而起的那阵大乱，我们逃进了山里。

===Original===
This was the last battle that I ever fought with Mexicans. United States troops were trailing us continually from this time until the treaty was made with General Miles in Skeleton Canyon.

===Chinese===
这是我此生同墨西哥人打的最后一仗。从这时起，直到在骷髅峡谷（Skeleton Canyon）同迈尔斯将军（General Miles）立约，美国部队一直在尾随追踪我们。

===Original===
During my many wars with the Mexicans I received eight wounds, as follows: shot in the right leg above the knee, and still carry the bullet; shot through the left forearm; wounded in the right leg below the knee with a saber; wounded on top of the head with the butt of a musket; shot just below the outer corner of the left eye; shot in left side; shot in the back. I have killed many Mexicans; I do not know how many, for frequently I did not count them. Some of them were not worth counting.

===Chinese===
在同墨西哥人的多次征战中，我先后八处负伤，情形如下：右腿膝盖上方中枪，子弹至今仍留在腿里；左前臂被子弹打穿；右腿膝盖下方被马刀砍伤；头顶被火枪枪托砸伤；左眼外角下方中枪；左肋中枪；背上中枪。我杀过许多墨西哥人；不知究竟有多少，因为我常常不数。他们中间，有些人不值得数。

===Original===
It has been a long time since then, but still I have no love for the Mexicans. With me they were always treacherous and malicious. I am old now and shall never go on the warpath again, but if I were young, and followed the warpath, it would lead into Old Mexico.

===Chinese===
从那时起，已经过去很久了，可我对墨西哥人依然没有一丝情义。在我身上，他们从来都是奸诈而恶毒的。如今我老了，再不会出征了；可我若还年轻，还走那征途，那路必是通向老墨西哥（Old Mexico）的。

## Part III The White Men / 第三部分 白人

## XIII Coming of the White Men / 十三 白人的到来

===Original===
About the time of the massacre of Kaskiyeh (1858) we heard that some white men were measuring land to the south of us. In company with a number of other warriors I went to visit them. We could not understand them very well, for we had no interpreter, but we made a treaty with them by shaking hands and promising to be brothers. Then we made our camp near their camp, and they came to trade with us. We gave them buckskin, blankets, and ponies in exchange for shirts and provisions. We also brought them game, for which they gave us some money. We did not know the value of this money, but we kept it and later learned from the Navajo Indians that it was very valuable.

===Chinese===
大约在卡斯基耶（Kaskiyeh）大屠杀（1858 年）的时候，我们听说有一些白人在我们南边丈量土地。我同另外几名武士一起去探望他们。我们听不太懂他们的话，因为我们没有通译，但我们同他们握了手、约定做兄弟，就此立了约。随后我们把营扎在他们营地近旁，他们来同我们做买卖。我们拿鹿皮、毯子和矮种马换他们的衬衣和粮食。我们也给他们送去猎物，他们为此给了我们一些钱。我们不知道这钱的价值，还是把它收下了，后来从纳瓦霍（Navajo）印第安人那里得知，这钱很贵重。

===Original===
Every day they measured land with curious instruments and put down marks which we could not understand. They were good men, and we were sorry when they had gone on into the west. They were not soldiers. These were the first white men I ever saw.

===Chinese===
他们天天用些奇异的器具丈量土地，画下一些我们看不懂的记号。他们是好人，他们继续西去之后，我们还很惋惜。他们不是士兵。这是我平生见到的第一批白人。

===Original===
About ten years later some more white men came. These were all warriors. They made their camp on the Gila River south of Hot Springs. At first they were friendly and we did not dislike them, but they were not as good as those who came first.

===Chinese===
约十年之后，又来了一些白人。这些人个个都是武士。他们在温泉（Hot Springs）以南的希拉河（Gila River）畔扎营。起初他们还算友善，我们也不厌恶他们，但他们不如先来的那些人好。

===Original===
After about a year some trouble arose between them and the Indians, and I took the warpath as a warrior, not as a chief.24 I had not been wronged, but some of my people had been, and I fought with my tribe; for the soldiers and not the Indians were at fault.

===Chinese===
约一年之后，他们同印第安人之间起了纠纷，我便出征了，是以武士的身份，不是以酋长的身份。24 我自己并没有受什么冤屈，可我的一些族人受过，我便同我的部落一起作战；因为错在士兵，不在印第安人。

===Original===
Not long after this some of the officers of the United States troops invited our leaders to hold a conference at Apache Pass (Fort Bowie). Just before noon the Indians were shown into a tent and told that they would be given something to eat. When in the tent they were25 attacked by soldiers. Our chief, Mangus-Colorado, and several other warriors, by cutting through the tent, escaped; but most of the warriors were killed or captured. Among the Bedonkohe Apaches killed at this time were Sanza, Kladetahe, Niyokahe, and Gopi. After this treachery the Indians went back to the mountains and left the fort entirely alone. I do not think that the agent had anything to do with planning this, for he had always treated us well. I believe it was entirely planned by the soldiers.

===Chinese===
此后不久，美国部队的几名军官邀请我们的首领到阿帕奇山口（Apache Pass，即鲍伊堡 Fort Bowie）开会。将近正午，印第安人被引进一顶帐篷，说会给他们东西吃。他们一进帐篷，便25遭到士兵的攻击。我们的酋长曼加斯-科罗拉多（Mangus-Colorado）和另外几名武士割开帐篷逃了出去；但大部分武士被杀或被俘。当时被杀的贝东科黑（Bedonkohe）阿帕奇人之中，有桑扎（Sanza）、克拉德塔赫（Kladetahe）、尼约卡赫（Niyokahe）和戈皮（Gopi）。这次背信之后，印第安人回到山里，全然不再理会那堡垒。我想那位代理官（agent）同这个谋划没有什么相干，因为他一向待我们很好。我相信这完全是士兵们谋划的。

===Original===
From26 the very first the soldiers sent out to our western country, and the officers in charge of them, did not hesitate to wrong the Indians. They never explained to the Government when an Indian was wronged, but always reported the misdeeds of the Indians. Much that was done by mean white men was reported at Washington as the deeds of my people.

===Chinese===
从26 一开始，派到我们西部地方来的士兵，以及统领他们的军官，作践印第安人从不迟疑。印第安人受了冤屈，他们从不向政府申明；印第安人的过失，他们却回回上报。卑劣白人干的许多事，都被报到华盛顿，算作我族人的作为。

===Original===
The Indians always tried to live peaceably with the white soldiers and settlers. One day during the time that the soldiers were stationed at Apache Pass I made a treaty with the post. This was done by shaking hands and promising to be brothers. Cochise and Mangus-Colorado did likewise. I do not know the name of the officer in command, but this was the first regiment that ever came to Apache Pass. This treaty was made about a year before we were attacked in a tent, as above related. In a few days after the attack at Apache Pass we organized in the mountains and returned to fight the soldiers. There were two tribes﻿—the Bedonkohe and the Chokonen Apaches, both commanded by Cochise. After a few days’ skirmishing we attacked a freight train that was coming in with supplies for the Fort. We killed some of the men and captured the others. These prisoners our chief offered to trade for the Indians whom the soldiers had captured at the massacre in the tent. This the officers refused, so we killed our prisoners, disbanded, and went into hiding in the mountains. Of those who took part in this affair I am the only one now living.

===Chinese===
印第安人总想同白人士兵和白人移民平安相处。士兵们驻扎在阿帕奇山口期间，有一天我同驻军立了约。立约的方式，是握手，约定做兄弟。柯奇斯（Cochise）和曼加斯-科罗拉多也照样立了约。我不知道指挥官的名字，但这是开到阿帕奇山口来的第一个团。这约立下约一年之后，我们才在帐篷里遭到攻击，情形如前所述。阿帕奇山口事发之后没几天，我们在山里集结起来，回去同士兵们交战。参战的有两个部落——贝东科黑阿帕奇和乔科嫩（Chokonen）阿帕奇——都由柯奇斯统领。小规模接仗几天之后，我们袭击了一支为堡垒运送补给的货运车队。我们杀了车上一些人，俘了其余的。我们的酋长提出用这些俘虏，去换士兵们在帐篷屠杀中掳走的印第安人。军官们不肯，我们便杀了我们的俘虏，各自散去，躲进山里。参与此事的人当中，如今在世的只剩我一个。

===Original===
In a few days troops were sent out to search for us, but as we were disbanded, it was, of course, impossible for them to locate any hostile camp. During the time they were searching for us many of our warriors (who were thought by the soldiers to be peaceable Indians) talked to the officers and men, advising them where they might find the camp they sought, and while they searched we watched them from our hiding places and laughed at their failures.

===Chinese===
过了几天，部队被派出来搜寻我们，但我们已经散伙，他们当然寻不到任何敌对的营地。他们搜寻我们的时候，我们的许多武士（士兵们以为他们是安分的印第安人）同军官和士兵们搭话，指点他们到何处去寻那要找的营地；他们搜寻时，我们便从藏身之处望着他们，笑话他们的一无所获。

===Original===
After this trouble all of the Indians agreed not to be friendly with the white men any more. There was no general engagement, but a long struggle followed. Sometimes we attacked the white men﻿—sometimes they attacked us. First a few Indians would be killed and then a few soldiers. I think the killing was about equal on each side. The number killed in these troubles did not amount to much, but this treachery on the part of the soldiers had angered the Indians and revived memories of other wrongs, so that we never again trusted the United States troops.

===Chinese===
这场事之后，所有印第安人都约定，再不同白人友善相待。没有过大规模的会战，接踵而来的却是一场漫长的争斗。有时我们攻白人——有时白人攻我们。先是死几个印第安人，再是死几个士兵。我想双方被杀的人数大致相当。这些纠纷里死的人算不上多，可士兵们这一场背信激怒了印第安人，唤起了往日别的冤屈的记忆，从此我们再也不信任美国部队。

## XIV Greatest of Wrongs / 十四 最深重的冤屈

===Original===
Perhaps the greatest wrong ever done to the Indians was the treatment received by our tribe from the United States troops about 1863. The chief of our tribe, Mangus-Colorado, went to make a treaty of peace for our people with the white settlement at Apache Tejo, New Mexico. It had been reported to us that the white men in this settlement were more friendly and more reliable than those in Arizona, that they would live up to their treaties and would not wrong the Indians.

===Chinese===
印第安人所受的一切冤屈之中，最深重的也许要数大约 1863 年我们部落遭受美国部队的那番对待。我们部落的酋长曼加斯-科罗拉多（Mangus-Colorado），为我们的族人去同新墨西哥（New Mexico）阿帕奇特霍（Apache Tejo）的白人定居地议和立约。我们听说，这处定居地的白人比亚利桑那（Arizona）的白人更友善、更可靠，他们会信守条约，不会亏待印第安人。

===Original===
Mangus-Colorado, with three other warriors, went to Apache Tejo and held a council with these citizens and soldiers. They told him that if he would come with his tribe and live near them, they would issue to him, from the Government, blankets, flour, provisions, beef, and all manner of supplies. Our chief promised to return to Apache Tejo within two weeks. When he came back to our settlement he assembled the whole tribe in council. I did not believe that the people at Apache Tejo would do as they said and therefore I opposed the plan, but it was decided that with part of the tribe Mangus-Colorado should return to Apache Tejo and receive an issue of rations and supplies. If they were as represented, and if these white men would keep the treaty faithfully, the remainder of the tribe would join him and we would make our permanent home at Apache Tejo. I was to remain in charge of that portion of the tribe which stayed in Arizona. We gave almost all of our arms and ammunition to the party going to Apache Tejo, so that in case there should be treachery they would be prepared for any surprise. Mangus-Colorado and about half of our people went to New Mexico, happy that now they had found white men who would be kind to them, and with whom they could live in peace and plenty.

===Chinese===
曼加斯-科罗拉多带着另外三名武士去了阿帕奇特霍，同那里的居民和士兵们开了议事会。他们对他说，只要他带着部落来住在他们近旁，他们就会替政府发给他毯子、面粉、粮食、牛肉和各式各样的物资。我们的酋长答应两周之内回到阿帕奇特霍。他回到我们的居地之后，把全部落召集起来开议事会。我不信阿帕奇特霍的人会照他们说的做，因此反对这个打算；但最后议定，由曼加斯-科罗拉多带着一部分部落回阿帕奇特霍去，领一次配发的口粮和物资。倘若他们名实相符，倘若这些白人忠实地守约，部落其余的人就去同他会合，我们便在阿帕奇特霍安家，永住下去。我则留下来，管带留在亚利桑那的那一部分族人。我们把几乎全部的枪械弹药都交给去阿帕奇特霍的那一拨人，这样一旦对方使诈，他们也好应付任何偷袭。曼加斯-科罗拉多带着约半数族人去了新墨西哥，满心欢喜，只道这一回寻着了肯善待他们的白人，可以同这些白人一道，太平安足地过活了。

===Original===
No word ever came to us from them. From other sources, however, we heard that they had been treacherously27 captured and slain. In this dilemma we did not know just exactly what to do, but fearing that the troops who had captured them would attack us, we retreated into the mountains near Apache Pass.

===Chinese===
从此再没有他们的半点音信传来。不过我们从别的渠道听说，他们中了奸计，27 被俘之后被杀死了。进退两难之际，我们不知究竟如何是好，但生怕掳走他们的那支部队再来攻我们，便退进了阿帕奇山口（Apache Pass）附近的山里。

===Original===
During the weeks that followed the departure of our people we had been in suspense, and failing to provide more supplies, had exhausted all of our store of provisions. This was another reason for moving camp. On this retreat, while passing through the mountains, we discovered four men with a herd of cattle. Two of the men were in front in a buggy and two were behind on horseback. We killed all four, but did not scalp them; they were not warriors. We drove the cattle back into the mountains, made a camp, and began to kill the cattle and pack the meat.

===Chinese===
族人离去后的那几个星期，我们一直悬着心，又没能再筹到物资，存粮已吃得精光。这也是拔营他迁的又一个缘故。撤退途中穿山而行时，我们发现四个男人赶着一群牛。两人坐在前头的一辆轻便马车上，两人骑马跟在后面。我们把四个人都杀了，但没有剥他们的头皮；他们不是武士。我们把牛群赶回山里，扎下营，动手宰牛、装贮牛肉。

===Original===
Before we had finished this work we were surprised and attacked by United States troops, who killed in all seven Indians﻿—one warrior, three women, and three children. The Government troops were mounted and so were we, but we were poorly armed, having given most of our weapons to the division of our tribe that had gone to Apache Tejo, so we fought mainly with spears, bows, and arrows. At first I had a spear, a bow, and a few arrows; but in a short time my spear and all my arrows were gone. Once I was surrounded, but by dodging from side to side of my horse as he ran I escaped. It was necessary during this fight for many of the warriors to leave their horses and escape on foot. But my horse was trained to come at call, and as soon as I reached a safe place, if not too closely pursued, I would call him to me.28 During this fight we scattered in all directions and two days later reassembled at our appointed place of rendezvous, about fifty miles from the scene of this battle.

===Chinese===
这活计还没有做完，美国部队便突袭了我们，一共杀了七个印第安人——一名武士、三个女人、三个孩子。政府的部队骑着马，我们也骑着马，可我们的武器太差，大部分刀枪都交给了去阿帕奇特霍的那部分族人，只好主要靠长矛、弓和箭作战。起初我有一杆长矛、一张弓和几支箭；可没过多久，长矛和箭就全用光了。有一回我被围住，但我伏在飞跑的马身上左右闪躲，脱了身。这一仗里，许多武士只得撇下马匹，徒步脱身。好在我的马驯得闻唤即来，我一到安全的地方，只要追兵逼得不太紧，就把它唤到身边来。28 这一仗里我们四散而去，两天后在一处约定的会合地点重新聚齐，那里离战场约有五十英里。

===Original===
About ten days later the same United States troops attacked our new camp at sunrise. The fight lasted all day, but our arrows and spears were all gone before ten o’clock, and for the remainder of the day we had only rocks and clubs with which to fight. We could do little damage with these weapons, and at night we moved our camp about four miles back into the mountains where it would be hard for the cavalry to follow us. The next day our scouts, who had been left behind to observe the movements of the soldiers, returned, saying that the troops had gone back toward San Carlos Reservation.

===Chinese===
约十天后，同一支美国部队在日出时分袭击了我们的新营地。这一仗打了一整天，可还不到十点，我们的箭和矛就用尽了，这一天的其余时候，我们手里只剩石块和木棒可以作战。凭这些家伙伤不了敌人多少，入夜之后，我们把营地朝山里后撤了约四英里，退到骑兵难以跟进的地方。第二天，我们留下来监视士兵动向的侦察兵回来了，说那支部队已经折回圣卡洛斯（San Carlos）保留地方向去了。

===Original===
A few days after this we were again attacked by another company of United States troops. Just before this fight we had been joined by a band of Chokonen Indians under Cochise, who took command of both divisions. We were repulsed, and decided to disband.

===Chinese===
此后几天，另一连美国部队又来攻击我们。临战之前，柯奇斯（Cochise）手下的一支乔科嫩（Chokonen）印第安人来同我们会合，两支人马都归他统领。我们被击退了，便决定散伙。

===Original===
After we had disbanded our tribe the Bedonkohe Apaches reassembled near their old camp vainly waiting for the return of Mangus-Colorado and our kinsmen. No tidings came save that they had all been treacherously slain.29 Then a council was held, and as it was believed that Mangus-Colorado was dead, I was elected Tribal Chief.

===Chinese===
部落散伙之后，贝东科黑（Bedonkohe）阿帕奇人在旧营地近旁重新聚拢，徒然等候曼加斯-科罗拉多和我们的亲人归来。传来的唯一音讯，就是他们全都被奸计害死了。29 于是开了一场议事会，既然大家都相信曼加斯-科罗拉多已经死了，我便被推举为部落酋长。

===Original===
For a long time we had no trouble with anyone. It was more than a year after I had been made Tribal Chief that United States troops surprised and attacked our camp. They killed seven children, five women, and four warriors, captured all our supplies, blankets, horses, and clothing, and destroyed our teepees. We had nothing left; winter was beginning, and it was the coldest winter I ever knew. After the soldiers withdrew I took three warriors and trailed them. Their trail led back toward San Carlos.

===Chinese===
此后很长一段时间，我们同谁也没有起过纠纷。我做了部落酋长一年多之后，美国部队突袭了我们的营地。他们杀了七个孩子、五个女人、四名武士，掳走了我们全部的物资、毯子、马匹和衣裳，捣毁了我们的梯皮帐篷（teepee）。我们一无所有了；冬天已经开始，那是我平生所知最冷的一个冬天。士兵们撤走之后，我带了三名武士，循迹追踪他们。他们的踪迹，折向圣卡洛斯方向去了。

## XV Removals / 十五 迁徙

===Original===
While returning from trailing the Government troops we saw two men, a Mexican and a white man, and shot them off their horses. With these two horses we returned and moved our camp. My people were suffering much and it was deemed advisable to go where we could get more provisions. Game was scarce in our range then, and since I had been Tribal Chief I had not asked for rations from the Government, nor did I care to do so, but we did not wish to starve.

===Chinese===
我们追踪政府部队回来时，望见两个人，一个墨西哥人和一个白人，把他们从马上射了下来。我们带着这两匹马回到营地，拔营迁徙。我的族人苦不堪言，大家觉得还是迁到能多弄到些粮食的地方为好。那时我们猎场里的野物稀少，自从做了部落酋长，我没有向政府讨过口粮，也不愿去讨，可我们总不想活活饿死。

===Original===
We had heard that Chief Victoria of the Chihenne (Oje Caliente) Apaches was holding a council with the white men near Hot Springs in New Mexico, and that he had plenty of provisions. We had always been on friendly terms with this tribe, and Victoria was especially kind to my people. With the help of the two horses we had captured, to carry our sick with us, we went to Hot Springs. We easily found Victoria and his band, and they gave us supplies for the winter. We stayed with them for about a year, and during this stay we had perfect peace. We had not the least trouble with Mexicans, white men, or Indians. When we had stayed as long as we should, and had again accumulated some supplies, we decided to leave Victoria’s band. When I told him that we were going to leave he said that we should have a feast and dance before we separated.

===Chinese===
我们听说，奇亨内（Chihenne，Oje Caliente，即温泉）阿帕奇人的酋长维多利亚（Victoria），正在新墨西哥（New Mexico）温泉（Hot Springs）附近同白人们开议事会，而且他那里粮食充足。我们同这个部落一向交好，维多利亚待我的族人尤其亲善。靠着俘来的那两匹马驮上我们的病人，我们前往温泉。我们不费事就寻到了维多利亚和他的队伍，他们给了我们过冬的物资。我们在他们那里住了约一年，这一年间我们享尽了太平。同墨西哥人、白人、印第安人，我们连一点纠纷也没有。住满了该住的时日，又攒下了一些物资之后，我们决定离开维多利亚的队伍。我告诉他我们要走了，他说，分手之前大家该设宴欢舞一场。

===Original===
The festivities were held about two miles above Hot Springs, and lasted for four days. There were about four hundred Indians at this celebration. I do not think we ever spent a more pleasant time than upon this occasion. No one ever treated our tribe more kindly than Victoria and his band. We are still proud to say that he and his people were our friends.

===Chinese===
欢庆设在温泉上游约两英里的地方，一连热闹了四天。参加这场欢庆的印第安人约有四百。我想我们从来不曾有过比这更快活的时光。待我们部落最厚道的，莫过于维多利亚和他的队伍。直到今天，我们仍能骄傲地说，他和他的族人曾是我们的朋友。

===Original===
When I went to Apache Pass (Fort Bowie) I found General Howard30 in command, and made a treaty with him. This treaty lasted until long after General Howard had left our country. He always kept his word with us and treated us as brothers. We never had so good a friend among the United States officers as General Howard. We could have lived forever at peace with him. If there is any pure, honest white man in the United States army, that man is General Howard. All the Indians respect him, and even to this day frequently talk of the happy times when General Howard was in command of our Post. After he went away he placed an agent at Apache Pass who issued to us from the Government clothing, rations, and supplies, as General Howard directed. When beef was issued to the Indians I got twelve steers for my tribe, and Cochise got twelve steers for his tribe. Rations were issued about once a month, but if we ran out we only had to ask and we were supplied. Now, as prisoners of war in this Reservation, we do not get such good rations.31

===Chinese===
我到阿帕奇山口（Apache Pass，即鲍伊堡 Fort Bowie）去的时候，见统管那里的是霍华德将军（General Howard）30，我便同他立了约。这条约一直维持到霍华德将军离开我们这片地方之后许久。他同我们说话从来算数，待我们如同兄弟。在美国军官当中，我们再没有过像霍华德将军这样好的朋友。同他在一起，我们本可以永享太平。倘若美国军队里还有纯良诚实的白人，那人便是霍华德将军。所有印第安人都敬重他，直到今天，还常常谈起霍华德将军统管我们那处驻地的快活日子。他临走时在阿帕奇山口派了一名代理官（agent），照霍华德将军的嘱咐，替政府向我们发放衣裳、口粮和物资。给印第安人发牛肉的时候，我为我的部落领到十二头阉牛，柯奇斯（Cochise）也为他的部落领到十二头。口粮大约每月发放一次，可若是吃尽了，只消开口讨，就会补给我们。如今我们作为战俘住在这片保留地上，就再领不到这样好的口粮了。31

===Original===
Out on the prairie away from Apache Pass a man kept a store and saloon. Some time after General Howard went away a band of outlawed Indians killed this man, and took away many of the supplies from his store. On the very next day after this some Indians at the Post were drunk on *tiswin*, which they had made from corn. They fought among themselves and four of them were killed. There had been quarrels and feuds among them for some time, and after this trouble we deemed it impossible to keep the different bands together in peace. Therefore we separated, each leader taking his own band. Some of them went to San Carlos and some to Old Mexico, but I took my tribe back to Hot Springs and rejoined Victoria’s band.

===Chinese===
在远离阿帕奇山口的草原上，有一个人开着一间铺子和一间酒馆。霍华德将军走后过了一阵，一伙亡命的印第安人杀了这个人，把他铺子里的许多货物掳了去。就在出事的第二天，驻地上的几个印第安人喝蒂斯温（*tiswin*）喝醉了，那酒是他们用玉米酿的。他们自相斗殴，死了四个。他们之间早有些口角仇怨，经这一场乱子，我们认为再要让各支队伍平安聚在一处，已是不可能了。于是我们分开，各首领带走自己的一支。有的去了圣卡洛斯（San Carlos），有的去了老墨西哥（Old Mexico），我却带着我的部落回到温泉，重新投到维多利亚的队伍里。

## XVI In Prison and on the Warpath / 十六 狱中与出征

===Original===
Soon after we arrived in New Mexico two companies of scouts were sent from San Carlos. When they came to Hot Springs they sent word for me and Victoria to come to town. The messengers did not say what they wanted with us, but as they seemed friendly we thought they wanted a council, and rode in to meet the officers. As soon as we arrived in town soldiers met us, disarmed us, and took us both to headquarters, where we were tried by court-martial. They asked us only a few questions and then Victoria was released and I was sentenced to the guardhouse. Scouts conducted me to the guardhouse and put me in chains. When I asked them why they did this they said it was because I had left Apache Pass.

===Chinese===
我们到了新墨西哥（New Mexico）不久，圣卡洛斯（San Carlos）派来了两个连的侦察兵。他们到了温泉（Hot Springs），捎话叫我和维多利亚（Victoria）进城去。传信的人没有说来寻我们做什么，但他们看去并无恶意，我们以为是要开议事会，便骑马进城去见军官们。我们刚一进城，士兵们就迎了上来，下了我们的械，把我们两个都带到司令部，在那里受军事法庭的审讯。他们只问了我们几句话，随后维多利亚被开释，我被判押入禁闭室。侦察兵把我押到禁闭室，给我戴上了镣铐。我问他们为什么这样待我，他们说，是因为我离开了阿帕奇山口（Apache Pass）。

===Original===
I do not think that I ever belonged to those soldiers at Apache Pass, or that I should have asked them where I might go. Our bands could no longer live in peace32 together, and so we had quietly withdrawn, expecting to live with Victoria’s band, where we thought we would not be molested. They also sentenced seven other Apaches to chains in the guardhouse.

===Chinese===
我从不认为我归阿帕奇山口那些士兵管辖，也不认为我出门该向他们讨去处。我们各支队伍再也无法平安32共处了，于是我们悄悄退出，指望同维多利亚的队伍住到一处，想着在那里不会再受搅扰。他们还判了另外七个阿帕奇人戴镣铐关禁闭室。

===Original===
I do not know why this was done, for these Indians had simply followed me from Apache Pass to Hot Springs. If it was wrong (and I do not think it was wrong) for us to go to Hot Springs, I alone was to blame. They asked the soldiers in charge why they were imprisoned and chained, but received no answer.

===Chinese===
我不明白为什么要这样处置，因为这些印第安人不过是跟着我从阿帕奇山口来到温泉罢了。倘若去温泉是桩错处（我并不认为那是桩错处），罪责也全在我一人。他们问看管的士兵，为什么把他们关起来、锁上镣铐，却没有得到回答。

===Original===
I was kept a prisoner for four months, during which time I was transferred to San Carlos. Then I think I had another trial, although I was not present. In fact I do not know that I had another trial, but I was told that I had, and at any rate I was released.

===Chinese===
我被关押了四个月，其间又被解送到圣卡洛斯。之后我想我又受了一回审判，虽然我并没有到场。其实我并不知道有没有这么一回审判，只是听人说有过，不管怎样，我被开释了。

===Original===
After this we had no more trouble with the soldiers, but I never felt at ease any longer at the Post. We were allowed to live above San Carlos at a place now called Geronimo. A man whom the Indians called Nick Golee was agent at this place. All went well here for a period of two years, but we were not satisfied.

===Chinese===
这以后我们同士兵们再没有起过纠纷，可我在那处驻地上，心里再不得安稳。他们准许我们住在圣卡洛斯上游一个地方，如今那里叫做杰罗尼莫（Geronimo）。有一个印第安人唤作尼克·戈利（Nick Golee）的人，在那里当代理官。这地方两年间诸事顺遂，可我们心里并不踏实。

===Original===
In the summer of 1883 a rumor was current that the officers were again planning to imprison our leaders. This rumor served to revive the memory of all our past wrongs﻿—the massacre in the tent at Apache Pass, the fate of Mangus-Colorado, and my own unjust imprisonment, which might easily have been death to me. Just at this time we were told that the officers wanted us to come up the river above Geronimo to a fort (Fort Thomas) to hold a council with them. We did not believe that any good could come of this conference, or that there was any need of it; so we held a council ourselves, and fearing treachery, decided to leave the reservation. We thought it more manly to die on the warpath than to be killed in prison.

===Chinese===
1883 年夏天，风声传开，说军官们又在谋划监禁我们的首领。这风声把我们历次冤屈的记忆全勾了起来——阿帕奇山口帐篷里那场屠杀、曼加斯-科罗拉多（Mangus-Colorado）的下场，还有我自身那场不公的监禁，那一遭本来很容易便要了我的命。恰在此时，有人来对我们说，军官们要我们沿河上到杰罗尼莫上方的一座堡垒（托马斯堡 Fort Thomas）去，同他们开议事会。我们不信这场会商能有什么好处，也不信有什么必要；于是我们自己开了议事会，又怕中了奸计，便决意离开保留地。我们想，死在征途上，总比死在牢里更像个汉子。

===Original===
There were in all about 250 Indians, chiefly the Bedonkohe and Nedni Apaches, led by myself and Whoa. We went through Apache Pass and just west of there had a fight with the United States troops. In this battle we killed three soldiers and lost none.

===Chinese===
一共约有二百五十名印第安人，主要是贝东科黑（Bedonkohe）阿帕奇和内德尼（Nedni）阿帕奇，由我和沃阿（Whoa）统领。我们取道阿帕奇山口，在山口正西同美国部队打了一仗。这一仗我们杀了三个士兵，自己一个没折。

===Original===
We went on toward Old Mexico, but on the second day after this United States soldiers overtook us about three o’clock in the afternoon and we fought until dark. The ground where we were attacked was very rough, which was to our advantage, for the troops were compelled to dismount in order to fight us. I do not know how many soldiers we killed, but we lost only one warrior and three children. We had plenty of guns and ammunition at this time. Many of the guns and much ammunition we had accumulated while living in the reservation, and the remainder we had obtained from the White Mountain Apaches when we left the reservation.

===Chinese===
我们继续朝老墨西哥（Old Mexico）去，可这之后的第二天下午三点钟光景，美国士兵追上了我们，我们一直打到天黑。我们遭袭的那片地界崎岖险恶，这于我们有利，因为部队要同我们交手，就不得不下马。我们杀了多少士兵，我不知道；我们只折了一名武士和三个孩子。这时我们的枪支弹药很充足。许多枪支和弹药，是我们住在保留地时积攒下的；其余的，是我们离开保留地时从白山阿帕奇（White Mountain Apaches）手里得来的。

===Original===
Troops did not follow us any longer, so we went south almost to Casa Grande and camped in the Sierra de Sahuaripa Mountains. We ranged in the mountains of Old Mexico for about a year, then returned to San Carlos, taking with us a herd of cattle and horses.

===Chinese===
部队不再追了，我们便南行，几乎到了卡萨格兰德（Casa Grande），在萨瓦里帕山脉（Sierra de Sahuaripa）扎了营。我们在老墨西哥的群山里往来盘桓了约一年，随后赶着一群牛马，回到圣卡洛斯。

===Original===
Soon after we arrived at San Carlos the officer in charge, General Crook, took the horses and cattle away from us. I told him that these were not white men’s cattle, but belonged to us, for we had taken them from the Mexicans during our wars. I also told him that we did not intend to kill these animals, but that we wished to keep them and raise stock on our range. He would not listen to me, but took the stock. I went up near Fort Apache and General Crook ordered officers, soldiers, and scouts to see that I was arrested; if I offered resistance they were instructed to kill me.

===Chinese===
我们到圣卡洛斯不久，主事的军官克鲁克将军（General Crook）便把我们的马和牛都夺了去。我告诉他，这些不是白人的牛，本是我们自己的，是我们历次同墨西哥人交战时从他们手里夺来的。我还告诉他，我们并不打算宰杀这些牲口，只想把它们留下，在自己的地界上牧养牲畜。他不肯听我的，还是把牲口夺走了。我上到阿帕奇堡（Fort Apache）附近，克鲁克将军便命令军官、士兵和侦察兵务必把我捉拿；我若抗拒，便格杀勿论。

===Original===
This information was brought to me by the Indians. When I learned of this proposed action I left for Old Mexico, and about four hundred Indians went with me. They were the Bedonkohe, Chokonen, and Nedni Apaches. At this time Whoa was dead, and Naiche was the only chief with me. We went south into Sonora and camped in the mountains. Troops followed us, but did not attack us until we were camped in the mountains west of Casa Grande. Here we were attacked by Government Indian scouts. One boy was killed and nearly all of our women and children were captured.33

===Chinese===
这消息是印第安人带给我的。我得知这一图谋，便动身前往老墨西哥，随我去的约有四百名印第安人。他们是贝东科黑、乔科嫩（Chokonen）和内德尼阿帕奇人。那时沃阿已经死了，随我的酋长只剩下纳切（Naiche）一个。我们南下入索诺拉（Sonora），在山里扎营。部队尾随着我们，却一直没有动手，直到我们在卡萨格兰德以西的山里扎下营。在这里，政府的印第安侦察兵袭击了我们。一个男孩被打死，我们的妇人孩子几乎全被掳走。33

===Original===
After this battle we went south of Casa Grande and made a camp, but within a few days this camp was attacked by Mexican soldiers. We skirmished with them all day, killing a few Mexicans, but sustaining no loss ourselves.

===Chinese===
这一仗之后，我们到了卡萨格兰德以南扎营，可没过几天，这营地又遭到墨西哥士兵的攻击。我们同他们周旋了一整天，杀了几个墨西哥人，自己却毫无折损。

===Original===
That night we went east into the foothills of the Sierra Madre Mountains and made another camp. Mexican troops trailed us, and after a few days attacked our camp again. This time the Mexicans had a very large army, and we avoided a general engagement. It is senseless to fight when you cannot hope to win.

===Chinese===
