当夜我们向东进了马德雷山脉（Sierra Madre Mountains）的山麓丘陵，另扎一营。墨西哥部队循迹追来，几天之后又来攻我们的营。这一回墨西哥人兵马极众，我们便避而不决战。明知没有取胜之望还要硬拼，是愚蠢的。

===Original===
That night we held a council of war; our scouts had reported bands of United States and Mexican troops at many points in the mountains. We estimated that about two thousand soldiers were ranging these mountains seeking to capture us.

===Chinese===
当夜我们开了作战会议；我们的侦察兵报称，美国部队和墨西哥部队的一队队人马，在山间许多地方出没。我们估计，大约有两千名士兵在这片山里四处搜捕我们。

===Original===
General Crook had come down into Mexico with the United States troops. They were camped in the Sierra de Antunez Mountains. Scouts told me that General Crook wished to see me and I went to his camp. When I arrived General Crook said to me, “Why did you leave the reservation?” I said: “You told me that I might live in the reservation the same as white people lived. One year I raised a crop of corn, and gathered and stored it, and the next year I put in a crop of oats, and when the crop was almost ready to harvest, you told your soldiers to put me in prison, and if I resisted to kill me. If I had been let alone I would now have been in good circumstances, but instead of that you and the Mexicans are hunting me with soldiers.” He said: “I never gave any such orders; the troops at Fort Apache, who spread this report, knew that it was untrue.” Then I agreed to go back with him to San Carlos.

===Chinese===
克鲁克将军已带着美国部队南下进了墨西哥。他们扎营在安图内斯山脉（Sierra de Antunez）。侦察兵告诉我，克鲁克将军想要见我，我便去了他的营地。我到的时候，克鲁克将军对我说：「你为什么离开保留地？」我说：「你曾对我说，我可以住在保留地里，像白人那样过活。有一年我种了一茬玉米，收好贮存起来；第二年我又种下一茬燕麦，眼看庄稼快能收割了，你却吩咐你的士兵把我关进监狱，我若抗拒便杀了我。倘若无人搅扰，我如今本可以过得宽裕；非但如此，你和墨西哥人反倒驱使着士兵四处追捕我。」他说：「我从来没有下过这样的命令；阿帕奇堡那边散布这话的部队，自己就知道那话不实。」于是我答应随他回圣卡洛斯去。

===Original===
It was hard for me to believe him at that time. Now I know that what he said was untrue,34 and I firmly believe that he did issue the orders for me to be put in prison, or to be killed in case I offered resistance.

===Chinese===
那时我很难相信他的话。如今我才知道他说的不是实话，34 而且我坚信，他确实下过把我关进监狱的命令，还下令我若抗拒便将我杀死。

## XVII The Final Struggle / 十七 最后的抗争

===Original===
We started with all our tribe to go with General Crook back to the United States, but I feared treachery and decided to remain in Mexico. We were not under any guard at this time. The United States troops marched in front and the Indians followed, and when we became suspicious, we turned back. I do not know how far the United States army went after myself, and some warriors turned back before we were missed, and I do not care.

===Chinese===
我们全部落的人起程，要随克鲁克将军（General Crook）回美国去，可我怕其中有诈，便决意留在墨西哥。这时并没有人看守我们。美国部队行在前面，印第安人跟在后面，待我们起了疑心，便折了回去。美国军队追了我多远，我不知道；有些武士趁人未察觉也折了回去；这些我都不放在心上。

===Original===
I have suffered much from such unjust orders as those of General Crook. Such acts have caused much distress to my people. I think that General Crook’s death35 was sent by the Almighty as a punishment for the many evil deeds he committed.

===Chinese===
像克鲁克将军下的这类不公的命令，我吃过许多苦头。这样的行径给我的族人带来了深重的苦难。我想，克鲁克将军之死35，是全能的神降下的惩罚，罚他生前作下的诸多恶事。

===Original===
Soon General Miles was made commander of all the western posts, and troops trailed us continually. They were led by Captain Lawton, who had good scouts. The Mexican36 soldiers also became more active and more numerous. We had skirmishes almost every day, and so we finally decided to break up into small bands. With six men and four women I made for the range of mountains near Hot Springs, New Mexico. We passed many cattle ranches, but had no trouble with the cowboys. We killed cattle to eat whenever we were in need of food, but we frequently suffered greatly for water. At one time we had no water for two days and nights and our horses almost died from thirst. We ranged in the mountains of New Mexico for some time, then thinking that perhaps the troops had left Mexico, we returned. On our return through Old Mexico we attacked every Mexican found, even if for no other reason than to kill. We believed they had asked the United States troops to come down to Mexico to fight us.

===Chinese===
不久，迈尔斯将军（General Miles）受命统领西部各哨所，部队便不停地循迹追捕我们。带队的是劳顿上尉（Captain Lawton），他手底下有些好侦察兵。墨西哥36士兵也变得更加活跃，人数也更多了。我们几乎天天有小仗要打，于是最后决意分散成小股。我带着六个男人、四个女人，直奔新墨西哥温泉（Hot Springs）附近的山岭而去。我们经过许多牧牛场，却没有同牛仔起过纠纷。缺吃的时候我们就宰牛充饥，可常常为水吃大苦头。有一回，我们两天两夜没有水喝，马匹几乎渴死。我们在新墨西哥的群山里往来盘桓了一些时日，后来想着部队也许已经离开墨西哥，便折了回去。归途穿行老墨西哥（Old Mexico）时，我们逢着墨西哥人就杀，哪怕别无缘故，单为杀戮也要杀。我们认定，是他们请了美国部队南下墨西哥来打我们。

===Original===
South of Casa Grande, near a place called by the Indians Gosoda, there was a road leading out from the town. There was much freighting carried on by the Mexicans over this road. Where the road ran through a mountain pass we stayed in hiding, and whenever Mexican freighters passed we killed them, took what supplies we wanted, and destroyed the remainder. We were reckless of our lives, because we felt that every man’s hand was against us. If we returned to the reservation we would be put in prison and killed; if we stayed in Mexico they would continue to send soldiers to fight us; so we gave no quarter to anyone and asked no favors.

===Chinese===
在卡萨格兰德（Casa Grande）以南，靠近一处印第安人唤作戈索达（Gosoda）的地方，有一条从镇上通出去的路。墨西哥人在这条路上经营着繁忙的货运。在道路穿山口而过的地方，我们潜伏下来，每逢墨西哥运货的人经过，我们就杀了他们，取走所要的物资，把余下的尽数毁掉。我们把自己的性命置之度外，因为我们觉得，人人的手都在与我们作对。回到保留地，就要被投进监狱、遭杀害；留在墨西哥，他们又会不断派兵来打我们；所以我们对谁都不留情，也不向谁乞恩。

===Original===
After some time we left Gosoda and soon were reunited with our tribe in the Sierra de Antunez Mountains.

===Chinese===
过了些时日，我们离开戈索达，不久便在安图内斯山脉（Sierra de Antunez）同自己的部落会合了。

===Original===
Contrary to our expectations the United States soldiers had not left the mountains in Mexico, and were soon trailing us and skirmishing with us almost every day. Four or five times they surprised our camp. One time they surprised us about nine o’clock in the morning, and captured all our horses37 (nineteen in number) and secured our store of dried meats. We also lost three Indians in this encounter. About the middle of the afternoon of the same day we attacked them from the rear as they were passing through a prairie﻿—killed one soldier, but lost none ourselves. In this skirmish we recovered all our horses except three that belonged to me. The three horses that we did not recover were the best riding horses we had.

===Chinese===
出乎我们的意料，美国士兵并没有离开墨西哥的山区，很快又循迹追来，几乎天天同我们打小仗。他们有四五次偷袭了我们的营地。有一回，早晨九点钟光景，他们袭了我们，把我们全部的马匹37（共十九匹）掳了去，还夺走了我们贮存的干肉。这一遭我们还折了三个印第安人。同一天下午过了一半，趁他们正穿过一片草原，我们从背后掩杀过去——杀了一名士兵，自己一个没折。这场小仗里，我们把马匹全数夺了回来，只除了属于我的三匹。没能夺回的那三匹，是我们最善骑的好马。

===Original===
Soon after this we made a treaty with the Mexican troops. They told us that the United States troops were the real cause of these wars, and agreed not to fight any more with us provided we would return to the United States. This we agreed to do, and resumed our march, expecting to try to make a treaty with the United States soldiers and return to Arizona. There seemed to be no other course to pursue.

===Chinese===
这之后不久，我们同墨西哥部队立了约。他们对我说，这些战事真正的根由在美国部队；只要我们肯回美国去，他们便答应不再同我们交战。我们应允了，便重新上路，指望试着同美国士兵立约，回亚利桑那（Arizona）去。除此之外，似乎再没有别的路可走。

===Original===
Soon after this scouts from Captain Lawton’s troops told us that he wished to make a treaty with us; but I knew that General Miles was the chief of the American troops, and I decided to treat with him.

===Chinese===
此后不久，劳顿上尉部队里的侦察兵来告诉我们，说他想同我们立约；可我知道迈尔斯将军才是美国部队的主帅，便决意同他去谈。

===Original===
We continued to move our camp northward, and the American troops also moved northward,38 keeping at no great distance from us, but not attacking us.

===Chinese===
我们继续拔营北上，美国部队也向北移动，38 与我们相距不远，却不来攻打。

===Original===
I sent my brother Porico (White Horse) with Mr. George Wratton on to Fort Bowie to see General Miles, and to tell him that we wished to return to Arizona; but before these messengers returned I met two Indian scouts﻿—Kayitah, a Chokonen Apache, and Marteen, a Nedni Apache. They were serving as scouts for Captain Lawton’s troops. They told me that General Miles had come and had sent them to ask me to meet him. So I went to the camp of the United States troops to meet General Miles.

===Chinese===
我派我的兄弟波里科（Porico，意为「白马」）同乔治·拉顿（George Wratton）先生前往鲍伊堡（Fort Bowie）去见迈尔斯将军，告诉他我们想回亚利桑那去；可这几位信使还没回来，我先遇见了两个印第安侦察兵——乔科嫩（Chokonen）阿帕奇人凯伊塔（Kayitah），和内德尼（Nedni）阿帕奇人马丁（Marteen）。他们正在劳顿上尉的部队里当侦察兵。他们告诉我，迈尔斯将军已经来了，差他们来请我去见他。于是我前往美国部队的营地，去见迈尔斯将军。

===Original===
When I arrived at their camp I went directly to General Miles and told him how I had been wronged, and that I wanted to return to the United States with my people, as we wished to see our families, who had been captured39 and taken away from us.

===Chinese===
到了他们的营地，我径直去见迈尔斯将军，向他诉说我受了怎样的冤屈，说我愿意带我的人回美国去，因为我们想见自己的家人，他们已被掳走39、被带离了我们身边。

===Original===
General Miles said to me: “The President of the United States has sent me to speak to you. He has heard of your trouble with the white men, and says that if you will agree to a few words of treaty we need have no more trouble. Geronimo, if you will agree to a few words of treaty all will be satisfactorily arranged.”

===Chinese===
迈尔斯将军对我说：「合众国总统差我来对你说话。他听闻了你同白人之间的纷争，说只要你应下寥寥数语的条约，便不必再起纷扰。杰罗尼莫，只要你应下寥寥数语的条约，一切都会得到妥善的安排。」

===Original===
So General Miles told me how we could be brothers to each other. We raised our hands to heaven and said that the treaty was not to be broken. We took an oath not to do any wrong to each other or to scheme against each other.

===Chinese===
就这样，迈尔斯将军对我讲了我们两人可以怎样结为兄弟。我们举手向天，言明这条约永不毁弃。我们起誓，彼此不做亏负对方的事，也不暗算对方。

===Original===
Then he talked with me for a long time and told me what he would do for me in the future if I would agree to the treaty. I did not greatly believe General Miles, but because the President of the United States had sent me word I agreed to make the treaty, and to keep it. Then I asked General Miles what the treaty would be. General Miles said to me:40 “I will take you under Government protection; I will build you a house; I will fence you much land; I will give you cattle, horses, mules, and farming implements. You will be furnished with men to work the farm, for you yourself will not have to work. In the fall I will send you blankets and clothing so that you will not suffer from cold in the winter time.

===Chinese===
随后他同我谈了许久，对我说，倘若我应下这条约，日后他会怎样待我。我并不十分相信迈尔斯将军，可合众国总统既给我捎了话来，我便应承立约，并守约不渝。于是我问迈尔斯将军，条约将是怎样的。迈尔斯将军对我说：40「我要把你们置于政府的保护之下；我要给你们盖一所房子；我要给你们圈出大片土地；我要给你们牛、马、骡子和农具。还会拨给人手替你们种地，因为你自己将不必劳作。入秋我会给你们送去毯子和衣裳，好叫你们冬天不受寒冻。

===Original===
“There is plenty of timber, water, and grass in the land to which I will send you. You will live with your tribe and with your family. If you agree to this treaty you shall see your family within five days.”

===Chinese===
「我要送你们去的那地方，木材、水和草都很充足。你们将同自己的部落、自己的家人住在一起。你若应下这条约，五天之内就能见到你的家人。」

===Original===
I said to General Miles: “All the officers that have been in charge of the Indians have talked that way, and it sounds like a story to me; I hardly believe you.”

===Chinese===
我对迈尔斯将军说：「凡是管治过印第安人的军官，个个都是这样说的，在我听来就像一段故事；我很难相信你。」

===Original===
He said: “This time it is the truth.”

===Chinese===
他说：「这一回是实话。」

===Original===
I said: “General Miles, I do not know the laws of the white man, nor of this new country where you are to send me, and I might break their laws.”

===Chinese===
我说：「迈尔斯将军，我不懂白人的法律，也不懂你要送我去的那片新地方的法律，我说不定会触犯他们的法律。」

===Original===
He said: “While I live you will not be arrested.”

===Chinese===
他说：「只要我活在世上一日，就不会有人逮捕你。」

===Original===
Then I agreed to make the treaty. (Since I have been a prisoner of war I have been arrested and placed in the guardhouse twice for drinking whisky.)

===Chinese===
于是我应承立约。（自从做了战俘，我已两次因喝威士忌被逮捕，关进禁闭室。）

===Original===
We stood between his troopers and my warriors. We placed a large stone on the blanket before us. Our treaty was made by this stone, and it was to last until the stone should crumble to dust; so we made the treaty, and bound each other with an oath.

===Chinese===
我们站在他的骑兵和我的武士之间。我们在面前的毯子上安放了一块大石头。我们的条约就凭着这块石头订立，要延续到石头碎为尘土的那一日；就这样，我们立了约，相互起誓为凭。

===Original===
I do not believe that I have ever violated that treaty; but General Miles41 never fulfilled his promises.

===Chinese===
我不认为自己曾违犯过那条约；可迈尔斯将军41却从未履行他的诺言。

===Original===
When we had made the treaty General Miles said to me: “My brother, you have in your mind how you are going to kill men, and other thoughts of war; I want you to put that out of your mind, and change your thoughts to peace.”

===Chinese===
立约之后，迈尔斯将军对我说：「我的兄弟，你心里还存着怎样去杀人的念头，还有别的战争的念头；我要你把这些从心里撇开，把心思转到和平上。」

===Original===
Then I agreed and gave up my arms. I said: “I will quit the warpath and live at peace hereafter.”

===Chinese===
于是我应承了，交出了武器。我说：「我从此不再出征，往后安享太平。」

===Original===
Then General Miles swept a spot of ground clear with his hand, and said: “Your past deeds shall be wiped out like this and you will start a new life.”

===Chinese===
随后，迈尔斯将军用手把一小块地面扫净了，说：「你过往的行事，就要这样被抹去，你将开始新的生活。」

## XVIII Surrender of Geronimo / 十八 杰罗尼莫的投降

===Original===
On February 11, 1887, the Senate passed the following resolution:

> **Resolved**, That the Secretary of War be directed to communicate to the Senate all dispatches of General Miles referring to the surrender of Geronimo, and all instructions given to and correspondence with General Miles in reference to the same.

===Chinese===
1887 年 2 月 11 日，参议院通过下列决议：

> **决议**：着令陆军部长向参议院呈交迈尔斯将军（General Miles）有关杰罗尼莫（Geronimo）投降事宜的全部公电，以及就此事项发给迈尔斯将军的一切训令及与其往来的一切函件。

===Original===
These papers are published in the Senate Executive Documents, Second Session, 49th Congress, 1886﻿–﻿7, Volume II, Nos. 111 to 125. For an exhaustive account of the conditions of Geronimo’s surrender the reader is referred to that document, but this chapter is given to show briefly the terms of surrender, and corroborate, at least in part, the statements made by Geronimo.
Upon assuming command of the Department of Arizona, General Nelson A. Miles was directed by the War Department to use most vigorous operations for the destruction or capture of the hostile Apaches.
The following extracts are from instructions issued April 20th, 1886, for the information and guidance of troops serving in the southern portion of Arizona and New Mexico.

===Chinese===
这些文件刊于《参议院行政文件》（Senate Executive Documents），第四十九届国会第二次会议，1886–1887 年，第二卷，第 111 至 125 号。欲求杰罗尼莫投降情形的详尽记载，请读者参阅那份文件；本章的设置，只为简略地展示投降的各项条件，并对杰罗尼莫所作的陈述至少给出一部分的印证。
纳尔逊·A·迈尔斯将军（General Nelson A. Miles）就任亚利桑那军区（Department of Arizona）司令之际，陆军部即命令他以最强力的军事行动，歼灭或俘获敌对的阿帕奇人。
以下几则摘自 1886 年 4 月 20 日颁发的训令，供驻亚利桑那和新墨西哥（New Mexico）南部地区的部队知悉并遵行。

===Original===
> The chief object of the troops will be to capture or destroy any band of hostile Apache Indians found in this section of country, and to this end the most vigorous and persistent efforts will be required of all officers and soldiers until the object is accomplished.

> A sufficient number of reliable Indians will be used as auxiliaries to discover any signs of hostile Indians, and as trailers.

> To avoid any advantage the Indians may have by a relay of horses, where a troop or squadron commander is near the hostile Indians he will be justified in dismounting one-half of his command and selecting the lightest and best riders to make pursuit by the most vigorous forced marches until the strength of all the animals of his command shall have been exhausted.

===Chinese===
> 部队的首要目标，是俘获或歼灭在本地区发现的任何一股敌对的阿帕奇印第安人；为此，全体军官和士兵务必尽最大努力，坚韧不辍，直至目标达成。

> 将使用足够数目的可靠印第安人充当辅助，用以发现敌对印第安人的任何踪迹，并担任追踪。

> 为防印第安人凭轮换的乘马占得便宜，凡骑兵连或骑兵中队指挥官逼近敌对印第安人时，即可令所部半数下马，挑选体重最轻、骑术最佳的士兵，以最强力的强行军实施追击，直至所部全部牲口的气力耗尽为止。

===Original===
The following telegrams show the efforts of the United States troops and the cooperation of Mexican troops under Governor Torres:

===Chinese===
下列电报显示了美国部队的种种努力，以及托雷斯州长（Governor Torres）麾下墨西哥部队的协同：

===Original===
> The following telegram just received from General Miles:
> In absence of division commander.
> **C. McKeever**, 

 Assistant Adjutant General.

===Chinese===
> 现刚收到迈尔斯将军如下电报：
> 师长缺席，代行。
> **C·麦基弗**（C. McKeever），

 副官长助理。

===Original===
> Following received from General Miles, dated 18th:
> **O. O. Howard**, 

 Major General.

===Chinese===
> 继收到迈尔斯将军 18 日电报如下：
> **O·O·霍华德**（O. O. Howard），

 少将。

===Original===
General O. O. Howard telegraphed from Presidio, San Francisco, California, September 24, 1886, as follows:

===Chinese===
1886 年 9 月 24 日，O·O·霍华德将军自加利福尼亚（California）旧金山（San Francisco）的普雷西迪奥（Presidio）拍发电报如下：

===Original===
> … The 6th of September General Miles reports the hostile Apaches made overtures of surrender, through Lieutenant Gatewood, to Captain Lawton. They desired certain terms and sent two messengers to me (Miles). They were informed that they must surrender as prisoners of war to troops in the field. They promised to surrender to me in person, and for eleven days Captain Lawton’s command moved north, Geronimo and Naiche moving parallel and frequently camping near it.﻿ ﻿… At Skeleton Canyon they halted, saying that they desired to see me (Miles) before surrendering.

===Chinese===
> ……9 月 6 日，迈尔斯将军报告说，敌对的阿帕奇人经由盖特伍德中尉（Lieutenant Gatewood），向劳顿上尉（Captain Lawton）提出投降之请。他们要求若干条件，并派了两名信使来见我（迈尔斯）。他们被告知，必须向战地部队投降，作为战俘。他们答应当面向我投降；此后十一天，劳顿上尉的部队向北转移，杰罗尼莫和纳切（Naiche）平行行进，屡屡在其近旁扎营。……到了骷髅峡谷（Skeleton Canyon），他们停了下来，说要先见我（迈尔斯），然后才肯投降。

===Original===
After Miles’s arrival he reports as follows:

===Chinese===
迈尔斯到达之后，报告如下：

===Original===
> Geronimo came from his mountain camp amid the rocks and said he was willing to surrender. He was told that they could surrender as prisoners of war; that it was not the way of officers of the Army to kill their enemies who laid down their arms.
> … Naiche was wild and suspicious and evidently feared treachery. He knew that the once noted leader, Mangus-Colorado, had, years ago, been foully murdered after he had surrendered, and the last hereditary chief of the hostile Apaches hesitated to place himself in the hands of the palefaces.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
> 杰罗尼莫从乱石间的山地营地里出来，说他愿意投降。他被告知，他们可以作为战俘投降；还说，杀死放下武器的敌人，不是陆军军官的做法。
> ……纳切桀骜而多疑，显然怕其中有诈。他知道当年那位一度威名显赫的首领曼加斯-科罗拉多（Mangus-Colorado），多年前投降之后惨遭卑鄙的杀害；这位敌对阿帕奇人的末代世袭酋长，便迟迟不肯把自己交到白人手里。……

===Original===
Continuing his report, General Howard says:

===Chinese===
霍华德将军在报告中继续写道：

===Original===
> … I believed at first from official reports that the surrender was unconditional, except that the troops themselves would not kill the hostiles. Now, from General Miles’s dispatches and from his annual report, forwarded on the 21st instant by mail, the conditions are plain: First, that the lives of all the Indians should be spared. Second, that they should be sent to Fort Marion, Florida, where their tribe, including their families, had already been ordered.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
> ……起初我根据官方报告，以为这次投降是无条件的，只有一项例外：部队自身不会杀害这些敌对者。如今，从迈尔斯将军的各件公电、从他本月 21 日邮寄的年度报告看来，条件是清楚的：第一，所有印第安人的性命一概保全。第二，他们应被送往佛罗里达（Florida）的马里恩堡（Fort Marion），他们的部落——包括他们的家眷——先前已奉命迁往那里。……

===Original===
D. S. Stanley, Brigadier General, telegraphs from San Antonio, Texas, October 22, 1886, as follows:

===Chinese===
1886 年 10 月 22 日，D·S·斯坦利准将（D. S. Stanley）自得克萨斯（Texas）圣安东尼奥（San Antonio）拍发电报如下：

===Original===
> … Geronimo and Naiche requested an interview with me when they first ascertained that they were to leave here, and in talking to them, I told them the exact disposition that was to be made of them. They regarded the separation of themselves from their families as a violation of the terms of their treaty of surrender, by which they had been guaranteed, in the most positive manner conceivable to their minds, that they should be united with their families at Fort Marion.
> There were present at the talk they had with me Major J. P. Wright, surgeon, United States Army; Captain J. G. Ballance, acting Judge-advocate, United States Army; George Wratton,42 the interpreter; Naiche, and Geronimo.
> The Indians were separated from their families at this place; the women, children, and the two scouts were placed in a separate car before they left.
> In an interview with me they stated the following incident, which they regard as an essential part of their treaty of surrender, and which took place at Skeleton Canyon before they had, as a band, made up their minds to surrender, and before any of them, except perhaps Geronimo, had given up their arms, and when they were still fully able to escape and defend themselves.
> General Miles said to them: “You go with me to Fort Bowie and at a certain time you will go to see your relatives in Florida.” After they went to Fort Bowie he reassured them that they would see their relatives in Florida in four and a half or five days.
> While at Skeleton Canyon General Miles said to them: “I have come to have a talk with you.” The conversation was interpreted from English into Spanish and from Spanish into Apache and vice versa. The interpreting from English into Spanish was done by a man by the name of Nelson. The interpreting from Spanish into Apache was done by José Maria Yaskes. José Maria Montoya was also present, but he did not do any of the interpreting.
> Dr. Wood, United States Army, and Lieutenant Clay, Tenth Infantry, were present.
> General Miles drew a line on the ground and said, “This represents the ocean,” and, putting a small rock beside the line, he said, “This represents the place where Chihuahua is with his band.” He then picked up another stone and placed it a short distance from the first, and said, “This represents you, Geronimo.” He then picked up a third stone and placed it a little distance from the others, and said, “This represents the Indians at Camp Apache. The President wants to take you and put you with Chihuahua.” He then picked up the stone which represented Geronimo and his band and put it beside the one which represented Chihuahua at Fort Marion. After doing this he picked up the stone which represented the Indians at Camp Apache and placed it beside the other two stones which represented Geronimo and Chihuahua at Fort Marion, and said, “That is what the President wants to do, get all of you together.”
> After their arrival at Fort Bowie General Miles said to them, “From now on we want to begin a new life,” and holding up one of his hands with the palm open and horizontal he marked lines across it with the finger of the other hand and said, pointing to his open palm, “This represents the past; it is all covered with hollows and ridges,” then, rubbing his other palm over it, he said, “That represents the wiping out of the past, which will be considered smooth and forgotten.”
> The interpreter, Wratton, says that he was present and heard this conversation. The Indians say that Captain Thompson, Fourth Cavalry, was also present.
> Naiche said that Captain Thompson, who was the acting assistant adjutant general, Department of Arizona, told him at his house in Fort Bowie, “Don’t be afraid; no harm shall come to you. You will go to your friends all right.” He also told them “that Fort Marion is not a very large place, and is not probably large enough for all, and that probably in six months or so you will be put in a larger place, where you can do better.” He told them the same thing when they took their departure in the cars from Fort Bowie.
> The idea that they had of the treaty of surrender given in this letter is forwarded at their desire, and, while not desiring to comment on the matter, I feel compelled to say that my knowledge of the Indian character, and the experience I have had with Indians of all kinds, and the corroborating circumstances and facts that have been brought to my notice in this particular case, convince me that the foregoing statement of Naiche and Geronimo is substantially correct.

===Chinese===
> ……杰罗尼莫和纳切最初得知自己将要被遣离此地时，曾请求同我会面；在同他们谈话时，我把将要如何发落他们的实情原原本本地告诉了他们。他们认为，把他们同家人分开，是违反投降条约条款的；依照那条约，曾以他们心目中所能想见的最确凿的方式向他们保证：他们将在马里恩堡同家人团聚。
> 出席他们同我那次谈话的，有美国陆军军医 J·P·赖特少校（Major J. P. Wright）、美国陆军代理军法官 J·G·巴兰斯上尉（Captain J. G. Ballance）、译员乔治·拉顿（George Wratton）42、纳切和杰罗尼莫。
> 印第安人就是在此地同家人分开的；动身之前，妇孺和那两名侦察兵被安置进了单独的一节车厢。
> 在同我的一次会谈中，他们陈述了下面这桩事。他们把它视为投降条约必不可少的组成部分。事情发生在骷髅峡谷，其时他们全队还没有拿定投降的主意，除了杰罗尼莫或许例外，还没有一个人交出武器，他们还完全有能力逃脱和自卫。
> 迈尔斯将军对他们说：「你们随我到鲍伊堡（Fort Bowie）去，到时候你们就会去佛罗里达见你们的亲人。」他们到了鲍伊堡之后，他又向他们保证，四天半或五天之内，他们就会在佛罗里达见到亲人。
> 在骷髅峡谷时，迈尔斯将军对他们说：「我是来同你们谈一谈的。」谈话由英语译成西班牙语，再由西班牙语译成阿帕奇语，并反向传译。英语译西班牙语，是由一个名叫纳尔逊（Nelson）的人担任的；西班牙语译阿帕奇语，是由何塞·玛丽亚·亚斯克斯（José Maria Yaskes）担任的。何塞·玛丽亚·蒙托亚（José Maria Montoya）也在场，但没有参与传译。
> 在场的还有美国陆军的伍德医生（Dr. Wood）和第十步兵团的克莱中尉（Lieutenant Clay）。
> 迈尔斯将军在地上画了一条线，说：「这代表大海。」又在线旁放上一块小石头，说：「这代表奇瓦瓦（Chihuahua）带着他的队伍所在的地方。」随后他拿起另一块石头，放在离第一块不远之处，说：「这代表你，杰罗尼莫。」接着他又拿起第三块石头，放在离其余两块稍远之处，说：「这代表阿帕奇营（Camp Apache）的印第安人。总统要把你们带走，同奇瓦瓦放到一处。」然后他拿起代表杰罗尼莫及其队伍的那块石头，放到那块代表身在马里恩堡的奇瓦瓦的石头旁边。做完这个，他又拿起代表阿帕奇营印第安人的那块石头，放在另外两块——代表身在马里恩堡的杰罗尼莫和奇瓦瓦——的石头旁边，说：「这就是总统想要做的事——把你们全都聚到一起。」
> 他们抵达鲍伊堡之后，迈尔斯将军对他们说：「从今往后，我们要开始一种新的生活。」他举起一只手，掌心摊开、放平，用另一只手的手指在掌上划出一道道线，指着摊开的掌心说：「这代表过去；它满是坑洼和沟坎。」然后他用另一只手掌在上面摩挲，说：「这代表把过去抹去，今后它将被看作平坦的、被忘却的。」
> 译员拉顿说，他当时在场，亲耳听到了这段谈话。印第安人说，第四骑兵队的汤普森上尉（Captain Thompson）当时也在场。
> 纳切说，汤普森上尉——时任亚利桑那军区代理副官长助理——在鲍伊堡他家里对他说：「不要怕；不会伤害你们的。你们会平平安安地到你们的朋友那里去。」他还告诉他们：「马里恩堡不是个很大的地方，恐怕未必容得下所有的人；大概过六个月左右，你们就会被安置到一个更大的地方，在那里你们可以过得更好。」他们乘火车离开鲍伊堡时，他又把同样的话说了一遍。
> 本信所述他们对该投降条约所持的理解，是应他们的请求转呈的。我本不愿就此事置评，却又不能不说：凭我对印第安人性格的了解，凭我同各式印第安人打交道的经历，再凭本案中引起我注意的种种佐证情节与事实，我确信纳切和杰罗尼莫前面的陈述大体属实。

===Original===
Extract from the annual report (1886) of the Division of the Pacific, commanded by Major General O. O. Howard, U.S. Army.

===Chinese===
以下摘自美国陆军 O·O·霍华德少将所统辖的太平洋师（Division of the Pacific）1886 年度报告。

===Original===
> **General**: I have the honor to submit the following report upon military operations and the condition of the Division of the Pacific for the information of the Lieutenant General, and to make some suggestions for his consideration:
> On the 17th of May, 1885, a party of about fifty of the Chiricahua prisoners, headed by Geronimo, Naiche, and other chiefs, escaped from the White Mountain Reserve, in Arizona, and entered upon a career of murder and robbery unparalleled in the history of Indian raids.
> Since then, and up to the time of my assuming command of this division, they had been pursued by troops with varying success.
> After the assassination of Captain Crawford, on January 11, by the Mexicans, the hostiles asked for a “talk,” and finally had a conference on March 25, 26, and 27, with General Crook, in the Canyon of Los Embudos, 25 miles south of San Bernardino, Mexico, on which latter date it was arranged that they should be conducted by Lieutenant Manus, with his battalion of scouts, to Fort Bowie, Ariz.
> The march commenced on the morning of March 28 and proceeded until the night of the 29th, when, becoming excited with fears of possible punishment, Geronimo and Naiche, with twenty men, fourteen women, and two boys, stampeded to the hills. Lieutenant Manus immediately pursued, but without success.
> Simultaneously with my taking command of the division Brigadier General Crook was relieved by Brigadier General Miles, who at once set out to complete the task commenced by his predecessor.
> Geronimo and his band were committing depredations, now in the United States and now in Mexico, and, being separated into small parties, easily eluded the troops, and carried on their work of murder and outrage.
> Early in May General Miles organized the hostile field of operations into districts, each with its command of troops, with specific instructions to guard the water holes, to cover the entire ground by scouting parties, and give the hostiles no rest.
> An effective command, under Captain Lawton, Fourth Cavalry, was organized for a long pursuit.
> On May 3 Captain Lebo, Tenth Cavalry, had a fight with Geronimo’s band twelve miles southwest of Santa Cruz, in Mexico, with a loss of one soldier killed and one wounded. After this fight the Indians retreated southward followed by three troops of cavalry.
> On May 12 a serious fight of Mexican troops with the hostiles near Planchos, Mexico, resulted in a partial defeat of the Mexicans.
> On May 15 Captain Hatfield’s command engaged Geronimo’s band in the Corrona Mountains, suffering a loss of two killed and three wounded, and the loss of several horses and mules, the Indians losing several killed.
> On May 16 Lieutenant Brown, Fourth Cavalry, struck the hostiles near Buena Vista, Mexico, capturing several horses, rifles, and a quantity of ammunition.
> The usual series of outrages, with fatiguing chase by troops, continued until June 21, when the Mexicans engaged the hostiles about forty miles southeast of Magdalena, Mexico, and after a stubborn fight repulsed them.﻿ ﻿…
> About the middle of August Geronimo and his band were so reduced and harassed by the tireless pursuit of the soldiers that they made offer of surrender to the Mexicans, but without coming to terms.
> Their locality thus being definitely known, disposition of the troops was rapidly made to act in conjunction with the Mexicans to intercept Geronimo and force his surrender.
> On August 25 Geronimo, when near Fronteraz, Mexico, recognizing that he was pretty well surrounded, and being out of ammunition and food, made overtures of capitulation, through Lieutenant Gatewood, Sixth Cavalry, to Captain Lawton. He desired certain terms, but was informed that a surrender as prisoner of war was all that would be accepted.
> The Indians then proceeded to the vicinity of Captain Lawton’s command, near Skeleton Canyon, and sent word that they wished to see General Miles.
> On September 3 General Miles arrived at Lawton’s camp, and on September 4 Naiche, the son of Cochise, and the hereditary chief of the Apaches, with Geronimo surrendered all the hostiles, with the understanding, it seems, that they should be sent out of Arizona.
> I am not informed of the exact nature of this surrender, at first deemed unconditional.﻿ ﻿…
> I am, sir, very respectfully, your obedient servant, 

===Chinese===
> **将军**：谨呈送下列关于太平洋师军事行动与现状的报告，供中将核阅，并提出若干建议，供其斟酌：
> 1885 年 5 月 17 日，约五十名奇里卡瓦（Chiricahua）战俘在杰罗尼莫、纳切和其他酋长的率领下，逃出亚利桑那的白山保留地（White Mountain Reserve），从此走上杀戮劫掠之路，其猖獗为印第安袭掠史所未见。
> 自那时起，直到我就任本师师长，部队对他们的追剿互有胜负。
> 1 月 11 日克劳福德上尉（Captain Crawford）遭墨西哥人杀害之后，这些敌对者提出「会谈」，终于 3 月 25、26、27 三日，在墨西哥圣贝纳迪诺（San Bernardino）以南 25 英里的洛斯恩布多斯峡谷（Canyon of Los Embudos）同克鲁克将军（General Crook）会商；就在最后一日，议定由马努斯中尉（Lieutenant Manus）率其侦察兵营，把他们护送到亚利桑那的鲍伊堡。
> 行军于 3 月 28 日晨开始，行至 29 日夜，杰罗尼莫和纳切因惧怕可能受到惩处而惊惶不安，带着二十名男人、十四名妇人和两个男孩，奔散入山。马努斯中尉立即追击，但未得手。
> 与我接掌本师的同时，克鲁克准将的职务由迈尔斯准将接替；迈尔斯随即着手完成其前任已经开始的任务。
> 杰罗尼莫及其队伍时而在美国、时而在墨西哥大肆劫掠；他们分成小股，轻易躲过部队，继续其杀戮暴行的勾当。
> 5 月初，迈尔斯将军将对敌作战地域划分为若干区，每区配置一支部队，并授予明确训令：把守水源，派侦察队布满整个地面，使敌对者不得片刻安宁。
> 另编成一支由第四骑兵队劳顿上尉统领的精干部队，专任长途追剿。
> 5 月 3 日，第十骑兵队莱博上尉（Captain Lebo）在墨西哥圣克鲁斯（Santa Cruz）西南 12 英里处同杰罗尼莫的队伍交战，折损一名士兵阵亡、一名受伤。此战之后，印第安人向南退却，三个骑兵连随后追击。
> 5 月 12 日，墨西哥部队在墨西哥普兰乔斯（Planchos）附近同敌对者激战一场，以墨西哥人的部分失利告终。
> 5 月 15 日，哈特菲尔德上尉（Captain Hatfield）所部在科罗纳山脉（Corrona Mountains）同杰罗尼莫的队伍交战，折损两人阵亡、三人受伤，还损失了几匹马骡；印第安人方面也折损数人。
> 5 月 16 日，第四骑兵队布朗中尉（Lieutenant Brown）在墨西哥布埃纳维斯塔（Buena Vista）附近袭击敌对者，缴获数匹马、数支步枪和一批弹药。
> 暴行照旧接连不断，部队疲于追击，一直持续到 6 月 21 日；是日，墨西哥人在墨西哥马格达莱纳（Magdalena）东南约 40 英里处同敌对者接战，经一场鏖战将其击退。……
> 8 月中旬，杰罗尼莫及其队伍被士兵们不知疲倦的追剿折磨得元气大丧、穷蹙不堪，于是向墨西哥人表示愿意投降，但未谈成条件。
> 他们的所在既已确知，部队便迅速作出部署，与墨西哥人协同行动，拦截杰罗尼莫，迫其投降。
> 8 月 25 日，杰罗尼莫在墨西哥弗龙特拉斯（Fronteraz）附近，眼见自己已陷入重围，弹药粮食俱绝，遂通过第六骑兵队的盖特伍德中尉，向劳顿上尉提出投降之请。他要求若干条件，但被告知：除作为战俘投降外，别无接受之途。
> 印第安人于是前往骷髅峡谷附近劳顿上尉部队的驻地，传话说他们愿意见迈尔斯将军。
> 9 月 3 日，迈尔斯将军抵达劳顿营地；9 月 4 日，柯奇斯（Cochise）之子、阿帕奇人的世袭酋长纳切，会同杰罗尼莫，率全体敌对者投降，看来双方谅解的是：他们应被送出亚利桑那。
> 此次投降的确切性质，我未获告知；它起初曾被认为是无条件的。……
> 谨向阁下致敬，恭顺地听候驱策，

===Original===
 **O. O. Howard**, 

 Major General, United States Army.

===Chinese===
 **O·O·霍华德**，

 美国陆军少将。

===Original===
> From 1882 to 1887 I lived in southern Arizona, and was employed by the Sansimone Cattle Company.
> In 1886 I was stationed in Skeleton Canyon, about 10 miles north of the boundary line between Arizona and Old Mexico, with J. D. Prewitt. It was our duty to ride the lines south of our range and keep the cattle of the Company from straying into Old Mexico.
> One afternoon, when returning from our ride, we discovered an Indian trail leading toward our camp. We rode hurriedly out of the hills into a broad valley so that we could better discover any attacking parties of Apaches and if assailed have at least a fighting chance for our lives. We knew the Apaches under Geronimo were on the warpath, but they were far down in Old Mexico. However, our knowledge of the Indians led us to expect anything at any time﻿—to always be ready for the worst.
> When we reached the valley we struck a cavalry trail also headed for our camp. This was perplexing, for neither the Indians nor the soldiers seemed to have been riding fast, and both trails led toward our camp in Skeleton Canyon. This canyon was a natural route from Old Mexico to Arizona, and almost all bands of Indians, as well as detachments of United States troops, passed and repassed through this valley when going to Old Mexico or returning therefrom, but never before had two hostile bands passed through here at the same time and traveling in the same direction, except when one fled and the other pursued. What this could mean was a mystery to us. Could it be that the troops had not seen the Indians? Were the redskins trying to head the troops off and attack them in their camp? Were the troops hunting for those Indians? Could this be Lawton’s command? Could that be Geronimo’s band? No, it was impossible. Then who were these troops and what Indians were those?
> Cautiously we rode to our camp, and nailed on the door of our cabin was this notice:
> Then we understood.

===Chinese===
> 从 1882 年到 1887 年，我住在亚利桑那南部，受雇于桑西蒙养牛公司（Sansimone Cattle Company）。
> 1886 年，我同 J·D·普鲁伊特（J. D. Prewitt）一起驻守骷髅峡谷，那地方在亚利桑那与老墨西哥（Old Mexico）交界线以北约 10 英里处。我们的职责是巡骑牧场以南的各条界线，防止公司的牛群走失到老墨西哥去。
> 一天下午，我们巡骑归来，发现一道印第安人的踪迹朝我们营地的方向而去。我们急忙驰出丘陵，进入一片开阔的谷地，这样倘有阿帕奇人来袭，我们能及早察觉，纵然受到攻击，也好歹有一条舍命一搏的活路。我们知道杰罗尼莫手下的阿帕奇人正在征途上，只是他们远在老墨西哥腹地。然而，凭我们对印第安人的了解，任何时候都可能出任何事情——须得时时作最坏的打算。
> 到了谷地，我们又踩上一道骑兵的踪迹，也是奔我们营地去的。这就叫人犯难了：印第安人和士兵们看去都不像在疾驰，两道踪迹却都通向我们在骷髅峡谷的营地。这道峡谷是老墨西哥往来亚利桑那的天然通道，几乎所有的印第安人队伍，连同美军的分队，去老墨西哥或从那里回来，都打这片谷地经过；可是两股敌对的人马同时、同向打这里过，以前还从未有过——除非是一逃一追。这究竟是怎么回事，我们捉摸不透。莫非部队没有看见印第安人？是那些红种人想抢到部队前头、趁他们扎营时下手？还是部队正在搜捕那些印第安人？这会不会是劳顿的部队？那会不会是杰罗尼莫的队伍？不，不可能。那么这支部队是谁，那些印第安人又是什么人？
> 我们小心翼翼地骑回营地，只见小屋门上钉着这样一张告示：
> 于是我们明白了。

===Original===
> A short distance above our cabin we found the camp of the troops and we had just finished talking with Captain Lawton, who advised us to remain in his camp rather than risk staying alone in our cabin, when up rode the chief, Geronimo. He was mounted on a blaze-faced, white-stockinged dun horse.
> He came directly to Captain Lawton and through an interpreter asked who we were and what we wanted.
> As soon as the explanation was given he nodded his approval and rode away.
> Prewitt and I rode away with him. We were well armed and well mounted and Geronimo was well mounted, but so far as we could see unarmed. I tried to talk with the chief (in English), but could not make him understand. Prewitt wanted to shoot43 him and said he could easily kill him the first shot, but I objected and succeeded in restraining him. While we were arguing the chief rode silently between us, evidently feeling perfectly secure. All this time we had been riding in the direction of our horses that were grazing in the valley about a mile distant from our corral. When we came to a place about a half mile from Lawton’s camp, where a spur of the mountain ran far out into the valley, Geronimo turned aside, saluted, said in fairly good Spanish, “*Adios, Señors*,” and began to ascend a mountain path. Later we learned that he was going directly toward his camp far up among the rocks. We rode on, drove our horses back to the corral and remained in our cabin all night, but were not molested by the Indians.

===Chinese===
> 在小屋上方不远处，我们找到了部队的营地。我们刚同劳顿上尉谈完话——他劝我们留在他的营地里，别冒险独自守着小屋——那位酋长杰罗尼莫就骑马过来了。他骑的是一匹额有白斑、四蹄白如着袜的黄褐马。
> 他径直来到劳顿上尉跟前，通过译员问我们是何人，来意为何。
> 解释一完，他点头认可，拨马而去。
> 普鲁伊特和我同他一道骑行。我们带着精良的武器，骑着壮健的马；杰罗尼莫的马也不错，但就我们所能看到的，他身上没有武器。我试着（用英语）同这位酋长攀谈，却没法让他听懂。普鲁伊特想开枪43打他，还说头一枪就能轻易要了他的命，我表示反对，好说歹说把他劝住了。我们争执的时候，酋长默然骑行在我们两人之间，显然心里安稳得很。这一路我们朝着我们的马匹走去——它们正在谷中吃草，离栏圈约一英里。走到离劳顿营地约半英里、山嘴远远探进谷中的地方，杰罗尼莫拨转方向，行了个礼，用相当不错的西班牙语说了声「*Adios, Señors*」（两位，再会），便踏上一条山径向上而去。后来我们才知道，他是径直回他远在乱石高处的营地去了。我们继续前行，把马赶回栏圈，在小屋里守了一夜，印第安人没有来搅扰。

===Original===
> The next day we killed three beeves for the Indians, and they were paid for by Captain Lawton. On the second day two mounted Mexican scouts came to Lawton’s camp. As soon as these Mexicans came in sight the Indians seized their arms and vanished, as it were, among the rocks.
> Captain Lawton wrote an account of conditions and delivered it to the Mexicans, who withdrew. After they had gone and their mission had been explained to Geronimo the Indians again returned to their camp and laid down their arms.
> On the next day word reached camp that General Miles was approaching and the Indians again armed and disappeared among the rocks. (Many of the Apache squaws had field glasses44 and were stationed every day on prominent mountain peaks to keep a lookout. No one could approach their camp or Lawton’s camp without being discovered by these spies.)
> Soon after General Miles joined Lawton’s command Geronimo rode into camp unarmed, and dismounting approached General Miles, shook hands with him, and then stood proudly before the officers waiting for General Miles to begin conversation with him.
> The interpreter said to Geronimo, “General Miles is your friend.” Geronimo said, “I never saw him, but I have been in need of friends. Why has he not been with me?” When this answer was interpreted everybody laughed. After this there was no more formality and without delay the discussion of the treaty was begun. All I remember distinctly of the treaty is that Geronimo and his band were not to be killed, but they were to be taken to their families.

===Chinese===
> 第二天，我们宰了三头牛给印第安人，牛钱由劳顿上尉付给。又过一天，两名骑马的墨西哥侦察兵来到劳顿的营地。这些墨西哥人刚一进入视线，印第安人便抓起武器，仿佛隐入乱石之中，霎时不见了踪影。
> 劳顿上尉写了一份情况记述交给墨西哥人，他们便撤走了。他们走后，有人把他们的使命向杰罗尼莫解说明白，印第安人这才回到营地，放下了武器。
> 又过了一天，消息传到营地，说迈尔斯将军正在前来，印第安人再度拿起武器，消失在乱石之间。（许多阿帕奇妇人备有双筒望远镜44，每天被派在突出的山峰上站哨瞭望。无论是他们的营地还是劳顿的营地，谁也无法靠近而不被这些暗探发觉。）
> 迈尔斯将军同劳顿的部队会合后不久，杰罗尼莫不带武器骑马进了营地，下马走到迈尔斯将军跟前，同他握了手，然后昂然立在众军官面前，等迈尔斯将军先开口同他谈话。
> 译员对杰罗尼莫说：「迈尔斯将军是你的朋友。」杰罗尼莫说：「我从来没有见过他，可我一直缺朋友。他为什么不早些到我身边来呢？」这句话传译出来，众人都笑了。此后再无客套，条约的商谈随即开始。条约的内容我如今记得真切的只有一条：杰罗尼莫和他的部众不会被处死，而要被带去同他们的家人团聚。

===Original===
> I remember this more distinctly, because the Indians were so much pleased with this particular one of the terms of the treaty.
> Geronimo, Naiche, and a few others went on ahead with General Miles, but the main band of Indians left under the escort of Lawton’s troops.
> The night before they left, a young squaw, daughter-in-law of Geronimo, gave birth to a child. The next morning the husband, Geronimo’s son, carried the child, but the mother mounted her pony unaided and rode away unassisted﻿—a prisoner of war under military escort.
> On the afternoon of the day of the treaty Captain Lawton built a monument (about ten feet across and six feet high) of rough stones at the spot where the treaty was made. The next year some cowboys on a roundup camped at the place, and tore down the monument to see what was in it. All they found was a bottle containing a piece of paper upon which was written the names of the officers who were with Lawton.
> After the Indians left we found one hundred and fifty dollars and twenty-five cents ($150.25) in Mexican money hidden in a rat’s nest45 near where the Indians had camped.

===Chinese===
> 这一条我记得格外真切，因为条约各款之中，印第安人最中意的正是这一条。
> 杰罗尼莫、纳切和另外几个人随迈尔斯将军先行，印第安人的大队人马则在劳顿部队的护送下动身。
> 动身前的那个夜里，杰罗尼莫的一个年轻儿媳产下一个孩子。第二天早晨，做丈夫的——杰罗尼莫的儿子——抱着孩子，那位母亲却不用人搀扶，自己跨上小马，自己骑马上路——一个由军队押送的战俘。
> 缔约当天下午，劳顿上尉在立约之处用乱石垒起一座石墩（底径约十英尺，高约六英尺）。第二年，一些围赶牛群的牛仔在那地方扎营，把石墩拆倒，想看看里面有什么。他们找到的只有一只瓶子，瓶里装着一张纸，纸上写着随劳顿在场的军官们的姓名。
> 印第安人走后，我们在他们扎过营的地方附近，发现一个鼠巢45里藏着墨西哥钱，共计一百五十美元二十五美分（$150.25）。

===Original===
> About ten o’clock on the morning after the Apaches and soldiers had gone away twenty Pimos Indians, accompanied by one white man, surrounded our camp and demanded to know of Geronimo’s whereabouts. We told them of the treaty and they followed the trail on toward Fort Bowie.
> That afternoon, thinking all danger from Apaches past, my partner, Prewitt, went to ride the lines and I was left in camp alone. I was pumping water (by horse-power) at the well, when I saw three Indians rounding up our horses about half a mile away. They saw me but did not disturb me, nor did I interfere with them, but as soon as they had driven that bunch of horses northward over the hill out of sight I rode quickly off in another direction and drove another bunch of horses into the corral. The rest of the afternoon I stayed in camp, but saw no more Indians.
> The next day we rode over the hill in the direction these Indians had gone and found that they had camped not three miles away. There were evidently several in the party and they had kept scouts concealed near the top of the hill to watch me, and to shoot me from ambush had I followed them. This we knew because we saw behind some rocks at the crest of the hill in the loose soil the imprints left by the bodies of three warriors where they had been lying down in concealment.
> At their camp we found the head and hoofs of my favorite horse, “Digger,” a fine little sorrel pony, and knew that he had served them for dinner. We followed their trail far into Old Mexico, but did not overtake them. We had been accustomed to say “it was Geronimo’s band,” whenever any depredation was committed, but this time we were not so positive.

===Chinese===
> 阿帕奇人和士兵们离开后的第二天上午十点钟左右，二十个皮莫（Pimos）印第安人由一个白人陪着，包围了我们的营地，追问杰罗尼莫的下落。我们把缔约的事告诉他们，他们便循着踪迹朝鲍伊堡方向追去。
> 那天下午，我心想阿帕奇人的危险已经过去，伙伴普鲁伊特巡边去了，营地里只剩我一人。我正在井边（用马力）抽水，看见约半英里外有三个印第安人在围赶我们的马。他们看见了我，没有来搅扰；我也没有去干涉他们。但等他们把那一群马朝北赶过山冈、赶得看不见了，我立刻朝另一个方向疾驰而去，把另一群马赶进栏圈。那个下午余下的时间我守在营地，再没有见到印第安人。
> 第二天，我们骑马越过山冈，朝那些印第安人去的方向找去，发现他们扎营的地方还不到三英里远。那一伙人显然有好几个，还在山顶附近藏着暗哨监视我，倘若我跟上去，就要从埋伏中朝我开枪。这一点我们知道，是因为在山顶的乱石后面松土上，看见三个武士伏身潜藏时留下的身体印痕。
> 在他们的营地里，我们找到了我心爱的马「迪格」（Digger）——一匹漂亮的红栗色小马——的头和蹄，知道它给他们当了一顿饭。我们顺着踪迹一路追进老墨西哥腹地，却没有追上。每逢有劫掠的事发生，我们一向习惯说「是杰罗尼莫那伙人干的」，可这一回，我们就不那么肯定了。

===Original===
We do not wish to express our own opinion, but to ask the reader whether, after having had the testimony of Apaches, soldiers, and civilians, who knew the conditions of surrender, and, after having examined carefully the testimony offered, it would be possible to conclude that Geronimo made an unconditional surrender?
Before passing from this subject it would be well also to consider whether our Government has treated these prisoners in strict accordance with the terms of the treaty made in Skeleton Canyon.

===Chinese===
我们不愿申说自己的看法，只想请问读者：既已听取了了解投降情形的阿帕奇人、士兵和平民的证词，又已仔细审查了这些证词，还能得出杰罗尼莫是无条件投降的结论吗？
在离开这个话题之前，不妨再想一想：我们的政府是否严格按照骷髅峡谷缔结的条约条款对待了这些战俘。

## XIX A Prisoner of War / 十九 战俘

===Original===
When I had given up to the Government they put me on the Southern Pacific Railroad and took me to San Antonio, Texas, and held me to be tried by their laws.

===Chinese===
我向政府投降之后，他们把我押上南太平洋铁路（Southern Pacific Railroad）的火车，解到得克萨斯（Texas）的圣安东尼奥（San Antonio），关押起来，要按他们的法律审判我。

===Original===
In forty days they took me from there to Fort Pickens (Pensacola), Florida. Here they put me to sawing up large logs. There were several other Apache warriors with me, and all of us had to work every day. For nearly two years we were kept at hard labor in this place and we did not see our families until May, 1887. This treatment was in direct violation of our treaty made at Skeleton Canyon.

===Chinese===
四十天后，他们把我从那里解往佛罗里达（Florida）的皮肯斯堡（Fort Pickens，彭萨科拉 Pensacola）。在那里，他们派我锯大圆木。还有几名阿帕奇（Apache）武士同我在一起，我们全都得天天做工。我们在这个地方服了近两年的苦役，直到 1887 年 5 月才见到家人。这样的待遇，直接违背了我们在骷髅峡谷（Skeleton Canyon）立下的条约。

===Original===
After this we were sent with our families to Vermont, Alabama, where we stayed five years and worked for the Government. We had no property, and I looked in vain for General Miles to send me to that land of which he had spoken; I longed in vain for the implements, house, and stock that General Miles had promised me.

===Chinese===
此后，我们连同家眷被送往亚拉巴马（Alabama）的弗蒙特（Vermont），在那里住了五年，为政府做工。我们一无所有；我指望迈尔斯将军（General Miles）把我送到他所说过的那片土地上去，指望落了空；我渴望他许给我的农具、房屋和牲畜，渴望也落了空。

===Original===
During this time one of my warriors, Fun, killed himself and his wife. Another one shot his wife and then shot himself. He fell dead, but the woman recovered and is still living.

===Chinese===
在这期间，我的一名武士芬（Fun）杀死了自己和妻子。另一名武士开枪打了妻子，又开枪打自己。他当场死了，那女人却活了下来，至今还活着。

===Original===
We were not healthy in this place, for the climate disagreed with us. So many of our people died that I consented to let one of my wives go to the Mescalero Agency in New Mexico to live. This separation is according to our custom equivalent to what the white people call “divorce,” and so she married again soon after she got to Mescalero. She also kept our two small children, which she had a right to do. The children, Lenna and Robbie, are still living at Mescalero, New Mexico. Lenna is married. I kept one wife, but she is dead now and I have only our daughter Eva with me. Since my separation from Lenna’s mother I have never had more than one wife at a time. Since the death of Eva’s mother I married another woman (December, 1905) but we could not live happily and separated. She went home to her people—that is an Apache divorce.

===Chinese===
在这个地方我们身子不健，因为气候与我们不合。族人死得太多，我便应允让我的一个妻子去新墨西哥（New Mexico）的梅斯卡莱罗事务署（Mescalero Agency）那边生活。按我们的习俗，这样的分离就相当于白人所说的「离婚」；所以她到梅斯卡莱罗不久便另嫁了。她把我们的两个年幼的孩子留在了自己身边，这是她的权利。这两个孩子，蕾娜（Lenna）和罗比（Robbie），如今仍住在新墨西哥的梅斯卡莱罗。蕾娜已经出嫁。我留下了一位妻子，可她如今也已去世，身边只剩我们的女儿伊娃（Eva）陪伴我。自从同蕾娜的母亲分开以来，我从未同时有过一个以上的妻子。伊娃的母亲去世后，我又娶了一个女人（1905 年 12 月），但我们过不到一处，便分开了。她回她族人那里去了——这就是阿帕奇式的离婚。

===Original===
Then,46 as now, Mr. George Wratton superintended the Indians. He has always had trouble with the Indians, because he has mistreated them. One day an Indian, while drunk, stabbed Mr. Wratton with a little knife. The officer in charge took the part of Mr. Wratton and the Indian was sent to prison.

===Chinese===
那时 46 也和现在一样，是乔治·拉顿（George Wratton）先生管辖印第安人。他同印第安人始终处不好，因为他虐待他们。有一天，一个印第安人喝醉了，用一把小刀捅了拉顿先生。负责的军官偏袒拉顿先生，那印第安人便被送进了监狱。

===Original===
When47 we first came to Fort Sill, Captain Scott was in charge, and he had houses built for us by the Government. We were also given, from the Government, cattle, hogs, turkeys and chickens. The Indians did not do much good with the hogs, because they did not understand how to care for them, and not many Indians even at the present time keep hogs. We did better with the turkeys and chickens, but with these we did not have as good luck as white men do. With the cattle we have done very well, indeed, and we like to raise them. We have a few horses also, and have had no bad luck with them.

===Chinese===
我们 47 初到西尔堡（Fort Sill）时，是斯科特上尉（Captain Scott）负责管理，他让政府为我们盖了房子。政府还给了我们牛、猪、火鸡和鸡。印第安人养猪没什么成效，因为他们不懂怎么照料猪，直到如今养猪的印第安人也不多。火鸡和鸡我们养得好些，可比起白人来，运气还是不如。养牛我们确实干得很好，也喜欢养牛。我们还有几匹马，养马也没出过什么岔子。

===Original===
In the matter of selling48 our stock and grain there has been much misunderstanding. The Indians understood that the cattle were to be sold and the money given to them, but instead part of the money is given to the Indians and part of it is placed in what the officers call the “Apache Fund.” We have had five different officers in charge of the Indians here and they have all ruled very much alike—not consulting the Apaches or even explaining to them. It may be that the Government ordered the officers in charge to put this cattle money into an Apache fund, for once I complained and told Lieutenant Purington49 that I intended to report to the Government that he had taken some of my part of the cattle money and put it into the Apache Fund, he said he did not care if I did tell.

===Chinese===
在出售 48 我们牲畜和谷物的事情上，有过许多误会。印第安人原以为牛卖掉之后，钱会交到他们手里；可实际上，钱只有一部分发给印第安人，另一部分放进了军官们所谓的「阿帕奇基金」（Apache Fund）。先后有五位不同的军官主管过这里的印第安人，他们的做法都十分相似——不同阿帕奇人商量，甚至不向他们说明。也许这是政府命令主管军官把卖牛的钱放进阿帕奇基金的；因为有一次我抱怨，对普林顿中尉（Lieutenant Purington）49 说，我要向政府告发，他把卖牛钱里属于我的那份拿走了一些、放进了阿帕奇基金，他却说，我就是去告发，他也不在乎。

===Original===
Several years ago the issue of clothing ceased. This, too, may have been by the order of the Government, but the Apaches do not understand it.

===Chinese===
几年前，发放衣物的供应停止了。这也可能是政府的命令，但阿帕奇人不明白这是为什么。

===Original===
If there is an Apache Fund, it should some day be turned over to the Indians, or at least they should have an account of it, for it is their earnings.

===Chinese===
倘若真有阿帕奇基金，总有一天应当交还印第安人，至少也该给他们一份账目，因为这本来就是他们挣来的钱。

===Original===
When General Miles last visited Fort Sill I asked to be relieved from labor on account of my age. I also remembered what General Miles had promised me in the treaty and told him of it. He said I need not work any more except when I wished to, and since that time I have not been detailed to do any work. I have worked a great deal, however, since then, for, although I am old, I like to work50 and help my people as much as I am able.

===Chinese===
迈尔斯将军上次来西尔堡时，我请求他念我年老，免了我的劳役。我还记得迈尔斯将军在条约里许过我的话，也对他说了。他说，往后我不必再做工，除非我自己愿意；从那时起，再没有派过我什么活。不过从那以后我仍干了许多活，因为人虽老了，我喜欢干活 50，愿意尽力帮助我的族人。

## Part IV The Old and the New / 第四部分 旧与新

## XX Unwritten Laws of the Apaches / 二十 阿帕奇人的不成文法律

#### Trials / 审判

===Original===
When an Indian has been wronged by a member of his tribe he may, if he does not wish to settle the difficulty personally, make complaint to the Chieftain. If he is unable to meet the offending parties in a personal encounter, and disdains to make complaint, anyone may in his stead inform the chief of this conduct, and then it becomes necessary to have an investigation or trial. Both the accused and the accuser are entitled to witnesses, and their witnesses are not interrupted in any way by questions, but simply say what they wish to say in regard to the matter. The witnesses are not placed under oath, because it is not believed that they will give false testimony in a matter relating to their own people.

===Chinese===
一个印第安人若被本族族人伤害，倘若他不愿私下自行了结，可以向酋长控诉。倘若他无力与冒犯的一方当面较量，又不屑于去控诉，旁人也可以代他把这情形告知酋长，于是就必须进行一番调查或审判。被告和原告都有权传证人；证人不受任何盘问打断，只是就这件事说出自己想说的话。证人不必宣誓，因为人们相信，在关乎本族人的事情上，他们不会作伪证。

===Original===
The chief of the tribe presides during these trials, but if it is a serious offense he asks two or three leaders to sit with him. These simply determine whether or not the man is guilty. If he is not guilty the matter is ended, and the complaining party has forfeited his right to take personal vengeance, for if he wishes to take vengeance himself, he must object to the trial which would prevent it. If the accused is found guilty the injured party fixes the penalty, which is generally confirmed by the chief and his associates.

===Chinese===
审判时由部落酋长主持；若是严重的罪过，他便请两三位首领同他一起坐堂。他们只裁定这个人是否有罪。若他无罪，事情便算了结，控诉的一方也就丧失了亲自复仇的权利；因为他若想亲自动手复仇，就必须反对这场阻止他复仇的审判。若被告被判有罪，刑罚由受害的一方定夺，通常再经酋长和他的同僚确认。

#### Adoption of Children / 收养子女

===Original===
If any children are left orphans by the usage of war or otherwise, that is, if both parents are dead, the chief of the tribe may adopt them or give them away as he desires. In the case of outlawed Indians, they may, if they wish, take their children with them, but if they leave the children with the tribe, the chief decides what will be done with them, but no disgrace attaches to the children.

===Chinese===
若有孩子因战事或其他缘故成了孤儿——也就是说父母双亡——部落酋长可以自行收养他们，也可以随意把他们送给别人。至于被放逐出族的印第安人，他们若愿意，可以带走自己的孩子；但若把孩子留在部落里，便由酋长决定如何处置，不过孩子并不因此蒙受耻辱。

#### “Salt Lake” / 「盐湖」

===Original===
We obtained our salt from a little lake in the Gila Mountains. This is a very small lake of clear, shallow water, and in the center a small mound arises above the surface of the water. The water is too salty to drink, and the bottom of the lake is covered with a brown crust. When this crust is broken cakes of salt adhere to it. These cakes of salt may be washed clear in the water of this lake, but if washed in other water will dissolve.

===Chinese===
我们的盐取自希拉山（Gila Mountains）里的一个小湖。那是一个很小的湖，水清而浅，湖心有一座小土丘露出水面。湖水咸得不能喝，湖底覆着一层棕色的硬壳。把这层壳打破，盐块便粘在上面。这些盐块可以在这湖水里洗净，可若放到别的水里洗，就会溶化。

===Original===
When visiting this lake our people were not allowed to even kill game or attack an enemy. All creatures were free to go and come without molestation.

===Chinese===
前往这个湖的时候，我们的族人连猎杀野兽、攻打仇敌都不许。一切生灵都可以自由来去，不受惊扰。

#### Preparation of a Warrior / 武士的历练

===Original===
To be admitted as a warrior a youth must have gone with the warriors of his tribe four separate times on the warpath.

===Chinese===
一个少年要被接纳为武士，必须四次随本族的武士踏上征途，一次便是一回。

===Original===
On the first trip he will be given only very inferior food. With this he must be contented without murmuring. On none of the four trips is he allowed to select his food as the warriors do, but must eat such food as he is permitted to have.

===Chinese===
初次出征，给他的只有极粗劣的食物。他必须吃得心甘情愿，不得有半句怨言。四次出征，哪一次他都不能像武士们那样挑选食物，只能吃获准吃的那一份。

===Original===
On each of these expeditions he acts as servant, cares for the horses, cooks the food, and does whatever duties he should do without being told. He knows what things are to be done, and without waiting to be told is to do them. He is not allowed to speak to any warrior except in answer to questions or when told to speak.

===Chinese===
每一次出征，他都充作仆役，照管马匹，做饭煮食，凡是他分内的事，不等吩咐就去做。他知道有哪些事该做，不等吩咐便须动手。除了回答问话或奉命说话，他不得同任何武士交谈。

===Original===
During these four wars he is expected to learn the sacred names of everything used in war, for after the tribe enters upon the warpath no common names are used in referring to anything appertaining to war in any way. War is a solemn religious matter.

===Chinese===
在这四次征战里，他须学会战争中一切事物的圣名；因为部落一旦踏上征途，凡与战事沾边的东西，一概不再用寻常的名字称呼。战争是一件庄严神圣的事。

===Original===
If, after four expeditions, all the warriors are satisfied that the youth has been industrious, has not spoken out of order, has been discreet in all things, has shown courage in battle, has borne all hardships uncomplainingly, and has exhibited no color of cowardice, or weakness of any kind, he may by vote of the council be admitted as a warrior; but if any warrior objects to him upon any account he will be subjected to further tests, and if he meets these courageously, his name may again be proposed. When he has proven beyond question that he can bear hardships without complaint, and that he is a stranger to fear, he is admitted to the council of the warriors in the lowest rank. After this there is no formal test for promotions, but by common consent he assumes a station on the battlefield, and if that position is maintained with honor, he is allowed to keep it, and may be asked, or may volunteer, to take a higher station, but no warrior would presume to take a higher station unless he had assurance from the leaders of the tribe that his conduct in the first position was worthy of commendation.

===Chinese===
四次出征之后，倘若全体武士都满意：这少年勤勉肯干，不曾乱开口说话，凡事谨慎，作战勇敢，历尽艰苦而毫无怨言，没有露出半分怯懦或任何软弱的样子——经议事会表决，便可接纳他为武士；但若有任何武士因任何缘故反对他，他就得再受进一步的考验；若他勇敢地通过了这些考验，他的名字可以重新提出。等到他无可置疑地证明自己能任劳任怨、不知恐惧为何物，他便被接纳进武士的议事会，位列最低的一级。此后晋升不再有正式的考验，而是经众人默许，由他在战场上担任一个位置；倘若这个位置他守得体面，便准他继续担任，也可能被推举、或者自己请命，去担任更高的位置；但没有一个武士胆敢僭居高位，除非他得到部落首领们确实的许诺：他在第一个位置上的表现值得称许。

===Original===
From this point upward the only election by the council in formal assembly is the election of the chief.

===Chinese===
由此再往上，议事会正式集会所进行的唯一选举，便是选举酋长。

===Original===
Old men are not allowed to lead in battle, but their advice is always respected. Old age means loss of physical power and is fatal to active leadership.

===Chinese===
老人不得统兵作战，但他们的意见始终受人敬重。年老意味着体力的衰竭，对亲临前线的统率而言是致命的。

#### Dances / 舞蹈

===Original===
All dances are considered religious ceremonies and are presided over by a chief and medicine men. They are of a social or military nature, but never without some sacred characteristic.

===Chinese===
一切舞蹈都被视为宗教仪式，由一位酋长和巫医们主持。舞蹈或属社交，或属军事，但从不缺少几分神圣的性质。

===Original===
Every summer we would gather the fruit of the yucca, grind and pulverize it and mold it into cakes; then the tribe would be assembled to feast, to sing, and to give praises to Usen. Prayers of Thanksgiving were said by all. When the dance began the leaders bore these cakes and added words of praise occasionally to the usual tone sounds of the music.

===Chinese===
每年夏天，我们采来丝兰（yucca）的果实，碾碎磨细，做成饼；然后全族聚在一起宴饮、歌唱、赞颂天神乌森（Usen）。人人都念感恩的祈祷。舞蹈开始时，领舞的人们捧着这些饼，不时在乐曲寻常的声调之中加进几句颂赞的话。

===Original===
After a council of the warriors had deliberated, and had prepared for the warpath, the dance would be started. In this dance there is the usual singing led by the warriors and accompanied with the beating of the *esadadene*, but the dancing is more violent, and yells and war whoops sometimes almost drown the music. Only warriors participated in this dance.

===Chinese===
武士们的议事会商议停当、备好出征之后，舞蹈便开始了。这种舞照例由武士们领唱，伴着埃萨达德内鼓（*esadadene*）的敲击；但跳得更加狂烈，呐喊与战嚎有时几乎淹没了乐声。只有武士才参加这种舞。

===Original===
After a war party has returned, a modification of the war dance is held. The warriors who have brought scalps from the battles exhibit them to the tribe, and when the dance begins these scalps, elevated on poles or spears, are carried around the campfires while the dance is in progress. During this dance there is still some of the solemnity of the war dance. There are yells and war whoops, frequently accompanied by discharge of firearms, but there is always more levity than would be permitted at a war dance. After the scalp dance is over the scalps are thrown away. No Apache would keep them, for they are considered defiling.

===Chinese===
出征的队伍回来之后，要举行一种由战舞变化而来的舞。从战场上带回头皮的武士们把头皮展示给全族看；舞蹈开始后，这些头皮高挑在长杆或矛上，在舞蹈进行时绕着营火巡行。这种舞里仍带着几分战舞的庄严。有呐喊，有战嚎，还常常伴着鸣枪，但总比战舞上许可的要轻快许多。头皮舞结束之后，头皮就被扔掉。没有一个阿帕奇人会留着它们，因为那被认为是不洁之物。

===Original===
In the early part of September, 1905, I announced among the Apaches that my daughter, Eva, having attained womanhood, should now put away childish things and assume her station as a young lady. At a dance of the tribe she would make her début, and then, or thereafter, it would be proper for a warrior to seek her hand in marriage. Accordingly, invitations were issued to all Apaches, and many Comanches and Kiowas, to assemble for a grand dance on the green by the south bank of Medicine Creek, near the village of Naiche, former chief of the Chokonen Apaches, on the first night of full moon in September. The festivities were to continue for two days and nights. Nothing was omitted in the preparation that would contribute to the enjoyment of the guests or the perfection of the observance of the religious rite.

===Chinese===
1905 年 9 月初，我在阿帕奇（Apache）人中宣布：我的女儿伊娃（Eva）已届成年，从此该收起孩童的玩意，取得少女的身份了。她将在全族的一场舞会上初次露面；从那时起，或在那之后，武士向她求婚便是合乎礼俗的了。于是，请帖发给了所有阿帕奇人，还有许多科曼奇人（Comanche）和基奥瓦人（Kiowa），请他们在 9 月第一个满月之夜，聚到梅迪辛河（Medicine Creek）南岸的草地上，举行一场盛大的舞会；那地方在乔科嫩阿帕奇人（Chokonen Apaches）故酋纳切（Naiche）的村子附近。欢庆要持续两天两夜。凡能使宾客尽兴、能使宗教仪节圆满的筹备，没有一样被疏漏。

===Original===
To make ready for the dancing the grass on a large circular space was closely mowed.

===Chinese===
为了备好舞场，一大块圆形场地上的草被割得干干净净。

===Original===
The singing was led by Chief Naiche, and I, assisted by our medicine men, directed the dance.

===Chinese===
歌唱由纳切酋长领唱，我则在巫医们的协助下指挥舞蹈。

===Original===
First Eva advanced from among the women and danced once around the campfire; then, accompanied by another young woman, she again advanced and both danced twice around the campfire; then she and two other young ladies advanced and danced three times around the campfire; the next time she and three other young ladies advanced and danced four times around the campfire; this ceremony lasted about one hour. Next the medicine men entered, stripped to the waist, their bodies painted fantastically, and danced the sacred dances. They were followed by clown dancers, who amused the audience greatly.

===Chinese===
起初，伊娃从妇女们中间走出来，绕营火跳了一圈；随后她与另一位少女一同上前，两人绕营火跳了两圈；再后她与另外两位少女上前，绕营火跳了三圈；下一次，她与另外三位少女上前，绕营火跳了四圈；这一仪式约摸持续了一个钟头。接着巫医们入场，赤着上身，身上彩绘得光怪陆离，跳起神圣的舞蹈。随后是滑稽舞者，逗得众人十分开心。

===Original===
Then the members of the tribe joined hands and danced in a circle around the campfire for a long time. All the friends of the tribe were asked to take part in this dance, and when it was ended many of the old people retired, and the “lovers’ dance” began.

===Chinese===
随后，族人们手拉着手，围着营火绕圈跳了许久。部落的所有朋友都被请来共舞；这场舞结束后，许多老人退了席，「情人舞」便开始了。

===Original===
The warriors stood in the middle of the circle and the ladies, two-and-two, danced forward and designated some warrior to dance with them. The dancing was back and forth on a line from the center to the outer edge of the circle. The warrior faced the two ladies, and when they danced forward to the center he danced backward: then they danced backward to the outer edge and he followed facing them. This lasted two or three hours and then the music changed. Immediately the warriors assembled again in the center of the circle, and this time each lady selected a warrior as a partner. The manner of dancing was as before, only two instead of three danced together. During this dance, which continued until daylight, the warrior (if dancing with a maiden) could propose51 marriage, and if the maiden agreed, he would consult her father soon afterward and make a bargain for her.

===Chinese===
武士们站在圆圈中央，女士们两人一排，舞步向前，指定某位武士与她们共舞。舞步沿一条从圆心到圈外的直线来回移动。武士面向两位女士：她们朝圆心跳来，他便向后退着跳；她们退向圈外，他便面向她们跟上去。这样跳了两三个钟头，乐声一变。武士们立刻又在圆心聚拢，这一回，每位女士各挑一位武士作舞伴。跳法同先前一样，只是两人共舞，不再是三人。这场舞一直跳到天亮；舞间（若是与少女共舞）武士可以求婚 51；少女若应允，他随后不久便去见她的父亲，为她议定婚事。

===Original===
Upon all such occasions as this, when the dance is finished, each warrior gives a present to the lady who selected him for a partner and danced with him. If she is satisfied with the present he says goodbye, if not, the matter is referred to someone in authority (medicine man or chief), who determines the question of what is a proper gift.

===Chinese===
每逢这样的场合，舞毕之后，每位武士都要送一件礼物给挑他作舞伴、与他共舞的那位女士。她若对礼物满意，他便道别；若不满意，这事便交由某位有权威的人（巫医或酋长）裁决，由他判定怎样才算一件合宜的礼物。

===Original===
For a married lady the value of the present should be two or three dollars; for a maiden the present should have a value of not less than five dollars. Often, however, the maiden receives a very valuable present.

===Chinese===
送给已婚女士的礼物，价值应为两三块钱；送给少女的礼物，价值不得少于五块钱。不过，少女收到的礼物往往十分贵重。

===Original===
During the “lovers’ dance” the medicine men mingle with the dancers to keep out evil spirits.

===Chinese===
「情人舞」进行时，巫医们混在舞者中间，驱逐邪灵，不使近前。

===Original===
Perhaps I shall never again have cause to assemble our people to dance, but these social dances in the moonlight have been a large part of our enjoyment in the past, and I think they will not soon be discontinued, at least I hope not.

===Chinese===
也许我再没有缘由召集我们的人跳舞了；但月光下的这些社交之舞，过去曾是我们欢乐的一大部分。我想它们不会很快废止——至少我希望不会。

## XXI At the World’s Fair / 二十一 在世界博览会上

===Original===
When I was at first asked to attend the St. Louis World’s Fair I did not wish to go. Later, when I was told that I would receive good attention and protection, and that the President of the United States said that it would be all right, I consented. I was kept by parties in charge of the Indian Department, who had obtained permission from the President. I stayed in this place for six months. I sold my photographs for twenty-five cents, and was allowed to keep ten cents of this for myself. I also wrote my name for ten, fifteen, or twenty-five cents, as the case might be, and kept all of that money. I often made as much as two dollars a day, and when I returned I had plenty of money—more than I had ever owned before.

===Chinese===
起初有人请我去参加圣路易斯（St. Louis）世界博览会，我不愿去。后来他们告诉我，我会得到很好的照料和保护，合众国总统也说这没有问题，我便答应了。照管我的是印第安事务部（Indian Department）的几位负责人，他们已得到总统的许可。我在那个地方待了六个月。我卖自己的相片，每张两角五分钱，准我留下一角钱归自己。我还给人签写名字，收一角、一角五分或两角五分钱不等，视情形而定，这些钱全归我自己。我常常一天挣到两块钱；回来的时候，我手里的钱很宽裕——比我这辈子曾经有过的都多。

===Original===
Many people in St. Louis invited me to come to their homes, but my keeper always refused.

===Chinese===
圣路易斯的许多人请我到他们家里去，可看守我的人总不肯。

===Original===
Every Sunday the President of the Fair sent for me to go to a wild west show. I took part in the roping contests before the audience. There were many other Indian tribes there, and strange people of whom I had never heard.

===Chinese===
每个礼拜天，博览会的会长都派人来接我去看「狂野西部」表演。我在观众面前参加了套绳比赛。那里还有许多别的印第安部落，还有我从未听说过的奇异的人。

===Original===
When people first came to the World’s Fair they did nothing but parade up and down the streets. When they got tired of this they would visit the shows. There were many strange things in these shows. The Government sent guards with me when I went, and I was not allowed to go anywhere without them.

===Chinese===
人们初到世界博览会的时候，什么也不做，只在街上来来回回地游行。游腻了，便去看各种演出。这些演出里有许多奇异的东西。我去的时候，政府派卫兵跟着我；没有他们跟着，我哪里也不许去。

===Original===
In one of the shows some strange men52 with red caps had some peculiar swords, and they seemed to want to fight. Finally their manager told them they might fight each other. They tried to hit each other over the head with these swords, and I expected both to be wounded or perhaps killed, but neither one was harmed. They would be hard people to kill in a hand-to-hand fight.

===Chinese===
在一处演出里，几个戴红帽子的怪人 52 拿着些奇形怪状的剑，看样子是想打架。后来他们的领班说，他们可以互相比试。他们抡起那些剑朝对方的头上打去；我以为两人都会受伤，说不定会丧命，可谁也没有伤着。这种人在徒手相搏的厮杀里，怕是很难杀死的。

===Original===
In another show there was a strange-looking negro. The manager tied his hands fast, then tied him to a chair. He was securely tied, for I looked myself, and I did not think it was possible for him to get away. Then the manager told him to get loose.

===Chinese===
另一处演出里有一个长相古怪的黑人。领班把他的双手牢牢捆住，又把他捆在椅子上。捆得结结实实——我亲自察看过——我想他绝不可能脱身。随后领班叫他挣脱开。

===Original===
He twisted in his chair for a moment, and then stood up; the ropes were still tied, but he was free. I do not understand how this was done. It was certainly a miraculous power, because no man could have released himself by his own efforts.

===Chinese===
他在椅子上扭了一阵，随即站了起来；绳子还捆着，人却自由了。我不明白这是怎么弄的。这必定是一种神奇的本领，因为寻常人靠自己的力气是决计挣脱不开的。

===Original===
In another place a man was on a platform speaking to the audience; they set a basket by the side of the platform and covered it with red calico; then a woman came and got into the basket, and a man covered the basket again with the calico; then the man who was speaking to the audience took a long sword and ran it through the basket, each way, and then down through the cloth cover. I heard the sword cut through the woman’s body, and the manager himself said she was dead; but when the cloth was lifted from the basket she stepped out, smiled, and walked off the stage. I would like to know how she was so quickly healed, and why the wounds did not kill her.

===Chinese===
在另一处，一个人站在台子上向观众讲话；他们在台边放了一只筐，用红印花布蒙上；随后来了一个女人，钻进筐里，一个人又用印花布把筐蒙住；接着，那个向观众讲话的人拿过一把长剑，横一遍竖一遍地穿透筐子，又从布罩正中直贯下去。我听见剑割过那女人的身体，领班自己也说她死了；可等布从筐上掀开，她跨了出来，微微一笑，走下了舞台。我很想知道，她的伤怎么好得这样快，为什么剑伤没有要她的命。

===Original===
I have never considered bears very intelligent, except in their wild habits, but I had never before seen a white bear. In one of the shows a man had a white bear that was as intelligent as a man. He would do whatever he was told—carry a log on his shoulder, just as a man would; then, when he was told, would put it down again. He did many other things, and seemed to know exactly what his keeper said to him. I am sure that no grizzly bear could be trained to do these things.

===Chinese===
我从来不认为熊有多聪明——除了它们野外的习性之外；可我从前也从没见过白熊。一处演出里，有个人养着一头白熊，聪明得跟人一样。叫它做什么它就做什么——像人那样把一根木头扛上肩；叫它放下，它便又放下。它还做了许多别的事，似乎饲养人对它说的话它全都明白。我敢说，没有一头灰熊能被训练来做这些事。

===Original===
One time the guards took me into a little house53 that had four windows. When we were seated the little house started to move along the ground. Then the guards called my attention to some curious things they had in their pockets. Finally they told me to look out, and when I did so I was scared, for our little house had gone high up in the air, and the people down in the Fair Grounds looked no larger than ants. The men laughed at me for being scared; then they gave me a glass to look through (I often had such glasses which I took from dead officers after battles in Mexico and elsewhere), and I could see rivers, lakes and mountains. But I had never been so high in the air, and I tried to look into the sky. There were no stars, and I could not look at the sun through this glass because the brightness hurt my eyes. Finally I put the glass down, and as they were all laughing at me, I too, began to laugh. Then they said, “Get out!” and when I looked we were on the street again. After we were safe on the land I watched many of these little houses going up and coming down, but I cannot understand how they travel. They are very curious little houses.

===Chinese===
有一回，卫兵带我进了一座有四扇窗子的小房子 53。我们刚坐下，小房子便顺着地面走动起来。接着卫兵们引我去看他们衣袋里的几件稀奇玩意。末了他们叫我往外看；我一看，吓了一跳——我们那座小房子已经升到高高的半空，下面博览会场里的人看上去比蚂蚁大不了多少。他们笑我胆小；随后递给我一支望远镜朝外看（这样的镜子我从前有过不少，都是在墨西哥等地打仗后从阵亡军官身上拿的），我看见了河流、湖泊和群山。可我从来没有到过这么高的天上，便想往天上看。天上没有星星；太阳又不能隔着镜子看，太亮了，刺得我眼睛疼。末了我放下镜子；见他们都在笑我，我也笑了起来。随后他们喊：「出去！」我一看，我们已经又回到了街上。平安落地之后，我望着许多这样的小房子升上去、落下来，可我还是闹不清它们是怎么走的。这些小房子真是稀奇。

===Original===
One day we went into another show, and as soon as we were in, it changed into night. It was real night, for I could feel the damp air; soon it began to thunder, and the lightnings flashed; it was real lightning, too, for it struck just above our heads. I dodged and wanted to run away, but I could not tell which way to go in order to get out. The guards motioned me to keep still, and so I stayed. In front of us were some strange little people who came out on the platform; then I looked up again and the clouds were all gone, and I could see the stars shining. The little people on the platform did not seem in earnest about anything they did; so I only laughed at them. All the people around where we sat seemed to be laughing at me.

===Chinese===
有一天，我们走进另一处演出，刚一进去，天就变成了黑夜。那是真的夜，因为我摸得着潮湿的空气；不多时打起雷来，电光闪闪；那也是真的闪电，因为它就劈在我们头顶上。我闪身躲避，想要逃跑，却辨不清该往哪边走才能出去。卫兵们打手势叫我别动，我便留下了。我们前面有些古怪的小人，走到台上出来；我再抬头看时，云全散了，看得见星星在闪亮。台上那些小人，做什么都不像是当真的；所以我只是笑他们。可是坐在我们四周的人，倒像都在笑我。

===Original===
We went into another place and the manager took us into a little room that was made like a cage; then everything around us seemed to be moving; soon the air looked blue, then there were black clouds moving with the wind. Pretty soon it was clear outside; then we saw a few thin white clouds; then the clouds grew thicker, and it rained and hailed with thunder and lightning. Then the thunder retreated and a rainbow appeared in the distance; then it became dark, the moon rose and thousands of stars came out. Soon the sun came up, and we got out of the little room. This was a good show, but it was so strange and unnatural that I was glad to be on the streets again.

===Chinese===
我们走进另一处，领班把我们带进一间造得像笼子的小屋；随后我们周围的一切仿佛都动了起来；不多时天色发蓝，接着乌云随风翻动。顷刻间外面又晴了；先看见几缕薄薄的白云；云层渐渐变厚，又是雨又是雹，雷鸣电闪。随后雷声远去，一道彩虹出现在远处；接着天又黑了，月亮升起，千万颗星星出来。不久太阳升起，我们便走出了那间小屋。这场演出很好，只是太古怪、太不像真的，能重新回到街上，我很高兴。

===Original===
We went into one place where they made glassware. I had always thought that these things were made by hand, but they are not. The man had a curious little instrument, and whenever he would blow through this into a little blaze the glass would take any shape he wanted it to. I am not sure, but I think that if I had this kind of an instrument I could make whatever I wished. There seems to be a charm about it. But I suppose it is very difficult to get these little instruments, or other people would have them. The people in this show were so anxious to buy the things the man made that they kept him so busy he could not sit down all day long. I bought many curious things in there and brought them home with me.

===Chinese===
我们走进一处制造玻璃器皿的地方。我一向以为这些东西是用手做出来的，其实不然。那人拿着一件古怪的小器具，只要朝一小簇火苗里吹气，玻璃便会变成他要的任何形状。我不敢肯定，但我想，若我有这么一件器具，想要什么就能做出什么。那里头像是有一种法术。不过我猜想，这样的小器具一定很难弄到，不然别人早就都有了。这场演出里的人们急着买那人做的东西，害得他忙得整天都坐不下来。我在那里买了许多稀奇玩意，带回了家。

===Original===
At the end of one of the streets some people were getting into a clumsy canoe, upon a kind of shelf, and sliding down into the water.54 They seemed to enjoy it, but it looked too fierce for me. If one of these canoes had gone out of its path the people would have been sure to get hurt or killed.

===Chinese===
在一条街的尽头，有些人登上一种搁在架子上的笨重独木舟，顺着滑进水里。54 他们看上去玩得很尽兴，可在我眼里那太凶险了。这些独木舟只要有一条滑出了轨道，舟里的人准会受伤，说不定丧命。

===Original===
There were some little brown people55 at the Fair that United States troops captured recently on some islands far away from here.

===Chinese===
博览会 55 上还有一些棕色的小个子人，是美国部队不久前在离这里很远的一些海岛上俘虏来的。

===Original===
They did not wear much clothing, and I think that they should not have been allowed to come to the Fair. But they themselves did not seem to know any better. They had some little brass plates, and they tried to play music with these, but I did not think it was music—it was only a rattle. However, they danced to this noise and seemed to think they were giving a fine show.

===Chinese===
他们身上穿得很少，我看就不该放他们到博览会上来。可他们自己也似乎不觉得有什么不妥。他们有几面小铜片，想用它奏乐；我听来那算不得音乐——不过是哗啷哗啷的响声。可他们竟随着这响声跳舞，还自以为演得很出色呢。

===Original===
I do not know how true the report was, but I heard that the President sent them to the Fair so that they could learn some manners, and when they went home teach their people how to dress and how to behave.

===Chinese===
不知这传闻有几分真：我听说，总统把他们送到博览会来，是想让他们学点规矩，回家之后好教他们的族人怎样穿衣、怎样行事。

===Original===
I am glad I went to the Fair. I saw many interesting things and learned much of the white people. They are a very kind and peaceful people. During all the time I was at the Fair no one tried to harm me in any way. Had this been among the Mexicans I am sure I should have been compelled to defend myself often.

===Chinese===
我庆幸自己去了博览会。我见到了许多有趣的东西，对白人也了解了许多。他们是很和善、爱好和平的人。我在博览会的全部日子里，没有人想以任何方式害我。倘若是在墨西哥人中间，我敢说，我准得常常被迫自卫。

===Original===
I wish all my people could have attended the Fair.56

===Chinese===
但愿我的人民都去过那博览会。56

## XXII Religion / 二十二 宗教

===Original===
In our primitive worship only our relations to Usen and the members of our tribe were considered as appertaining to our religious responsibilities. As to the future state, the teachings of our tribe were not specific, that is, we had no definite idea of our relations and surroundings in after life. We believed that there is a life after this one, but no one ever told me as to what part of man lived after death. I have seen many men die; I have seen many human bodies decayed, but I have never seen that part which is called the spirit; I do not know what it is; nor have I yet been able to understand that part of the Christian religion.

===Chinese===
在我们原始的崇拜里，只有我们同天神乌森（Usen）的关系、同本族族人的关系，才算属于我们的宗教本分。至于来世的境况，我们部族的教诲并不明确；也就是说，对于死后我们的关系和处境，我们并没有确切的概念。我们相信人死后还有一生，可从没有人告诉过我，人身上是哪一部分在死后还活着。我见过许多人死去；我见过许多人的身体腐烂，可我从来没有见过那个被称作灵魂的部分；我不知道它是什么；基督教教义中的那一层道理，我至今也未能明白。

===Original===
We held that the discharge of one’s duty would make his future life more pleasant, but whether that future life was worse than this life or better, we did not know, and no one was able to tell us. We hoped that in the future life family and tribal relations would be resumed. In a way we believed this, but we did not know it.

===Chinese===
我们认为，尽了自己的本分，来生便会过得更好些；可那来生究竟比今生坏些还是好些，我们不知道，也没有人能告诉我们。我们盼望在来生里，家庭与部落的关系能够延续。在某种程度上我们相信这一点，但我们并不确知。

===Original===
Once when living in San Carlos Reservation an Indian told me that while lying unconscious on the battlefield he had actually been dead, and had passed into the spirit land.

===Chinese===
有一回，住在圣卡洛斯（San Carlos）保留地时，一个印第安人告诉我，他昏死在战场上的那阵子，其实已经死了，还到过亡灵之地。

===Original===
First he came to a mulberry tree growing out from a cave in the ground. Before this cave a guard was stationed, but when he approached without fear the guard let him pass. He descended into the cave, and a little way back the path widened and terminated in a perpendicular rock many hundreds of feet wide and equal in height. There was not much light, but by peering directly beneath him he discovered a pile of sand reaching from the depths below to within twenty feet of the top of the rock where he stood. Holding to a bush, he swung off from the edge of the rock and dropped onto the sand, sliding rapidly down its steep side into the darkness. He landed in a narrow passage running due westward through a canyon which gradually grew lighter and lighter until he could see as well as if it had been daylight; but there was no sun. Finally he came to a section of this passage that was wider for a short distance, and then closing abruptly continued in a narrow path; just where this section narrowed two huge serpents were coiled, and rearing their heads, hissed at him as he approached, but he showed no fear, and as soon as he came close to them they withdrew quietly and let him pass. At the next place, where the passage opened into a wider section, were two grizzly bears prepared to attack him, but when he approached and spoke to them they stood aside and he passed unharmed. He continued to follow the narrow passage, and the third time it widened and two mountain lions crouched in the way, but when he had approached them without fear and had spoken to them they also withdrew. He again entered the narrow passage. For some time he followed this, emerging into a fourth section beyond which he could see nothing: the further walls of this section were clashing together at regular intervals with tremendous sounds, but when he approached them they stood apart until he had passed. After this he seemed to be in a forest, and following the natural draws, which led westward, soon came into a green valley where there were many Indians camped and plenty of game. He said that he saw and recognized many whom he had known in this life, and that he was sorry when he was brought back to consciousness.

===Chinese===
他先来到一棵桑树前，那树从地上的一个洞穴里长出来。洞穴前站着一个守卫，可他毫无惧色地走上前去，守卫便放他过去了。他下到洞里，走不多远，路径豁然开阔，尽头是一面垂直的岩壁，宽达数百英尺，高也与宽相仿。那里光线不多，可他朝脚底细细张望，发现一堆沙，从下面的深处一直堆到离他站立处的岩顶不到二十英尺的地方。他攀着一丛灌木，从岩边荡身跃下，落在沙堆上，顺着陡峭的沙坡疾滑而下，滑进黑暗里。他落在一条狭窄的通道里，通道笔直向西，穿过一道峡谷，越走越亮，亮得他看东西如同白昼一般；只是没有太阳。最后他来到通道中一段较宽的地方，短短一段之后又骤然收窄，仍是一条窄路；恰在这段收窄的地方，盘着两条巨蛇，昂着头，朝走近的他嘶嘶吐信；他毫无惧色，等他走到近前，两条蛇便悄悄退开，放他过去。下一处，通道又敞开成较宽的一段，那里立着两头灰熊，作势要扑他；可他走上前去，对它们说了话，它们便站到一旁，让他安然走过。他继续沿着窄路前行，第三次路径放宽，两头美洲狮伏在道中；他毫无惧色地走上前去，对它们说了话，它们也退开了。他再次进入窄路。沿这条路走了一阵，他进入第四段开阔处，再往前便什么都看不见了：这一段远端的两面石壁正有节奏地相互撞合，声若巨雷；可他走近时，两壁便分开立定，等他过去。此后他仿佛置身一片森林，顺着向西的天然沟壑走，不久便来到一座绿色的山谷，那里扎营着许多印第安人，猎物也很丰富。他说，他见到了许多今生认识的人，还认出了他们；被人救醒带回人世时，他心里还很懊恼。

===Original===
I told him if I knew this to be true I would not want to live another day, but by some means, if by my own hands, I would die in order to enjoy these pleasures. I myself have lain unconscious on the battlefield, and while in that condition have had some strange thoughts or experiences; but they are very dim and I cannot recall them well enough to relate them. Many Indians believed this warrior, and I cannot say that he did not tell the truth. I wish I knew that what he said is beyond question true. But perhaps it is as well that we are not certain.

===Chinese===
我对他说，倘若我确知这是真的，我便一天也不想再活了，总要想法子死去——哪怕亲手下手——好去享受那些快乐。我自己也曾在战场上昏死过去，在那状态里也有一些奇异的念头或经历；可它们都很模糊，我回想不起来，讲不周全。许多印第安人相信这个武士的话，我也不能说他讲的不是真话。但愿我能确知他说的话千真万确。可也许，我们不确定反倒好。

===Original===
Since my life as a prisoner has begun I have heard the teachings of the white man’s religion, and in many respects believe it to be better than the religion of my fathers. However, I have always prayed, and I believe that the Almighty has always protected me.

===Chinese===
自从沦为战俘，我听到了白人宗教的教诲，并且在许多方面相信它比我祖辈的宗教更好。不过，我从来都祈祷，也相信全能的神一直在护佑我。

===Original===
Believing that in a wise way it is good to go to church, and that associating with Christians would improve my character, I have adopted the Christian religion.57 I believe that the church has helped me much during the short time I have been a member. I am not ashamed to be a Christian, and I am glad to know that the President of the United States is a Christian, for without the help of the Almighty I do not think he could rightly judge in ruling so many people. I have advised all of my people who are not Christians, to study that religion, because it seems to me the best religion in enabling one to live right.

===Chinese===
我相信，明智地上教堂是好的，与基督徒交往能改进我的品性，于是我皈依了基督教。57 我相信，在我入会的短短时日里，教会帮了我很多。做一个基督徒，我并不羞愧；得知合众国总统也是基督徒，我很高兴；因为没有全能的神相助，我想他统治这么多的人，是断难裁断公正的。我已劝告我所有还不是基督徒的族人，去研习那宗教；因为在我看来，那是引导人正直生活的最好的宗教。

## XXIII Hopes for the Future / 二十三 对未来的希望

===Original===
I am thankful that the President of the United States has given me permission to tell my story. I hope that he and those in authority under him will read my story and judge whether my people have been rightly treated.

===Chinese===
合众国总统准许我讲述我的故事，我心怀感激。我盼望他和他的属下当权者们读到我的故事，裁断我的人民是否得到了公正的对待。

===Original===
