There is a great question between the Apaches and the Government. For twenty years we have been held prisoners of war under a treaty which was made with General Miles, on the part of the United States Government, and myself as the representative of the Apaches. That treaty has not at all times been properly observed by the Government, although at the present time it is being more nearly fulfilled on their part than heretofore. In the treaty with General Miles we agreed to go to a place outside of Arizona and learn to live as the white people do. I think that my people are now capable of living in accordance with the laws of the United States, and we would, of course, like to have the liberty to return to that land which is ours by divine right. We are reduced in numbers, and having learned how to cultivate the soil would not require so much ground as was formerly necessary. We do not ask all of the land which the Almighty gave us in the beginning, but that we may have sufficient lands there to cultivate. What we do not need we are glad for the white men to cultivate.

===Chinese===
阿帕奇人（Apaches）与政府之间，有一桩大事悬而未决。二十年来，我们一直被当作战俘关押，所依据的是同迈尔斯将军（General Miles）订立的一项条约——他代表合众国政府，我代表阿帕奇人。这项条约，政府并未时时切实遵行，尽管眼下他们履行得比以往更接近条约了。在同迈尔斯将军的条约里，我们同意前往亚利桑那（Arizona）以外的一个地方，学会像白人那样生活。我想，我的人民如今已经有能力遵照美国的法律生活了；我们当然愿意获得自由，回到那片凭神授之权属于我们的土地。我们人数已经减少，又学会了耕种，不再需要从前那么多土地。我们并不要求全能的神当初赐给我们的全部土地，只求在那里有足够的土地可以耕种。我们用不了的土地，乐意让白人去耕种。

===Original===
We are now held on Comanche and Kiowa lands, which are not suited to our needs—these lands and this climate are suited to the Indians who originally inhabited this country, of course, but our people are decreasing in numbers here, and will continue to decrease unless they are allowed to return to their native land. Such a result is inevitable.

===Chinese===
我们如今被安置在科曼奇人（Comanche）和基奥瓦人（Kiowa）的土地上，这里不合我们的需要——这片土地和这种气候，当然适合原本就居住在这一带的印第安人，可我们的族人在这里日渐减少，除非准许他们返回故土，还会继续减少下去。这样的结果是不可避免的。

===Original===
There is no climate or soil which, to my mind, is equal to that of Arizona. We could have plenty of good cultivating land, plenty of grass, plenty of timber and plenty of minerals in that land which the Almighty created for the Apaches. It is my land, my home, my fathers’ land, to which I now ask to be allowed to return. I want to spend my last days there, and be buried among those mountains. If this could be I might die in peace, feeling that my people, placed in their native homes, would increase in numbers, rather than diminish as at present, and that our name would not become extinct.

===Chinese===
依我看，天下的气候和土壤，没有一处比得上亚利桑那。在那片全能的神为阿帕奇人创造的土地上，我们有的是良田、牧草、木材和矿藏。那是我的土地，我的家园，我祖辈的土地；如今我请求准许我回去。我要在那里度过余生，葬在那些群山之间。倘若能够如此，我便可安然死去，因为我知道，我的人民回到故土之后，人丁会重新兴旺，不像如今这样凋零，我们的名字也不会灭绝。

===Original===
I know that if my people were placed in that mountainous region lying around the headwaters of the Gila River they would live in peace and act according to the will of the President. They would be prosperous and happy in tilling the soil and learning the civilization of the white men, whom they now respect. Could I but see this accomplished, I think I could forget all the wrongs that I have ever received, and die a contented and happy old man. But we can do nothing in this matter ourselves—we must wait until those in authority choose to act. If this cannot be done during my lifetime—if I must die in bondage—I hope that the remnant of the Apache tribe may, when I am gone, be granted the one privilege which they request—to return to Arizona.

===Chinese===
我知道，倘若把我的人民安置在希拉河（Gila River）源头四周的那片山区，他们会安居乐业，遵照总统的意旨行事。他们会耕种土地，学习他们如今敬重的白人的文明，兴旺而幸福。只要能亲眼见到这一天，我想，我遭受过的一切冤屈都能忘却，心满意足、快快乐乐地做一个老人死去。可是在这件事上，我们自己无能为力——只能等当权者们愿意行动。倘若我生前见不到这一天——倘若我必须戴着枷锁死去——我希望，在我身后，阿帕奇部落的余众能蒙准他们所求的唯一一项恩典——回到亚利桑那去。

## Endnotes / 尾注

===Original===
1. See Chapter IV. ↩︎

===Chinese===
1. 见第四章。 ↩︎

===Original===
2. *Usen* is the Apache word for God. It is used here because it implies the attributes of deity that are held in their primitive religion. *Apache* means “Enemy.” ↩︎

===Chinese===
2. 乌森（*Usen*）是阿帕奇语中对神的称呼。此处沿用原词，因为它包含着阿帕奇人原始宗教中所持的神明属性。阿帕奇（*Apache*）意为「敌人」。 ↩︎

===Original===
3. The boundary lines established at different times between Mexico and the United States did not conform to the boundary lines of these Apache tribes, of course, and the Indians soon saw and took advantage of the international questions arising from the conflicting interests of the two governments. ↩︎

===Chinese===
3. 墨西哥与美国之间在不同时期划定的边界线，自然与这些阿帕奇部落的界线不相吻合；印第安人很快便看出两国政府利益冲突所引出的国际问题，并借以自利。 ↩︎

===Original===
4. The Apache Indian held prisoners of war are greatly decreasing in numbers. There seems to be no particular cause, but nevertheless their numbers grow smaller. ↩︎

===Chinese===
4. 被当作战俘关押的阿帕奇印第安人，人数正在锐减。似乎并无特别的原因，然而他们的人数日渐稀少。 ↩︎

===Original===
5. Four is a magic number with the Bedonkohe Apaches. The dragon had four coats of scales; he took little Apache’s meat four times; they (the dragon and Apache) exchanged four shots—the dragon rolled down four precipices. There are four moccasins used in the tribal game of Kah, and only four plays that can be made. A boy must accompany the warriors four times on the warpath before he can be admitted to the council.

 Geronimo is the fourth of a family of four boys and four girls. He has had four wives that were full-blood Bedonkohe Apaches, and four that were part Bedonkohe Apache and part other Apache blood. Four of his children have been killed by Mexicans and four have been held in bondage by the U.S. Government. He firmly believes in destiny and in the magic of the number four. Besides Geronimo, only four full-blood Bedonkohe Apaches are now living. They are Porico (White Horse), Nah-da-ste, Moh-ta-neal, and To-klon-nen. ↩︎

===Chinese===
5. 四对于贝东科黑（Bedonkohe）阿帕奇人是一个具有魔力的数字。龙有四层鳞甲；它四次抢走小阿帕奇的肉；两者（龙与阿帕奇）互射了四箭——龙翻滚着跌下了四道峭壁。部落的赌戏卡戏（Kah）用四只莫卡辛鞋，能出的招数也只有四种。男孩必须随武士出征四次，方能进入议事会。

 杰罗尼莫在家中排行第四，家中原有四个男孩、四个女孩。他先后娶过四位纯血统的贝东科黑阿帕奇妻子，又娶过四位兼有贝东科黑与其他阿帕奇血统的妻子。他的孩子中，四个被墨西哥人杀害，四个被美国政府囚禁。他坚信命运，也坚信数字四的魔力。如今在世的纯血统贝东科黑阿帕奇人，除杰罗尼莫外仅剩四人：波里科（Porico，白马 White Horse）、纳达斯特（Nah-da-ste）、莫塔尼尔（Moh-ta-neal）和托克隆嫩（To-klon-nen）。 ↩︎

===Original===
6. The Apaches did not smoke the peace pipe, unless it was proposed by some other Indians. They had no large pipes; in fact, they usually smoked cigarettes made by rolling the tobacco in wrappers of oak leaves. ↩︎

===Chinese===
6. 阿帕奇人不抽和平烟斗，除非是别的印第安人提议。他们没有大烟斗；实际上，他们通常抽的是用橡树叶卷裹烟丝做成的烟卷。 ↩︎

===Original===
7. The only foundation for the statement, frequently made, that Geronimo was a medicine man. ↩︎

===Chinese===
7. 此即人们常说杰罗尼莫是巫医的唯一依据。 ↩︎

===Original===
8. The Apaches recognized no duties to any man outside their tribe. It was no sin to kill enemies or to rob them. However, if they accepted any favor from a stranger, or allowed him to share their comforts in any way, he became (by adoption) related to the tribe and they must recognize their duty to him. ↩︎

===Chinese===
8. 阿帕奇人不对本族以外的人承担任何义务。杀死敌人或劫掠敌人都不算罪过。然而，他们若受了外人任何恩惠，或容外人以任何方式分享他们的安逸，此人便（经由收养）成了部族的亲族，他们就必须对他尽义务。 ↩︎

===Original===
9. Maco was chief of the Nedni Apaches. His son (Geronimo’s father) had married a Bedonkohe Apache (Geronimo’s mother) and joined her tribe, thereby losing his right to rule by heredity. By this it will be seen Geronimo could not become chief by hereditary right, although his grandfather was a chieftain. It is also shown that Geronimo’s father could not be chief, hence the accession of Mangus-Colorado. ↩︎

===Chinese===
9. 马科（Maco）是内德尼阿帕奇人的酋长。他的儿子（杰罗尼莫的父亲）娶了贝东科黑阿帕奇女子（杰罗尼莫的母亲），并入赘她的部落，因而丧失了世袭统治之权。由此可见，杰罗尼莫虽有一位做过酋长的祖父，却不能凭世袭之权成为酋长。这也说明杰罗尼莫的父亲做不成酋长，于是才有了曼加斯-科罗拉多（Mangus-Colorado）的继位。 ↩︎

===Original===
10. The Apaches will not keep any of the property of a deceased relative. Their unwritten tribal laws forbid it, because they think that otherwise the children or other relatives of one who had much property might be glad when their father or relatives died. ↩︎

===Chinese===
10. 阿帕奇人不保留亡故亲属的任何财物。部族的不成文法禁止这样做，因为他们认为，若不如此，家产多的人的子女或其他亲属，可能会盼着父亲或亲人死去。 ↩︎

===Original===
11. Beads were obtained from the Mexicans. The Apaches also got money from the Mexicans, but deemed it of no value, and either gave it to their children to play with or threw it away. ↩︎

===Chinese===
11. 珠子是从墨西哥人那里得来的。阿帕奇人也从墨西哥人那里得到过钱，却认为它毫无价值，或是给孩子当玩物，或是随手扔掉。 ↩︎

===Original===
12. According to custom he should not have kept the property of his deceased relatives, but he was not compelled to destroy his own teepee or the playthings of his children. ↩︎

===Chinese===
12. 按习俗，他本不应保留亡故亲属的财物，但人们并没有强迫他毁掉自己的梯皮帐篷或孩子们的玩具。 ↩︎

===Original===
13. Strips of buckskin about two inches wide fastened around the head. ↩︎

===Chinese===
13. 缠在头上的鹿皮条，宽约两英寸。 ↩︎

===Original===
14. At this time the Mexican Government offered a reward in gold for Apache scalps—one hundred dollars for warrior’s scalp, fifty dollars for squaw’s scalp, and twenty-five dollars for child’s scalp. ↩︎

===Chinese===
14. 此时，墨西哥政府悬赏黄金收购阿帕奇人的头皮——武士的头皮一幅一百元，妇人的头皮五十元，孩子的头皮二十五元。 ↩︎

===Original===
15. From the moment the command for war is given with the Apaches everything assumes a religious guise. The manner of camping, cooking, etc., are exactly prescribed. Every object appertaining to war is called by its sacred name; as if, for instance, in English, one should say not horse, but warhorse or charger; not arrow, but missile of death. The Indian is not called by his ordinary name, but by a sacred name to which is subjoined “brave” or “chief” as the case may be. Geronimo’s Indian name was Go-khlä-yeh, but the Mexicans at this battle called him Geronimo, a name he has borne ever since both among the Indians and white men. ↩︎

===Chinese===
15. 在阿帕奇人那里，开战的号令一经发出，万事便都披上宗教的外衣。扎营、做饭等等的方式，都有严格的规定。凡与战争相关的事物，都改用它的圣名称呼；好比在英语里，不说马，而说战马或坐骑；不说箭，而说死亡之矢。印第安人也不再叫他的寻常名字，而是称呼他的圣名，并视情况缀上「勇士」或「酋长」。杰罗尼莫的印第安名字是戈克莱耶（Go-khlä-yeh），但墨西哥人在这一仗里叫他杰罗尼莫；此后无论在印第安人还是白人中间，他一直沿用这个名字。 ↩︎

===Original===
16. Geronimo had married again. ↩︎

===Chinese===
16. 杰罗尼莫那时已再娶。 ↩︎

===Original===
17. They had never eaten bacon and did not learn to do so for a long time. Even now they will not eat bacon or pork if they can get other meat. Geronimo positively refuses to eat bacon or pork. ↩︎

===Chinese===
17. 他们从未吃过腌肉，很久也没学会吃。直到如今，只要能得到别的肉，他们就不吃腌肉或猪肉。杰罗尼莫则断然不肯吃腌肉和猪肉。 ↩︎

===Original===
18. The interpreter, Asa, son of Whoa, remembers a little captive Mexican girl who used to play with the Apache children, but was finally exchanged.

 One of Geronimo’s wives and her child were killed at this time, and thenceforth until he became a prisoner of war he had two wives. He might have had as many wives as he wished, but he says that he was so busy fighting Mexicans that he could not support more than two. ↩︎

===Chinese===
18. 译员阿萨（Asa）是沃阿之子，他记得一个小小的墨西哥女俘，她从前常同阿帕奇孩子一起玩耍，后来被交换了回去。

 杰罗尼莫的一位妻子和她的孩子于此时遇害；此后直到沦为战俘，他一直有两位妻子。他本可以想要多少妻子便娶多少，可他说，他忙于同墨西哥人打仗，养活不起两个以上。 ↩︎

===Original===
19. Forty-five miles. ↩︎

===Chinese===
19. 四十五英里。 ↩︎

===Original===
20. Mescal is a fiery liquor produced in Mexico from several species of agave. ↩︎

===Chinese===
20. 梅斯卡尔（Mescal）是墨西哥用几种龙舌兰酿制的烈酒。 ↩︎

===Original===
21. Gulf of California. ↩︎

===Chinese===
21. 加利福尼亚湾。 ↩︎

===Original===
22. It is impossible to get Geronimo to understand that these troops served the general government instead of any particular town. He still thinks each town independent and each city a separate tribe. He cannot understand the relation of cities to the general government. ↩︎

===Chinese===
22. 要叫杰罗尼莫明白这些部队效命的是联邦政府而非某个城镇，是不可能的。他至今仍以为每个镇子各自为政，每座城是一个独立的部落。他无法理解城市与联邦政府之间的关系。 ↩︎

===Original===
23. Geronimo has a fair knowledge of the Spanish language. ↩︎

===Chinese===
23. 杰罗尼莫的西班牙语掌握得颇为不错。 ↩︎

===Original===
24. As a tribe they would fight under their tribal chief, Mangus-Colorado. If several tribes had been called out, the war chief, Geronimo, would have commanded. ↩︎

===Chinese===
24. 单以部落出战时，他们在部落酋长曼加斯-科罗拉多麾下作战。若是征召了几个部落，便由战酋杰罗尼莫统率。 ↩︎

===Original===
25. Regarding this attack, Mr. L. C. Hughes, editor of *The Star*, Tucson, Arizona, to whom I was referred by General Miles, writes as follows:

 “It appears that Cochise and his tribe had been on the warpath for some time and he with a number of subordinate chiefs was brought into the military camp at Bowie under the promise that a treaty of peace was to be held, when they were taken into a large tent where handcuffs were put upon them. Cochise, seeing this, cut his way through the tent and fled to the mountains; and in less than six hours had surrounded the camp with from three to five hundred warriors; but the soldiers refused to make fight.”

 ↩︎

===Chinese===
25. 关于这次袭击，亚利桑那图森（Tucson）《星报》（*The Star*）主编 L. C. 休斯（L. C. Hughes）先生——迈尔斯将军让我向他求教——来信如下：

 「据知，柯奇斯和他的部族已在征途上有些时日。他与几位下属酋长得到将要缔结和约的许诺，被带进鲍伊堡（Bowie）的军营，随后他们被引入一顶大帐篷，被人上了手铐。柯奇斯见状，割开帐篷夺路而走，逃进山里；不出六个钟头，便带着三五百名武士把军营围了个水泄不通；可士兵们拒绝交战。」

 ↩︎

===Original===
26. This sweeping statement is more general than we are willing to concede, yet it may be more nearly true than our own accounts. ↩︎

===Chinese===
26. 这一概而论的断言，比我们愿意承认的更为笼统；然而它或许比我们自己的记载更接近事实。 ↩︎

===Original===
27. General Miles telegraphed from Whipple Barracks, Arizona, Sept. 24, 1886, relative to the surrender of the Apaches. Among other things he said: “Mangus-Colorado had years ago been foully murdered after he had surrendered.” ↩︎

===Chinese===
27. 1886 年 9 月 24 日，迈尔斯将军自亚利桑那惠普尔兵营（Whipple Barracks）就阿帕奇人投降一事发出电报。其中说道：「曼加斯-科罗拉多多年前已在投降之后惨遭杀害。」 ↩︎

===Original===
28. Geronimo often calls his horses to him in Fort Sill Reservation. He gives only one shrill note and they run to him at full speed. ↩︎

===Chinese===
28. 在西尔堡保留地，杰罗尼莫常常唤他的马。他只发出一声尖唳，马匹便全速向他奔来。 ↩︎

===Original===
29. Regarding the killing of Mangus-Colorado, L. C. Hughes of the Tucson, Ariz., *Star*, writes as follows:

 “It was early in the year ’63, when General West and his troops were camped near Membras, that he sent Jack Swilling, a scout, to bring in Mangus, who had been on the warpath ever since the time of the incident with Cochise at Bowie. The old chief was always for peace, and gladly accepted the proffer; when he appeared at the camp General West ordered him put into the guardhouse, in which there was only a small opening in the rear and but one small window. As the old chief entered he said: ‘This is my end. I shall never again hunt over the mountains and through the valleys of my people.’ He felt that he was to be assassinated. The guards were given orders to shoot him if he attempted to escape. He lay down and tried to sleep, but during the night, someone threw a large stone which struck him in the breast. He sprang up and in his delirium the guards thought he was attempting escape and several of them shot him; this was the end of Mangus.
 “His head was severed from his body by a surgeon, and the brain taken out and weighed. The head measured larger than that of Daniel Webster, and the brain was of corresponding weight. The skull was sent to Washington, and is now on exhibition at the Smithsonian Institution.”

 ↩︎

===Chinese===
29. 关于曼加斯-科罗拉多被杀一事，亚利桑那图森《星报》的 L. C. 休斯来信如下：

 「那是 '63 年初，韦斯特（West）将军和他的部队驻扎在梅姆布拉斯（Membras）附近，他派侦察兵杰克·斯威林（Jack Swilling）去把曼加斯带来——自从鲍伊堡柯奇斯那桩事之后，曼加斯一直在征途上。老酋长向来主和，欣然应允了这一邀约；可他一到营地，韦斯特将军便下令把他关进禁闭室，那屋子后墙只有一个小开口，仅有一扇小窗。老酋长进门时说道：『我的末日到了。我再也不能在我人民的山岭和谷地间狩猎了。』他自觉将要被害。卫兵奉令，他若试图逃脱便开枪。他躺下想睡，夜里却有人扔来一块大石头，砸在他胸口。他猛地跳起，昏乱之中，卫兵以为他要逃跑，几人一齐向他开枪；曼加斯就这样终了。
 「他的头由一名外科医生从躯体上切下，脑髓被取出称重。他的头颅量起来比丹尼尔·韦伯斯特（Daniel Webster）的还大，脑髓的重量也与之相称。头骨被送往华盛顿，现藏于史密森学会（Smithsonian Institution）展出。」

 ↩︎

===Original===
30. General O. O. Howard was not in command, but had been sent by President Grant, in 1872, to make peace with the Apache Indians. The general wrote me from Burlington, Vt., under date of June 12, 1906, that he remembered the treaty, and that he also remembered with much satisfaction subsequently meeting Geronimo. —Editor ↩︎

===Chinese===
30. O. O. 霍华德（O. O. Howard）将军当时并非统帅，而是于 1872 年受格兰特总统派遣，与阿帕奇印第安人议和。这位将军于 1906 年 6 月 12 日自佛蒙特州伯灵顿（Burlington）致函于我，说他记得那项条约，也记得其后与杰罗尼莫的会面，并引为欣慰。——编者 ↩︎

===Original===
31. They do not receive full rations now, as they did then. ↩︎

===Chinese===
31. 如今他们领不到当年那样的全额口粮了。 ↩︎

===Original===
32. Victoria, chief of the Hot Spring Apaches, met his death in opposing the forcible removal of his band to a reservation, because having previously tried and failed he felt it impossible for separate bands of Apaches to live at peace under such arrangement. ↩︎

===Chinese===
32. 温泉阿帕奇人的酋长维多利亚，死于反抗强行把他的队伍迁往保留地之举；因为此前他已尝试过并遭失败，他认定阿帕奇人各支队伍分处各地，在此等安排之下绝无安宁共处之可能。 ↩︎

===Original===
33. Geronimo’s whole family, excepting his eldest son, a warrior, were captured. ↩︎

===Chinese===
33. 杰罗尼莫的全家都被俘获，只有他的长子——一名武士——除外。 ↩︎

===Original===
34. Geronimo’s exact words, for which the Editor disclaims any responsibility. ↩︎

===Chinese===
34. 此为杰罗尼莫的原话，编者对此概不负责。 ↩︎

===Original===
35. These are the exact words of Geronimo. The Editor is not responsible for this criticism of General Crook. ↩︎

===Chinese===
35. 这些是杰罗尼莫的原话。对克鲁克将军的这番批评，编者不负其责。 ↩︎

===Original===
36. Governor Torres of Sonora had agreed to cooperate with our troops in exterminating or capturing this tribe. ↩︎

===Chinese===
36. 索诺拉州州长托雷斯（Torres）已同意与我们的部队合作，剿灭或俘获这个部落。 ↩︎

===Original===
37. Captain Lawton reports officially the same engagement, but makes no mention of the recapture (by the Apaches) of the horses. ↩︎

===Chinese===
37. 劳顿上尉在官方报告中记述了同一场战斗，但未提及（阿帕奇人）夺回马匹一事。 ↩︎

===Original===
38. See note 37. ↩︎

===Chinese===
38. 见尾注 37。 ↩︎

===Original===
39. See here. ↩︎

===Chinese===
39. 见此处。 ↩︎

===Original===
40. For terms of treaty see here. ↩︎

===Chinese===
40. 条约条款见此处。 ↩︎

===Original===
41. The criticisms of General Miles in the foregoing chapter are from Geronimo, not from the Editor. ↩︎

===Chinese===
41. 前一章中对迈尔斯将军的批评出自杰罗尼莫之口，并非编者之见。 ↩︎

===Original===
42. Mr. George Wratton is now at Fort Sill, Oklahoma, acting as Superintendent of Apaches. He has been with the Apaches as interpreter and superintendent since their surrender. ↩︎

===Chinese===
42. 乔治·拉顿先生现驻俄克拉何马（Oklahoma）西尔堡，任阿帕奇人总监。自阿帕奇人投降以来，他一直以译员和总监的身份同他们相处。 ↩︎

===Original===
43. Recently Mr. Melton told Geronimo of this conversation. The wily old chief laughed shyly and said, “What if Prewitt’s pistol had been knocked out of his hand? Other men have tried to shoot me and at least some of them failed. But I’m glad he didn’t try it.” ↩︎

===Chinese===
43. 近来，梅尔顿（Melton）先生把这段谈话讲给了杰罗尼莫。狡黠的老酋长讪讪一笑，说：「要是普鲁伊特（Prewitt）的手枪真被磕飞了呢？别人也开枪打过我的，至少有几个没打中。不过，幸亏他没动手。」 ↩︎

===Original===
44. These field glasses were taken from soldiers and officers (Mexicans and Americans) whom the Apaches had killed. ↩︎

===Chinese===
44. 这些双筒望远镜是从被阿帕奇人杀死的士兵和军官（墨西哥人和美国人）身上缴获的。 ↩︎

===Original===
45. This was a stick nest built on top of the ground by a species of woods rat. ↩︎

===Chinese===
45. 这是一种林鼠在地面上用树枝搭起的巢。 ↩︎

===Original===
46. These are not the words of the Editor, but of Geronimo. ↩︎

===Chinese===
46. 这些话并非编者所言，而是杰罗尼莫的话。 ↩︎

===Original===
47. They were in Alabama from May, 1888, to October, 1894. ↩︎

===Chinese===
47. 他们自 1888 年 5 月至 1894 年 10 月居留在亚拉巴马。 ↩︎

===Original===
48. The Indians are not allowed to sell the cattle themselves. When cattle are ready for market they are sold by the officer in charge, part of the money paid to the Indians who owned them and part of it placed in a general (Apache) fund. The supplies, farming implements, etc., for the Apaches are paid for from this fund. ↩︎

===Chinese===
48. 印第安人不获准自行售卖牛只。牛只达到出栏标准后，由主管军官出售，一部分钱付给牛主印第安人，一部分拨入一项总（阿帕奇）基金。阿帕奇人的给养、农具等等，即由这项基金支付。 ↩︎

===Original===
49. The criticism of Lieutenant Purington is from Geronimo. The Editor disclaims any responsibility for it, as in all cases where individuals are criticised by the old warrior. ↩︎

===Chinese===
49. 对普林顿中尉的批评出自杰罗尼莫。编者对此不负其责；凡是这位老武士批评个人之处，编者均不作担保。 ↩︎

===Original===
50. Geronimo helps make hay and care for the cattle, but does not receive orders from the Superintendent of the Indians. ↩︎

===Chinese===
50. 杰罗尼莫帮着晒干草、照看牛群，但不接受印第安人总监的差遣。 ↩︎

===Original===
51. Apache warriors do not go “courting” as our youths do. The associations in the villages afford ample opportunity for acquaintance, and the arranging for marriages is considered a business transaction, but the courtesy of consulting the maiden, although not essential, is considered very polite. ↩︎

===Chinese===
51. 阿帕奇武士不像我们的青年那样「追求」异性。村落中的日常往来为相识提供了充分的机会，婚姻的安排被视为一桩交易；不过，征询少女本人这一点，虽非必不可少，却被视为十分体面的礼数。 ↩︎

===Original===
52. Turks. ↩︎

===Chinese===
52. 土耳其人。 ↩︎

===Original===
53. Ferris wheel. ↩︎

===Chinese===
53. 摩天轮（Ferris wheel）。 ↩︎

===Original===
54. Shooting the Chute. ↩︎

===Chinese===
54. 滑水滑道（Shooting the Chute）。 ↩︎

===Original===
55. Iggorrotes from the Philippines. ↩︎

===Chinese===
55. 来自菲律宾的伊戈罗特人（Iggorrotes）。 ↩︎

===Original===
56. Geronimo was also taken to both the Omaha and the Buffalo Expositions, but during that period of his life he was sullen and took no interest in things. The St. Louis Exposition was held after he had adopted the Christian religion and had begun to try to understand our civilization. ↩︎

===Chinese===
56. 杰罗尼莫还被带去过奥马哈（Omaha）和布法罗（Buffalo）两届博览会，但在他人生的那个时期，他闷闷不乐，对诸事无心。圣路易斯博览会举行时，他已皈依基督教，并已开始努力理解我们的文明。 ↩︎

===Original===
57. Geronimo joined the Dutch Reformed church and was baptized in the summer of 1903. He attends the services regularly at the Apache Mission, Ft. Sill Military Reservation. ↩︎

===Chinese===
57. 杰罗尼莫加入了荷兰归正会（Dutch Reformed church），于 1903 年夏受洗。他定期参加西尔堡军区保留地阿帕奇布道所（Apache Mission）的礼拜。 ↩︎

## List of Illustrations / 插图目录

===Original===
1. How the book was made.
2. Geronimo
3. Dressed as in days of old.
4. Naiche (Natches), son of Cohise. Hereditary chief of the Chiricahua Apaches. Naiche was Geronimo’s lieutenant during the protracted wars in Arizona.
5. Last of the Bedonkohe Apache tribe: Tuklonnen, Nadeste, Na-ta-neal, Porico (White Horse).

===Chinese===
1. 这本书是怎样写成的。
2. 杰罗尼莫（Geronimo）
3. 身着往昔岁月的装束。
4. 纳切（Naiche，又作 Natches），柯奇斯（Cohise）之子，奇里卡瓦（Chiricahua）阿帕奇人的世袭酋长。在亚利桑那的长期战事中，纳切是杰罗尼莫的副手。
5. 贝东科黑（Bedonkohe）阿帕奇部落的最后传人：托克隆嫩（Tuklonnen）、纳达斯特（Nadeste）、莫塔尼尔（Na-ta-neal）、波里科（Porico，白马 White Horse）。

===Original===
6. Work stock in Apache Corral
7. The Conquered Weapon.
8. Apache Princess.
9. Geronimo, Nanne, Ozone, Chihuahua, Loco.
10. Naiche, his mother, his two wives, and his children.

===Chinese===
6. 阿帕奇畜栏中的役畜
7. 被征服的武器。
8. 阿帕奇公主。
9. 杰罗尼莫、纳内（Nanne）、奥宗（Ozone）、奇瓦瓦（Chihuahua）、洛科（Loco）。
10. 纳切、他的母亲、他的两位妻子和他的孩子们。

===Original===
11. Asa Deklugie, wife, and children.
12. Apache scouts: Natchie, Goody, John Loco, Porico, Jasen, Capt. Seyers, Hugh, Sam, Kelburn, Asa Deklugie.
13. Three Apache chieftans: Naiche, son of Coche; Asa, son of Whoa; Charley, son of Victoria.
14. Apache camp.
15. Apache mission, valley of Medicine Creek, Fort Sill Military Reservation.

===Chinese===
11. 阿萨·德克卢吉（Asa Deklugie）、他的妻子和孩子们。
12. 阿帕奇侦察兵：纳切（Natchie）、古迪（Goody）、约翰·洛科（John Loco）、波里科、贾森（Jasen）、塞耶斯上尉（Capt. Seyers）、休（Hugh）、萨姆（Sam）、凯尔本（Kelburn）、阿萨·德克卢吉。
13. 三位阿帕奇酋长：纳切，柯奇斯（Coche）之子；阿萨，沃阿（Whoa）之子；查理（Charley），维多利亚（Victoria）之子。
14. 阿帕奇营地。
15. 西尔堡军区保留地梅迪辛河（Medicine Creek）谷的阿帕奇布道所。

===Original===
16. Geronimo, Apache war chief; Asa Deklugie, official interpreter for Geronimo; son of Whoa, chief of the Nedni Apaches; chief elect to succeed Geronimo at the latter’s death.
17. Lone Wolf, chief of Kiowas, and Geronimo, Apache war chief.
18. Quanna Parker, chief of Comanche Indians.
19. Gotebo, war chief, Kiowa Indians.
20. Kaytah and Nahteen, Apache scouts who were with Gen. Lawton.

===Chinese===
16. 杰罗尼莫，阿帕奇战酋；阿萨·德克卢吉，杰罗尼莫的正式译员；沃阿之子，内德尼（Nedni）阿帕奇人的酋长；杰罗尼莫身后当选继任的酋长。
17. 孤狼（Lone Wolf），基奥瓦（Kiowa）人酋长，与阿帕奇战酋杰罗尼莫。
18. 夸纳·帕克（Quanna Parker），科曼奇印第安人酋长。
19. 戈特博（Gotebo），基奥瓦印第安人战酋。
20. 凯塔（Kaytah）和纳廷（Nahteen），曾随劳顿将军（Gen. Lawton）作战的阿帕奇侦察兵。

===Original===
21. Emma Tuklonen
22. W. F. Melton, at whose camp in Skeleton Canyon Geronimo surrendered.
23. Chihuahua and family
24. Mrs. Asa Deklugie, niece of Geronimo and daughter of Chihuahua, a famous Apache chieftain; Eva Geronimo, Geronimo’s youngest daugher, sixteen years old.
25. Ready for church.

===Chinese===
21. 埃玛·托克隆嫩（Emma Tuklonen）
22. W. F. 梅尔顿（W. F. Melton），杰罗尼莫在其骷髅峡谷（Skeleton Canyon）营地中投降。
23. 奇瓦瓦及其家人
24. 阿萨·德克卢吉夫人，杰罗尼莫的侄女，著名阿帕奇酋长奇瓦瓦之女；伊娃·杰罗尼莫（Eva Geronimo），杰罗尼莫最小的女儿，十六岁。
25. 准备去教堂。
